# Architecture

Status: **authoritative**, v1.2, 2026-10-05. This document supersedes the four design proposals it was synthesized from. v1.1 resolves the principal-engineer review of v1.0; Appendix D indexes each finding and where it is resolved. v1.2 incorporates the accepted M0 deviations of `Docs/Decisions/0002-m0-deviations.md` (Appendix D, last paragraph). Where this document and `Docs/CodeStyle.md` overlap, CodeStyle governs formatting and naming; this document settles every topic CodeStyle marks **[Architecture]** (see Appendix A).

The display name "Yggdrasil" appears only in the premake workspace name, the `ENGINE_PRODUCT_NAME` define (editor window titles, and the user-data folder of the Editor and Tests) and documentation. Exported games use their manifest `Name` instead (§14.1). Code says `Engine`.

**Provenance.** Structure and scope discipline come from the simplicity proposal (highest mean judge score). The correctness core comes from the robustness proposal: UI-free `EditorCore`, canonical iteration order, sorted physics events, seeded runtime UUIDs, per-step input latching, serializer-based Play copy, and test infrastructure. The automation layer comes from the AI-control proposal. Renderer verification and quality details come from the rendering proposal. Every flaw and uncovered requirement the judges raised is resolved in the text; Appendix B indexes where.

---

## 1. Goals, non-goals, guiding principles

### 1.1 Goals
- **G1 Spec features.** glTF PBR meshes with materials and textures, gizmo placement, IBL from Poly Haven HDRIs, soft shadows, GTAO, HDR with tonemapping, Jolt physics authored as components and driven by scripts, miniaudio, Luau scripting with entity creation, destruction and prefab spawning, an editor, and export to a standalone runtime.
- **G2 Rock-solid.** Every subsystem is unit-testable without a window, GPU or audio device. Bugs assert, expected failures are values, nothing fails silently.
- **G3 Deterministic.** The same source built with the same toolchain on the same platform, given the same inputs and seed, produces the same state hash. This holds whatever the frame rate, the Jolt thread count and the build configuration (Debug, Release, Dist). Recorded replays and expected hashes are therefore valid in every configuration (§9.1, §15.8).
- **G4 Full AI control.** An agent builds, tests and exports a game through the editor automation API alone. The proof is Tetris and Rolling Ball 3D, built by an agent through automation with a committed transcript and a file-provenance audit.
- **G5 Verifiable completeness.** The FeatureTest project mechanically proves that every component, component field, enum value, project setting, asset format, script function, method, property, callback and automation method is exercised, in the editor and in exported Release and Dist builds. A gap fails CI.
- **G6 Honest platform status.** Windows x64 is built and tested on every change. Linux and macOS are compile-targeted and generation-checked locally. Windows is the only verified platform. First-party code stays portable by rule (the `Platform/` layering, no Windows-only shortcuts, the local portability stage's premake generation and clang-cl builds), but Linux and macOS are never verified: the Linux/macOS catch-up was dropped by the product owner, because there is no Linux or macOS machine without GitHub. (`Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 10.)

### 1.2 Non-goals (v1)
Each can be revisited through an ADR in `Docs/Decisions/`. The backlog program (`Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md`) schedules some of them, each marked with its milestone below, and confirms others as won't do; until its milestone lands, a scheduled item is still a non-goal.
- Graphics backends other than NVRHI-Vulkan. Ray tracing, bindless rendering, GPU-driven rendering, render-graph aliasing. (Won't do, ADR 0028.)
- TAA, histogram auto-exposure, colour grading LUTs, SSR (scheduled for M26 by ADR 0028), clustered light culling, point-light shadows (scheduled for M23), GI beyond IBL plus AO (baked irradiance and reflection probes scheduled for M26; dynamic GI won't do, ADR 0028).
- GPU texture compression (no approved encoder until ADR 0028 approved CMP_Core), EXR import, Ogg Opus import (Ogg Vorbis is supported, §7.4), mesh instancing. All scheduled for M23 by ADR 0028.
- Skeletal and morph animation (glTF skins and animations are skipped with a warning; scheduled for M23 by ADR 0028), particles, terrain (scheduled for M26), visual scripting (scheduled for M24), native gameplay modules (scheduled for M19, on Windows only), networking, mobile, consoles, web. Networking, mobile and web stay non-goals; consoles won't do (ADR 0028).
- Live nested prefabs (nested instances are flattened when a prefab is created), deleting prefab-member entities inside an instance (deactivate or unpack instead; refused with that hint from the program's setup commit). Both scheduled for M20 by ADR 0028.
- Multiple script components per entity (use child entities; scheduled for M20 by ADR 0028), ImGui multi-viewport OS windows (scheduled for M24, on Windows only), native file dialogs (scheduled for M18, on Windows only), a UI framework beyond `TextComponent` (scheduled for M22).
- Native Wayland (Linux runs X11, including XWayland sessions), cross-OS export (each host exports for itself), bit-exact cross-OS or cross-compiler determinism. Jolt itself is built cross-platform deterministic (§9.1), but the CRT `pow` behind Luau's general `^` operator is not guaranteed to match across C runtimes. These stay non-goals (ADR 0028).
- Identity code signing and notarization (macOS bundles are only ad-hoc signed, §14.2). Won't do (ADR 0028).

### 1.3 Guiding principles
1. **Pure core, thin effectful shell.** Step scheduling, device scoring, cascade math, light packing, undo, pak layout, input latching and coverage evaluation are pure functions with table-driven tests. Device wrappers stay thin.
2. **One source of truth per concept.** The type registry drives the inspector, serialization, Luau proxies, automation schemas, validation, docs and coverage. The script API registry drives bindings, `Engine.d.luau`, docs and counters. The method registry drives dispatch, the MCP catalogue and coverage. Generated files are committed and staleness-checked by CI.
3. **One path per job.** One mutation path (Commands), one load path (cooked bytes through the same loader in editor and runtime), one scene-copy path (the serializer), one render path (headless equals windowed).
4. **Deterministic by construction.** Fixed step, seeded RNG and runtime UUIDs, canonical iteration order wherever the order is observable, sorted physics events, simulation time only, precise floating point everywhere, and deterministic transcendental functions on the simulation path (§4.12).
5. **Errors are values at boundaries.** Programmer errors assert. Expected failures return `Result<T>`. Script faults are isolated. Fatal environment failures exit with a documented code.
6. **Parity.** Anything a human can do in the editor, an agent can do through automation on the same code path. No editor feature lands without its automation method and test.
7. **Bounded and located.** Every automation response is size-bounded. Every error carries a code, a location and, where possible, a hint.
8. **Pays for itself.** A new abstraction needs a written justification in its PR. The non-goals list is a scope guard.

---

## 2. Repository layout and build system

### 2.1 Layout
```
D:/Engine
├─ premake5.lua  Dependencies.lua  AGENTS.md  CLAUDE.md (@AGENTS.md)  .mcp.json (generated by Setup.py, gitignored)
├─ GameEngineDoc.md                        (the original specification)
├─ .clang-format  .clang-tidy  .editorconfig  .gitattributes  .gitignore
├─ .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md          (§13.10)
├─ .github/workflows/ci.yml                (runs Scripts/CI.py only when started by hand, workflow_dispatch, from the program's setup commit; GitHub is an off-site backup only, ADR 0028 decision 10)
├─ Engine/    premake5.lua
│             Config/entt/ext/config.h      (routes ENTT_ASSERT to ENGINE_CORE_ASSERT)
│             Source/EnginePCH.h|.cpp  Source/Engine/<Module>/...  (include root: Engine/Source)
├─ Editor/    premake5.lua (projects EditorCore = StaticLib, Editor = ConsoleApp)
│             Source/EditorPCH.h  Source/EditorCore/...  Source/Editor/...  (include root: Editor/Source)
├─ Runtime/   premake5.lua  Source/Runtime/{RuntimeMain.cpp, RuntimeApp.h|.cpp}
├─ Tests/     premake5.lua  Source/TestsPCH.h  Source/TestMain.cpp  Source/Support/
│             Source/Engine/<Module>/*Tests.cpp  Source/EditorCore/*Tests.cpp
│             Source/{Editor,Runtime}/*Tests.cpp (the executables' code)  Source/Golden/*Tests.cpp (§15.4)
│             Source/ThirdParty/*Tests.cpp  (configuration of vendored libraries, e.g. Jolt's version ID)
│             Data/ (fixtures + LICENSES.md)  Golden/<DeviceClass>/*.png
│             Automation/test_*.py  Projects/Breakout/ (dry-run project, M16, §18)
├─ Resources/ Shaders/{Shared/*.h, Common/*.slang, Passes/*.slang (the IBL passes included), Shaders.json}
│             Scripting/Engine.d.luau (generated)  Templates/{Projects,Scripts}/  Audio/*.sfx (§10.3)
│             Environments/*.hdr (2 x CC0 Poly Haven, 1k)  Fonts/Inter-Regular.ttf (SIL OFL)
│             EngineAssets.json  LICENSES.md  Licenses/ (license texts LICENSES.md references)
│             (editor icons and blue noise are generated in code, §8.4, §12.2)
├─ Vendor/<Lib>/ (as vendored; premake5.lua + VENDOR.md each)   Vendor/premake/bin/premake5.exe
├─ Scripts/   *.py entry points  Lib/ (shared helpers)  Premake/{CompileCommands,XcodeHeaderSearchPaths}.lua
│             ModuleRules.json
├─ Tools/     MCP/{run.py, engine_mcp/, catalog.json (generated), requirements.lock, tests/}
│             Automation/engine_client.py (stdlib JSON-RPC client shared by MCP and tests)
├─ Projects/  FeatureTest/  Tetris/ (M17)  RollingBall/ (M27)
└─ Docs/      Architecture.md  Roadmap.md  CodeStyle.md  ReviewChecklist.md  Backlog.md  Decisions/
              Reference/ (generated: Components, ScriptAPI, Automation, Validation, FileFormats, BuiltinAssets,
                          Methods.json, Schemas/)
```
A game project is `Projects/<Name>/<Name>.eproj`, `Assets/**` (each asset with a `.meta` sidecar), `AGENTS.md` (game notes), `Automation/` (committed: `BuildLog.jsonl`, the MCP transcript, and `Provenance.json`, §13.12) and `Library/` (cache, autosave, `Editor.lock`, offloaded automation results; gitignored).

### 2.2 Premake
Windows uses x64 host tools as well as an x64 target: the workspace sets `preferredtoolarchitecture "x86_64"`. `CheckBuildConfig.py` checks the generated project setting, with a seeded regression fixture (ADR 0019 decision 9).
`premake5.lua` declares `workspace "Yggdrasil"`, `configurations { "Debug", "Release", "Dist" }`, `architecture "x86_64"` on Windows and Linux and `"ARM64"` on macOS, `startproject "Editor"`, and `OutputDir = "%{cfg.buildcfg}-%{cfg.system}-%{cfg.architecture}"`. Vendor projects are included under `group "Dependencies"`: `Vendor/GLFW`, `Vendor/NVRHI`, `Vendor/imgui`, `Vendor/ImGuizmo`, `Vendor/Luau` (projects `Luau`, `LuauAnalysis`), `Vendor/JoltPhysics`, `Vendor/miniaudio`, `Vendor/MikkTSpace` (ADR 0010 decision 16), `Vendor/spdlog`. Header-only libraries (glm, EnTT, nlohmann/json, doctest, cgltf, stb) have no project; their implementation TUs live in Engine (`AssetPipeline/ThirdParty/{CgltfImplementation,StbImageResizeImplementation,TrueTypeRasterizer}.cpp`, `Graphics/ThirdParty/StbImageImplementation.cpp` (ADR 0009 decision 11) and `ImGui/ImGuiGlfwImplementation.cpp`, all compiled without the PCH through `enablepch "Off"`, because premake 5.0.0 has no `NoPCH` flag; ADR 0009 decision 15). The exception is stb_vorbis, miniaudio's Ogg Vorbis decoder: it compiles inside the miniaudio project through the local translation unit `Vendor/miniaudio/miniaudio_vorbis.c` (the stb_vorbis declarations, `MINIAUDIO_IMPLEMENTATION` with `miniaudio.h`, then the stb_vorbis implementation; `Vendor/stb` is on that project's include path), which replaces upstream's `miniaudio.c` (ADR 0015 decision 2).

**Workspace-scope rule (ODR).** `filter "configurations:Dist" defines { "NDEBUG" }` is set at workspace scope and nowhere else. `NDEBUG` changes the layout of vulkan.hpp's dispatcher (`Vendor/NVRHI/VENDOR.md`), so it must be identical for NVRHI and every consumer. The vendored Luau project additionally defines `NDEBUG` for its own TUs in Release; this is allowed because no Luau layout depends on it and no Luau TU includes vulkan.hpp.

**`Dependencies.lua`** defines `IncludeDir`, `ApplyFirstPartySettings()` and one `Use<Lib>()` function per library. Each function's defines are copied verbatim from that library's `VENDOR.md`:

| Function | Consumer settings (must match the vendored project) |
|---|---|
| `UseNVRHI()` | `VULKAN_HPP_DISPATCH_LOADER_DYNAMIC=1`; Windows `VK_USE_PLATFORM_WIN32_KHR`, `NOMINMAX`; no other `VULKAN_HPP_*`; never link `vulkan-1` |
| `UseJoltPhysics()` | exactly the table in `Vendor/JoltPhysics/VENDOR.md`: `JPH_OBJECT_STREAM`, `JPH_OBJECT_LAYER_BITS=16`, **`JPH_CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC` in every configuration**, SSE4.1/4.2 on x86_64 (`/arch:SSE4.2` for MSVC, `-msse4.2 -mpopcnt` for GCC, Clang and clang-cl), `JPH_DEBUG_RENDERER JPH_PROFILE_ENABLED JPH_ENABLE_ASSERTS` in Debug and Release, `JPH_FLOATING_POINT_EXCEPTIONS_ENABLED` in MSVC Debug, `JPH_NO_DEBUG` in Release and Dist |
| `UseSpdlog()` | `SPDLOG_COMPILED_LIB`, `SPDLOG_USE_STD_FORMAT`, MSVC `/utf-8 /Zc:__cplusplus` |
| `UseMiniaudio()` | `MA_NO_ENCODING`; macOS `MA_NO_RUNTIME_LINKING` + CoreAudio frameworks |
| `UseLuau(analysis)` | include dirs only; `links { "LuauAnalysis", "Luau" }` when `analysis`, else `{ "Luau" }` |
| `UseGLFW()` | `GLFW_INCLUDE_NONE`; platform link libraries from `Vendor/GLFW/VENDOR.md` |
| `UseImGui()` | none (unmodified `imconfig.h`); ImGuizmo include `Vendor/ImGuizmo/src` |
| `UseMikkTSpace()` | include directory only; `links { "MikkTSpace" }`; precise floating point like Jolt (below) |
| first-party only | `GLM_FORCE_DEPTH_ZERO_TO_ONE`, `GLM_ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL`, `GLM_FORCE_CTOR_INIT`, `JSON_USE_IMPLICIT_CONVERSIONS=0`, `WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN`, `ENGINE_PLATFORM_{WINDOWS,LINUX,MACOS}` |

All vendor include directories use `externalincludedirs` with `externalwarnings "Off"`. `Scripts/CheckBuildConfig.py` generates the workspace into a temp directory for every generator and toolset the build or CI uses (vs2026 with MSVC and with clang-cl, gmake with GCC and with Clang, xcode4), parses every generated project and computes each compile unit's effective defines in command-line order (undefines and `-D`/`/D` in compiler options count). It fails if, for any target and configuration, an ABI-relevant define differs between a vendor project and a consumer that includes its headers. The families come from each `VENDOR.md`: `JPH_*`; `SPDLOG_*`; `MA_NO_*`, `MINIAUDIO_IMPLEMENTATION`; `VULKAN_HPP_*`, `VK_USE_PLATFORM_*`, `NOMINMAX`, `VK_ENABLE_BETA_EXTENSIONS`, `NVRHI_SHARED_LIBRARY_*`; Luau's `LUA_USE_LONGJMP`, `LUA_API`, `LUACODE_API`, `LUA_VECTOR_*`; Dear ImGui's and ImGuizmo's `IMGUI_*`, `ImTextureID`, `ImDrawIdx`, `USE_IMGUI_API`, `IMGUIZMO_NAMESPACE`; GLFW's `_GLFW_*`, `GLFW_DLL`; and `NDEBUG` for every library. It also fails if `JPH_CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC` is missing from JoltPhysics or any consumer; if a Jolt consumer uses other instruction-set options than JoltPhysics (Jolt derives `JPH_USE_AVX2` and the like from the compiler's `__AVX2__` macros, which `JPH_VERSION_ID` does not encode); or if any first-party, Jolt, MikkTSpace or Luau project uses a non-precise floating-point model (`/fp:fast`, `FloatingPointModel=Fast`, `/fp:contract`, `/Qfast_transcendentals`, `-ffast-math` or any of its components, or any effective `-ffp-contract` other than `off`). The same rules apply to the flags each shared vendor-cache entry a build would link recorded (below). At runtime `JPH::VerifyJoltVersionID()` is checked at startup and a mismatch is fatal. The version ID encodes `JPH_CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC`, so a consumer built without the define cannot run.

**Floating-point model (determinism, §9.1).** Every first-party project, JoltPhysics, MikkTSpace and the Luau VM project (`Luau`, whose interpreter and `vector`/`math` libraries run scripts' gameplay arithmetic) compile with precise floating point. On MSVC this is `/fp:precise` (premake `floatingpoint "Default"`), which since VS 2022 never generates FMA contractions. GCC and Clang use `-fno-fast-math -ffp-contract=off` on every architecture, and clang-cl the same options as `/clang:-fno-fast-math /clang:-ffp-contract=off`. Clang's default model is already precise, so this is precise without contraction; `-ffp-model=precise -ffp-contract=off` is not used, because `-ffp-model=precise` implies `-ffp-contract=on` and Clang reports the override as `-Woverriding-option`, an error under `-Werror` (for the same reason premake's `floatingpoint "Precise"` is not used). `-fno-fast-math` also resets the `-ffast-math` that premake's xcode4 generator adds for `optimize "Full"` (Dist). The vendored Jolt premake file therefore replaces upstream's MSVC `floatingpoint "Fast"` and its ARM64 `-ffp-contract=on`. That is a build-configuration change recorded in `Vendor/JoltPhysics/VENDOR.md`; Jolt's source stays unmodified (Roadmap M0). Jolt documents the cost of cross-platform determinism at roughly 8 %, which is accepted. In exchange, replays and expected hashes are valid in every configuration. The vendored Luau premake file adds the same options to `Luau` (recorded in `Vendor/Luau/VENDOR.md`; M13 review F01); upstream's `-fno-math-errno` stays, since it changes no value. `LuauAnalysis` only type-checks in the editor and keeps the compiler defaults.

**`ApplyFirstPartySettings()`** (Engine, EditorCore, Editor, Runtime, Tests): `language "C++"`, `cppdialect "C++23"`, `staticruntime "off"`, `warnings "Extra"`, `fatalwarnings { "All" }`, `multiprocessorcompile "On"`, exceptions on, RTTI at compiler default (first-party code never uses `dynamic_cast` or `typeid`; lint-enforced). MSVC adds `/utf-8 /permissive- /Zc:__cplusplus /Zc:preprocessor` and the precise FP model above. GCC/Clang add `-Wshadow` and the precise FP flags above. Windows Debug executables link with `/STACK:2097152` (Luau analysis recursion). Each Windows executable embeds `Engine/Source/Engine/Platform/Windows/App.manifest` (PerMonitorV2 DPI, UTF-8 active code page, long paths).

| | Debug | Release | Dist |
|---|---|---|---|
| Defines | `ENGINE_DEBUG` | `ENGINE_RELEASE` | `ENGINE_DIST`, `NDEBUG` (workspace) |
| Optimize / symbols | Off / On | Speed / On | Full + `linktimeoptimization "On"` (xcode4: `LLVM_LTO = YES`, because premake ignores `linktimeoptimization` there) / On (PDB/dSYM archived in `bin/Symbols`, never shipped) |
| Engine asserts, Trace logs | on | on | off |
| Vulkan + NVRHI validation | on by default | `--gpu-validation` (always on in tests) | never |
| Projects built | all | all | Engine, Runtime, vendor (`removeconfigurations { "Dist" }` on EditorCore, Editor, Tests) |
| Runtime kind | ConsoleApp | ConsoleApp | WindowedApp (`entrypoint "mainCRTStartup"`) |

PCH: `EnginePCH.h` (Engine), `EditorPCH.h` (compiled separately by EditorCore and Editor), `TestsPCH.h`. Runtime uses none. Headers never include a PCH.

**Shaders** are compiled by a dedicated `Shaders` project (`kind "Utility"`, group `Engine`) into `bin/<OutputDir>/Shaders/` (§8.12). Its single custom build rule runs `python Scripts/CompileShaders.py --config %{cfg.buildcfg} --output-dir <bin/<OutputDir>/Shaders>` on `Resources/Shaders/Shaders.json` (with the interpreter `Generate.py` passes as `--python` when it does, `python3` on Linux and macOS otherwise). The rule declares `buildinputs` for every `Resources/Shaders/**.slang` and `**.h` file and `buildoutputs` for a stamp file `bin/<OutputDir>/Shaders/.stamp`, so Visual Studio's fast up-to-date check re-runs it when only a shader changes. `Engine` declares `dependson { "Shaders" }`. The ninja generator is the exception: premake 5.0.0's ninja generator supports neither Utility projects nor `dependson` edges to them, so for ninja `Engine/premake5.lua` attaches the same rule to `Engine` itself and every Engine object depends on the stamp. A prebuild step on the Engine static library is not used, because the IDE skips it when no C++ input changed. Dev builds mount `engine://` at `<repo>/Resources` (the `ENGINE_REPO_ROOT` define) and `shaders://` at the repository's `bin/<OutputDir>/Shaders` (the `ENGINE_SHADER_DIRECTORY` define, from the workspace's `ShaderOutputDirectory`, which stays in the repository's `bin` even with `Generate.py --to`); both defines are absent in Dist. The VFS mounts whole schemes, so the SPIR-V gets its own scheme rather than `engine://Shaders` (ADR 0009 decision 4). Exported games read `engine://Shaders` from `Engine.pak`, which contains the target configuration's SPIR-V (§14.2).

**`Scripts/Premake/CompileCommands.lua`** adds a custom action, `premake5 compile-commands`, that writes `compile_commands.json` (clang-style flags derived from the same project settings) for clang-tidy.

**Shared vendor-library cache** (`Scripts/Lib/vendor_cache.py`; `Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 16). Every worktree of the repository compiles the vendored static libraries (the projects whose compiled sources all lie under `Vendor/`) from the same pinned sources with the same settings, so MSBuild builds through `Build.py` link them from one cache instead of compiling them in each checkout. The make, ninja and Xcode builds compile them as before.
- **Location.** `<git common directory>/vendor-cache` (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`: `D:/Engine/.git` for the main checkout and every worktree of it). It is shared by exactly the checkouts of the repository, lies outside every working tree (no `bin/` removal or `git clean` touches it) and is never tracked. Deleting it, or an entry, is safe only while no build runs in any checkout (`Scripts/Locks.py` lists no build holder), since a running build links the entries it verified where they are; the next build compiles and publishes what it needs. Entries are never evicted, so the sets of an older toolchain or vendor change stay until then (an accepted limit, `Docs/Backlog.md` §6). Below `v1/`: `entries/<Project>/<Configuration>-<key>/` (one entry: `entry.json`, the library and its compiler PDBs), `staging/` (compiles in progress), `trash/` (rejected entries) and `populate.lock`.
- **Key.** The first 32 hex digits of the SHA-256 of an entry's inputs as canonical JSON: the project's generated settings for the configuration (every property, tool setting and compiled file with its per-file settings of its `.vcxproj`, so every define, the ABI-relevant ones of `AGENTS.md` among them, the floating-point model and options, the instruction set such as Jolt's `/arch:SSE4.2`, the sanitizer (`EnableASAN`), the runtime library, optimization and debug-information settings and the platform toolset, `v145` or `ClangCL`), the toolchain (Visual Studio, MSVC tools and Windows SDK versions, and the per-user property sheet `Microsoft.Cpp.x64.user.props` every generated project imports), and the source pin of every `Vendor/<Lib>` directory the project compiles from, includes or force-includes (the version and commit of its `VENDOR.md`, and a digest of every file git lists there, `VENDOR.md` and `premake5.lua` included). MSBuild compiles with exactly that toolchain: `Build.py` removes the variables that would select another (`VCToolsVersion`, `VCToolsInstallDir`, `LLVMInstallDir`, `LLVMToolsVersion`) or add compiler options (`CL`, `_CL_`) from its environment. The key is fail-closed: a vendored project whose include, library, `#using` or forced-include paths, or items, name anything outside `Vendor/` or a path built from an MSBuild property (a first-party configuration header in `Engine/Config`, an SDK's `$(VULKAN_SDK)/Include`), whose content the key cannot digest, is never cached and compiles in the workspace. Paths are relative to the repository root and the output and intermediate directories are left out, so every checkout and every workspace location (in place, `--to bin-int/Asan`, `bin-int/Portability`) computes the same key for the same build. The AddressSanitizer workspace's Release and the clang-cl workspace therefore have their own entries; its uninstrumented Debug and Dist share the plain ones.
- **Publishing.** A missing entry is compiled by `Build.py` under `populate.lock` (other builds wait, then link it), in `staging/` with its own `OutDir` and `IntDir` and without its project references; its library and compiler PDBs move into a staging directory with `entry.json` (the inputs, every file's size and SHA-256, the warnings the compile reported), which is then renamed to the entry's name, so a reader sees no entry or a complete one. Entries are immutable. A failed compile publishes nothing and fails the build with its errors.
- **Verification.** Before an entry is linked, its record must parse, have this format, hash to its key and record exactly the current inputs, and every file must have its recorded size and SHA-256. An entry that fails is never linked: it moves to `trash/` and is compiled and published again. A linked entry's recorded warnings count in the build, so a cache hit passes or fails `Build.py`'s warning rules as compiling the library would.
- **Linking in place.** `Build.py` passes MSBuild `ForceImportAfterCppTargets` (the documented hook that imports a `.targets` file after `Microsoft.Cpp.targets` in every C++ project of a build) with the file it writes into the workspace's `bin-int/VendorCache/`: a cached vendor project builds nothing, every project drops its `ProjectReference` to it, and every linking project lists the cache's library in `Link.AdditionalDependencies`. Build output is never copied between checkouts (MSBuild's `.tlog` files hold absolute paths): consumers link the cache's files where they are, and the linker finds each library's compiler PDB beside it. Debug information and `__FILE__` strings of a cached library name the vendored sources of the checkout that compiled the entry (identical files, by the key). The generated workspace is unchanged, so its project sets stay those of the other generators (the portability stage's check), and a build in the IDE or with `Build.py --no-vendor-cache` compiles the vendored libraries in the workspace.
- **Check.** `CheckBuildConfig.py` (rule 9) finds the entry each Visual Studio target would link for every vendored project and configuration and fails when its record is broken, records other settings than the generated project, or records flags that break the ABI-define, determinism-define, instruction-set, floating-point or sanitizer rules against the workspace's consumers. Entries under other keys (another branch's vendor set, an older toolchain) are never linked and not checked, nor is a project the key cannot digest.

### 2.3 Python scripts (Python 3.10+, stdlib only; `Tools/MCP` uses a venv created by Setup.py)
Every script has `--help`, `--json` output and a non-zero exit code on failure.

| Script | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `Setup.py` | Check Python, Git, `VULKAN_SDK` (≥ 1.4.350, `slangc`, `spirv-val`), MSVC via vswhere, Linux packages (`xorg-dev`). Download the pinned premake 5.0.0 into `Vendor/premake/bin/` (SHA-256 verified; the directory is gitignored) when absent. Create `Tools/MCP/.venv` from the hash-locked `requirements.lock`, then write `.mcp.json` with the venv interpreter's absolute path (§13.8), so no `python` on `PATH` is assumed. |
| `Generate.py [--action] [--toolset] [--to <dir>] [--sanitize address]` | `premake5 vs2026` (Windows), `gmake`/`ninja` (Linux), `xcode4` (macOS); then `compile-commands`. `--sanitize address` generates the hardening stage's AddressSanitizer workspace (Release of every project instrumented, workspace scope; used with `--to bin-int/Asan`, ADR 0024 decision 12). |
| `Build.py --config C [--project P] [--workspace-dir D] [--jobs N] [--no-vendor-cache] [--vendor-cache-dir D] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | MSBuild via vswhere, or make/ninja/xcodebuild, under the machine-wide build lock (below): `--jobs N` with N at most half the logical CPUs is a capped build (one of the lock's two slots, at most N compilers for the whole build: MSBuild runs up to `-m` projects at once, each with `/MP` at `CL_MPCount`, the per-project compiler count that `Microsoft.Cl.Common.props` makes every project's `/MP` count, so a capped build gets `-m:2` and `-p:CL_MPCount=N/2`, `-m:1` below 4 jobs), more is uncapped (both slots, `/MP` at the logical CPU count), and without `--jobs` the build is uncapped on a free lock and capped with half the CPUs beside one capped build. MSBuild builds link the vendored static libraries from the shared vendor cache (§2.2; ADR 0028 decision 16): each entry is verified before it is linked, and a missing or rejected one is compiled once, published atomically and linked; `--no-vendor-cache` compiles them in the workspace and `--vendor-cache-dir` names another cache; the cache's compiles of missing entries run inside the build, under its build-lock slot and compiler cap. After building Runtime on Windows, copies the app-local CRT (`vcruntime140.dll`, `vcruntime140_1.dll`, `msvcp140.dll` from `VC/Redist/MSVC/<newest>/x64/Microsoft.VC145.CRT`) into `bin/<OutputDir>/Runtime/Redist/`. From M15 (ADR 0024 decisions 2, 5 and 8), after building Runtime on every OS it writes `bin/<OutputDir>/Runtime/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` from the `## Notices` sections of `Vendor/*/VENDOR.md` and `Resources/LICENSES.md`; on macOS it stages the SDK's `libvulkan.1.dylib`, `libMoltenVK.dylib` and `MoltenVK_icd.json` in `Runtime/Frameworks/` and `Runtime/Vulkan/icd.d/`; after a Dist build it archives the Runtime's symbols and map in `bin/Symbols/<OutputDir>/Runtime/`. |
| `CompileShaders.py [--program P] [--force]` | The only place slangc flags are defined (§8.12). |
| `Test.py --suite unit,gpu,golden,feature,automation,export,determinism,games [--config C] [--require-gpu] [--vulkan-api 1.3\|1.4] [--update-golden] [--junit] [--allow-skips] [--editor-report F] [--build-root D] [--sanitizer address] [--test-timeout-scale S] [--fuzz-scale S] [--exclude-test-suites S] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]`; `Test.py --merge-coverage <Editor.json> <Release.json> <Dist.json>` | Runs the suites (§15) in the configurations given by the §15.8 matrix. A run that includes a GPU-using suite (gpu, golden, feature, automation, export, determinism, games) holds the machine-wide GPU lock from its first suite to its end; any other run only waits until no gate runs (below); `--merge-coverage` takes no lock. With the default `--suite all`, a suite that does not take a requested configuration is reported as not run in it; naming that suite together with that configuration is a usage error. The gpu suite runs once per Vulkan API cap (1.4, then capped with `--vulkan-api=1.3`; `--vulkan-api` restricts it to one). JUnit XML into `bin/TestResults/<Suite>-<Config>[-Vulkan13\|-Vulkan14].xml`. Without `--require-gpu`, a GPU or golden test case that finds no Vulkan device passes without running and the run ends with the status warning; with it, the test case fails. The automation suite (`Tests/Automation` and `Tools/MCP/tests` in the MCP virtual environment, then the method coverage gate) does the same for its tests that start a rendering editor (`harness.require_gpu`, ADR 0009 decision 33). A gpu run under the 1.4 cap also warns about skipped host-image-copy cases, and a golden run about missing goldens (smoke mode) and about candidates `--update-golden` wrote. Every run fails on, and names, any test case skipped outside the child-process targets (`Test::ChildTargetSuite`), from the Tests binary's `--no-skip --list-test-cases --reporters=xml` listing of the run's selection; `--allow-skips` is contract mode (Roadmap rule 3, `Docs/Decisions/0004-contract-stub-gate.md`). The feature suite runs `Editor --headless --project Projects/FeatureTest --run-tests --strict` per configuration (§6) and fails on a non-zero exit, on a per-mode gate that is absent, unevaluated or failed (`UNCOVERED gate <g> (Editor): <id>` lines), on a skipped case row and on any change under `Projects/FeatureTest` outside `Library/`; with `--junit` the editor's own reports are `bin/TestResults/Feature-<Config>.xml` and `.json`; it needs a Vulkan device and follows the automation suite's rule without one. The determinism suite verifies every `Testing.Replays` file of FeatureTest with `input.replay {verify, strictHash}` in the `--renderer none --strict` editor of each configuration, comparing every state-hash checkpoint on the way, then requires every replay with input events to fail `input.replay {verify, strictHash, withoutInput}` in those editors (the input check, §13.6), and fails on a failed verification, a replay that passes its input check, or a hash that differs from the committed `FinalStateHash` or between configurations (JUnit `bin/TestResults/Determinism.xml`; M14 runs the Debug and Release editors, M15 adds the exported Release and Dist runs, whose hashes it reads from the export suite's reports, ADR 0024 decision 10). The export suite (M15, ADR 0024 decision 9; `--config Release,Dist`) exports a copy of FeatureTest from a `--strict` editor with `project.export {testing: true}` into `bin/Exports/<Config>/FeatureTest/`, runs each package's `--feature-test`, evaluates each report's per-mode gates and merges them with the Editor report (`--editor-report`, default `bin/TestResults/Feature-Release.json`); it needs a Vulkan device and follows the automation suite's rule without one. `--build-root` and `--sanitizer address` run the unit and feature suites from the AddressSanitizer workspace, giving every process they start the AddressSanitizer options and the runtime's directory first on `PATH` (ADR 0024 decision 12); the Debug unit run passes `--fuzz-scale=0.1`, so each fuzz case runs the first tenth of its own sequence (at least its minimum), while the Release unit run executes every count and the hardening stage 1,000,000 per reader (the product owner's decision, ADR 0024 decision 14); `--fuzz-scale S` sets another scale. `--exclude-test-suites` (unit suite only) leaves the named doctest suites out of the unit runs: the hardening stage's ASan unit step leaves out `Fuzz`, which its fuzz step runs as a superset. `--merge-coverage` runs the cross-mode merge of §15.6 alone and prints one `merge: <rule>: <subject>` line per failure (ADR 0022 decisions 6 and 7). The games suite (M16, ADR 0026 decisions 7 and 8; any of Debug, Release and Dist) runs over the game projects staged below `Projects/` and `Tests/Projects/` (`Projects/FeatureTest` excluded): with none it passes with one step, `games`, and a project on disk but not staged gets one passed step naming it. Per project: the provenance audit of its staged files (`AuditProvenance.py`'s index mode); for Debug and Release an editor half, the strict rendering editor on the staged blobs materialized into a temporary directory (first the build settings (`games.check_build_settings`: `StartScene` set, `Export.BuildScenes` not empty and listing it; ADR 0027 decision 4, B1), then `script.check` and `project.validate` clean, `project.upgrade {dryRun: true}` with no changed file, `test.run {}` with a row for every Editor-mode suite and every `Testing.Replays` match passing at its committed `FinalStateHash` (every checkpoint compared on the way), at least one of those replays with input events (`games.check_replay_acceptance`: a game whose `Testing.Replays` matches no replay, or only replays without input, fails, as the demo acceptance of Roadmap M16, M17, M25 and M27 asks), every replay with input events failing `input.replay {verify, strictHash, withoutInput}` (the input check, §13.6), no unmatched `.replay` below `Assets/`, a clean exit and the copy unchanged outside `Library/`); for Dist a half the Release editor drives (a testing Dist export installed to `bin/Exports/Dist/<Game>/`, `<Game>` being its manifest's `Name`, and run with `--feature-test` in a temporary working directory, each replay's hash equal to the committed one and the editor halves', the same replay rule, then a plain Dist export whose smoke test must exit 0; one that did not run warns, and fails with `--require-gpu`). In a run without Release the Dist half's editor session is its own, so whatever fails after its checks (a refused `session.shutdown`, a broken connection, a timeout, the editor's exit code) fails the Dist step. JUnit `bin/TestResults/Games-<Config>.xml`, reports `Games-<Name>-Editor-<Config>.json` and `Games-<Name>-Dist.{json,xml}`, `<Name>` in step and report names being the `.eproj`'s file stem; the editor and Dist halves need a Vulkan device and follow the automation suite's rule without one. |
| `Format.py [--check]` | clang-format 22.x over first-party C++; skips `Vendor/`. |
| `Lint.py` | `CheckIncludes` step (module rules and the explicit layer-5 DAG from `ModuleRules.json`, plus the NVRHI-free rule for `RenderSnapshot.h`/`DebugDrawList.h`, §3), banned-API scan (incl. `std::sin`/`cos`/`tan`/`asin`/`acos`/`atan`/`atan2`/`exp`/`log`/`pow` in simulation-path modules, §4.12), clang-tidy naming checks, header self-containment (each first-party header compiled alone), Python syntax via `ast.parse` (also against the Python 3.10 grammar; not `compileall`, which writes `.pyc` files into the tree and checks only the running interpreter's grammar) + AST style checks and the 120-column line limit (`python-line-length`, tabs counted as 4), JSON validity of all project files, and the `contract` step: `ENGINE_CONTRACT_STUB` anywhere but its definition in `Core/Base.h` (`contract-stub`) and `doctest::skip` outside a decorator expression that carries `doctest::test_suite(Test::ChildTargetSuite)` (`test-skip`); `--allow-contract-stubs` is contract mode (Roadmap rule 3). `--mode` selects the checkers that need clang tools (clang-tidy naming, clang-query json access): `auto` uses each tool that is installed and the regex fallback otherwise, `clang` requires them, `regex` forces the fallbacks. |
| `GenerateDocs.py [--config Debug\|Release] [--check]` | Runs `Editor --headless --renderer none --dump-reference <dir>` of the given configuration (Debug by default); writes `Docs/Reference/*` (Methods.json, ScriptAPI.md, Components.md, Automation.md, Validation.md, FileFormats.md, BuiltinAssets.md and `Schemas/`), `Resources/Scripting/Engine.d.luau`, `Tools/MCP/catalog.json`. `Docs/Reference/` is fully generated: `--check` fails on any missing, stale or extra file and writing removes extras (ADR 0022 decision 13). |
| `Scaffold.py [--config C] [--check] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | Holds the machine-wide GPU lock for its whole run (below). Regenerates `Projects/FeatureTest`: refuses while an editor holds the project's lock (probed on every OS before anything is removed), removes the scaffolds' outputs, restores `Scaffold/Baseline/`, then runs every scaffold of `Scaffold/Scaffolds.json` in order with its `--id-seed` and renderer (a manifest that lists a file twice or gives two scaffolds one IdSeed is refused, naming both entries; ADR 0028 decision 16); `--check` regenerates a temporary copy and fails, naming each file, unless it is byte-identical outside `Library/` (ADR 0022 decision 8). |
| `CheckBuildConfig.py [--vendor-cache-dir D] [--no-vendor-cache]` | Vendor/consumer define consistency, the Jolt instruction set and the precise floating-point model, for every build target (§2.2); from M15 also the `windows-asan` target: AddressSanitizer in the Release configuration of every project there and in no other configuration or target (ADR 0024 decision 12); from the backlog program's setup also every shared vendor-cache entry an MSBuild build of a Visual Studio target would link: an intact record of exactly the generated project's settings whose flags pass the same rules (rule 9, §2.2; ADR 0028 decision 16). |
| `GenerateDetMathReference.py [--check] [--self-test]` | Writes `Tests/Source/Engine/Core/DetMathReferenceData.h`: seeded DetMath arguments and their correctly rounded results, computed with exact arithmetic and byte-for-byte reproducible (§4.12, `Docs/Decisions/0007-detmath-reference-oracle.md`). `--check` runs the self-test, then compares a fresh run with the committed file; it is one of the static checks (`detmath reference`, §15.8, ADR 0025). `--self-test` checks the rounding routine and known values only. |
| `FetchAssets.py hdri <id> --res 1k|2k|4k --dest <dir>` | Poly Haven download restricted to `api.polyhaven.com` and `dl.polyhaven.org`, md5-verified. Also `font` for the pinned Inter release (sha256), `defaults [--check]` for the committed engine resources under `Resources/` (ADR 0010 decision 25), and `fixture [--check]` for the pinned format fixtures that are not generated first-party: the OTF font and the public-domain MP3 and Ogg Vorbis files (§15.5; sha256, sources and licenses in `Tests/Data/LICENSES.md`; WAV and FLAC are written by `Tests/Data/Generate/MakeAudioFixtures.py`, ADR 0015 decision 25). Downloads need operator approval, which is collected up front in Roadmap M0. |
| `Export.py --project P --config C --out D [--testing] [--smoke-test] [--editor-config Debug\|Release] [--renderer vulkan\|none] [--user-data-dir U] [--engine-cache-dir E] [--timeout S] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | Wraps `Editor --headless --export <D> --config C --export-report <temp>`, holding the machine-wide GPU lock (below) when the editor renders (`--renderer vulkan`, the default) or the smoke test runs the game, and otherwise only waiting until no gate runs; exits with the editor's code (0 exported, 1 failed, 3 project or build output missing; ADR 0024 decision 3), 5 when it stopped the editor at `--timeout`. `--engine-cache-dir` gives the editor its own engine cooked cache instead of the checkout's `bin/EngineCache`. |
| `CheckRuntimeMap.py [--map M] [--config Dist] [--self-test]` | The Dist map-file check of §14.4 (ADR 0024 decision 11): MSVC, lld-link, GNU ld and ld64 maps; `--self-test` runs the seeded fixtures of `Tests/Data/MapFiles/`. |
| `Hardening.py --steps perf,soak,asan-generate,asan-build,asan-unit,asan-feature,fuzz [--gpu-optional] [--soak-only P] [--soak-frames N] [--fuzz-iterations N] [--fuzz-processes N] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | A run with perf, soak or asan-feature holds the machine-wide GPU lock from its start to its end (below). The hardening stage's steps in order, failing fast (§15.1 T6; ADR 0024 decisions 12 to 16 and 19): the perf capture (`Perf.py`, warning-only), the soak (`bin/TestResults/Soak.json`, `Soak-Release.xml`), the AddressSanitizer workspace's generation and build, its unit and feature suites, and the fuzz (`Fuzz-Release-Asan.xml`). The fuzz step is sized from per-target costs (ADR 0028 decision 16, T-FUZZMODEL): each target's seconds per iteration as the last full, passing fuzz step on this machine measured them, in the report it keeps in `<git common dir>/fuzz-costs/Fuzz-Release-Asan.xml` for every checkout (only a passing run of 1,000,000 iterations writes it, so a development or failed run in any checkout never replaces it), or before the first such run as M17's gate did (`Tests/Data/Hardening/FuzzM17.xml`); a target it lacks costs the measured average, and without any measurement the reference run of ADR 0024, 52 min at 19 shards for 35 targets, scaled by the target count, sizes the step. The costs sum into the step's work. Its minimum shard count is the fewest whose expected time (the work over the shard count) fits 80 % of its budget, 5,400 s per 35 targets, and its timeout is the expected time over 80 % at the shard count that runs, counting at most the reference run's 19 shards and the logical CPUs (more shards run no faster), and at most the budget; every target keeps its 1,000,000 iterations. It needs as many logical CPUs and GiB of available memory (shards of 1 GiB) as that minimum, 14 with M17's measured costs; with less, the step refuses to start with exit code 3, before it starts anything. `--soak-only`, `--fuzz-iterations` and `--fuzz-processes` are development aids that turn their step into a warning; `--fuzz-processes` still cannot go below the minimum shard count (exit code 3). `--soak-frames N` (default 10,000; at least that and a multiple of 250) soaks every subject for N ticks or frames, the step's deadline growing in proportion: a collection cycle longer than about a quarter of the soak can leave the script heap rule inconclusive depending only on its phase, and a longer soak of that scene confirms it (ADR 0025). |
| `Perf.py [--config Release] [--output F] [--gpu-optional] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | The Release perf capture into `bin/TestResults/Perf.json`, warning-only (ADR 0024 decision 16), under the machine-wide GPU lock (below). |
| `Locks.py` | Lists the holders of the machine-wide locks (below): for each of gate, gpu and build, free or each holder's pid, worktree, command and since when (and, for build, its mode). It changes nothing. |
| `AuditProvenance.py (--project P [--project P ...] \| --demos \| --self-test) [--files index\|disk]` | The §13.12 demo acceptance audit (M16; ADR 0026 decisions 3 and 4; the rules, finding codes and warnings are `Scripts/Lib/provenance.py`'s): every engine-consumed file of a project (its root `*.eproj` and `.luaurc` and every file below `Assets/`, `.meta` files included; `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `.gitignore` and `Automation/**` are exempt) is checked against the committed `Automation/Provenance.json` and `Automation/BuildLog.jsonl`, and nothing below `Library/` is read. `--files index`, the default and the acceptance mode "from a fresh clone", audits the project's staged blobs, materialized into a temporary directory with `git ls-files` and `git checkout-index` with only the staged `.gitattributes`' conversions (the user's `core.autocrlf`, `core.eol`, `core.attributesFile` and `core.symlinks` overridden, and the system-wide gitattributes file skipped with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM`), so untracked and ignored files are absent, and staged paths that are equal once NFC-normalized and case-folded are `path-collision` findings on every platform (a case-insensitive file system holds one file for them, so a clone there would lose the others); `--files disk` audits the working tree, `*.bak` files left out. `--project` takes a project directory or its `.eproj`; `--demos` audits every game project below `Projects/` and `Tests/Projects/` (`Projects/FeatureTest` excluded; in index mode only the staged ones, each unstaged one named in a passed step); `--self-test` audits the seeded fixtures of `Tests/Data/Provenance/` (written by `Tests/Data/Generate/MakeProvenanceFixtures.py`) against their `Expected.json` and is one of the static checks. Exit codes: 0 passed (warnings allowed), 1 findings or a self-test mismatch, 2 usage error, 3 a missing project, `Automation/Provenance.json` or `Automation/BuildLog.jsonl`, or git missing or failing in index mode. The games suite runs the same index-mode audit of every staged demo (§15.8). |
| `CI.py [--stages ...] [--configs ...] [--milestone] [--contract] [--gpu-optional] [--lock-timeout S \| --no-wait]` | The single entry point (§15.8). Before its first stage it takes the machine-wide gate lock, then the GPU lock, then the build lock uncapped (below), and holds them for its whole run; its children run under them. `--milestone` adds the `hardening` stage to every available stage and requires the clang-cl builds, at each phase's last lander (`Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 12; from M15 to M17 the commit that completed every milestone); it is a usage error together with `--stages`, `--skip-stages`, `--configs`, `--toolset`, `--no-clang-cl`, `--gpu-optional` or `--contract`, and without it the summary reports hardening as not run (ADR 0024 decision 19). Strict by default; `--contract` passes `--allow-contract-stubs` to `Lint.py` and `--allow-skips` to `Test.py`, exactly like `PreCommit.py --contract` (both stay available, but no gate run of the backlog program uses them: a contract checkpoint runs `Lint.py --allow-contract-stubs` and `Test.py --allow-skips` directly, ADR 0028 decision 11); `--gpu-optional` runs the gpu, golden, feature, automation, export, determinism and games stages without `--require-gpu` (hosted runners without a GPU; the determinism stage then leaves missing or stale exported runs out with a warning, and the games stage's editor and Dist halves pass without running). |
| `PreCommit.py [--contract] [--gpu-optional]` | Generate, the static checks of `CI.py`'s lint stage (the one list `Scripts/Lib/scripts.py` `run_static_checks` runs for both gates, §15.8 and §15.9), Debug build, the `references` check (`GenerateDocs.py --check --config Debug`: the generated references match the built editor, §15.9), unit, gpu, golden, feature, automation and games suites in Debug (the automation suite against the Debug editor, in the MCP virtual environment `Setup.py` creates; the games suite audits every staged demo and runs its Debug editor half, with no Dist half because PreCommit builds no Dist, ADR 0026 decision 8; `--gpu-optional` on a machine without a Vulkan device). The gate of a commit straight to main outside a milestone (§15.9); checkpoint commits on a milestone branch run only the tests and suites their change touches, and the milestone end runs `CI.py` instead (ADR 0028 decisions 11 and 12). Strict by default; `--contract` (contract mode: `Lint.py --allow-contract-stubs`, `Test.py --allow-skips`) stays available, but no gate run of the backlog program uses it (up to M17 it was the gate of a milestone's contract commit). |

**Machine-wide locks** (`Scripts/Lib/locks.py`; `Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 16). The checkouts of the repository (the main one and its worktrees) share one machine, so three locks, whose files are in the git common directory (`<git common dir>/machine-locks/`, the same for every worktree), keep them from overloading it:
- **build** (counted): one uncapped build, which holds both slots, or up to two capped builds, one slot each, each running at most its jobs' worth of compilers (`Build.py`'s rule in its row above). An uncapped request first takes a claim that holds off new capped builds until the running ones end, so a stream of capped builds cannot starve it.
- **gpu** (exclusive): one GPU run at a time: `Test.py` with a GPU-using suite, `Scaffold.py`, `Perf.py`, `Hardening.py` with perf, soak or asan-feature, `Export.py` when it renders or smoke-tests, and the MCP bridge (§13.8) from the launch of a rendering editor (`renderer: "vulkan"`) until that editor exits, however it exits, or the bridge itself exits (an editor left running with unsaved changes holds none, below).
- **gate** (exclusive): `CI.py` holds it for its whole run and takes the gpu and then the build lock after it, so its stages begin once the runs that held them have ended. While the gate is held, every other build or gpu acquisition, every `Test.py` run, every `Export.py` run and every editor launch of the bridge waits.

The order is gate, then gpu, then build, and nothing that holds a later lock waits for an earlier one. A holder exports its locks in `ENGINE_HELD_LOCKS` (`<lock>=<token>` pairs), and a process whose environment names a lock that its file still records and that is still held runs under that hold: it neither waits for it nor takes another build slot. So `CI.py`'s own `Build.py` and `Test.py` runs, the automation suite's bridge tests under its `Test.py`, and `Hardening.py`'s children run under their parent's locks, and a process inside a run that holds any lock never waits for a gate that began after that run (the gate waits for the run instead; waiting for the gate would deadlock). An MCP client that starts the bridge with an environment it curates (the MCP SDK's stdio client passes only an allow-list and the variables it is given) must pass `ENGINE_HELD_LOCKS`, and `ENGINE_LOCK_DIRECTORY` when set, or the bridge waits for the hold it runs under. Every wait polls and reports the holder (pid, worktree, command and since when) when it starts, when the holder changes and every five minutes. Whether a lock is held is learned by taking it and letting it go at once, so a free lock can look held for the moment another process probes it: a wait polls a few more times, 50 ms apart, before it refuses, and a waiting uncapped build gives up its claim only for a gate that stays held. The scripts bound it with `--lock-timeout S` (3 h by default; then exit code 5) and refuse at once with `--no-wait` (exit code 3); the bridge waits at most 30 min and then fails the launch with `LockUnavailable`, naming the holder, without spawning an editor. Each lock is an operating-system lock on its file (`msvcrt.locking` of one byte far past the holder record on Windows, `fcntl.flock` on POSIX), which the operating system releases when its holder's process ends, however it ends: a crashed holder never wedges the machine, and nothing is ever cleared by hand. `Locks.py` lists the holders; a holder that should not run any more is stopped like any process. `ENGINE_LOCK_DIRECTORY` replaces the directory (the tests' own locks), and a checkout without git keeps them in `bin-int/machine-locks/`. Processes started directly take no lock: a Tests or Editor binary run by hand, or an editor the bridge leaves running with unsaved changes when its MCP client goes away (its hold ends with the bridge).

---

## 3. Module architecture and dependency rules

All engine code is in `namespace Engine`. Sub-namespaces: `Utils`, `Detail` (CodeStyle), `Automation` (method handler free functions), `ScriptBindings` (Luau C functions), `Lua` (Scripting's checked helpers and protected VM entry points, §11.4/§11.7; `Docs/Decisions/0021-review-fixes.md`) and `Test` (test support). EditorCore, Editor, Runtime and Tests code also lives in `namespace Engine`; editor classes carry an `Editor` prefix where a name would be ambiguous (`EditorContext`, `EditorCamera`).

| L | Module (`Engine/Source/Engine/…`) | Contents | May include | Third-party (public headers / .cpp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `Core` | Base, Assert, Log (LogContext, RingBufferSink), Result/Error, FatalError, UUID and UUIDGenerator, Hash (XXH64, FNV-1a), Random, DetMath, Time/Clock (System, Manual, Scripted), FixedStepScheduler, Json (Reader/Writer), VirtualFileSystem (VfsPath; native-directory, memory and overlay mounts), FileSystem, PathGlob, Utf8, BinaryReader/Writer, Buffer, Aabb, JobSystem, MainThreadQueue, EventLog, Handle, Profiler, UniqueFunction | — | glm, spdlog (`Log.h` only; also Core's `.cpp` and `Private/` files), `nlohmann/json_fwd.hpp` / nlohmann |
| 1 | `Reflection` | TypeRegistry, TypeList (the compile-time type lists, such as `BuiltinComponents`), TypeInfo, StructInfo, ComponentInfo, FieldInfo, FieldType, EnumInfo, Value, VariantValue, MergePatch, ValidationContext, RandomValueGenerator, JSON Schema generation, fuzzy name suggestion | Core | — |
| 1 | `Platform` | Window, GlfwLibrary, Events, Input (InputState, InputActionMap, KeyCodes), Process, CrashHandler, ErrorDialog, Environment, Paths, PollingFileWatcher, ProjectLock, ResourcePatcher (M15, §14.2), NativeFileDialog (M18; a Windows backend only, ADR 0029), SecureRandom, Socket, ThreadClock; OS code in `Windows/` and `Posix/` (shared by Linux and macOS; `Linux/` and `MacOS/` when a difference grows, Appendix A), helpers both halves share in `Private/` | Core | — / GLFW, OS headers (in the OS folders), Vulkan-Headers (`GlfwLibrary.cpp` only: GLFW gets the engine's loader before `glfwInit`, §4.1) |
| 1 | `Graphics` | GraphicsDevice, GraphicsSpecification, RenderContext (what `Application::OnRender` receives, §4.1), VulkanDispatch (the process-level loader, §8.1), DeviceSelection, Swapchain, FramePacer, HostImageUpload, OffscreenTarget, ShaderLibrary, ShaderReflection, PipelineFactory, RenderTargetPool, Readback, Image (the PNG codec), GpuProfiler, GpuResourceTracker, GpuDiagnostics, GpuRecreation (M18: the recreation registry of the device-loss recovery, ADR 0029) | Core, Platform, `ShaderShared` | NVRHI, Vulkan-Headers / GLFW (`GraphicsDevice.cpp` and `Swapchain.cpp` only: instance extensions, presentation support and the surface, ADR 0005 decision 8), stb (`Image.cpp` and `ThirdParty/StbImageImplementation.cpp`, ADR 0009 decision 11) |
| 1 | `Physics` | PhysicsEngine, PhysicsWorld (ECS-agnostic), PhysicsTypes, PhysicsDiagnostics, PhysicsLayers, PhysicsShape, ContactBuffer, PhysicsQueries, CharacterController; it takes mesh geometry as vertex and index arrays and layers as names, since it cannot include `Asset` or `Project` | Core, Reflection | — / Jolt (pimpl, `Private/`) |
| 1 | `Audio` | AudioEngine (ECS-agnostic), voices, groups, AudioTypes (the shared vocabulary, `Attenuation` and `AudioGroup` included), AudioVfs (the VFS bridge), AudioDecoder (probing for the importer, decoding for the engine), SoundSynth | Core | — / miniaudio |
| 1 | `Automation/Protocol` | JSON-RPC framing and messages, the handshake, ProtocolServer (transport), the session file, Dispatcher, MethodRegistry, MethodContext (the per-request context handlers receive), MetaBuilder, PendingOperation, JsonReference (result references of batches, §13.4), result offload, watchdog | Core, Reflection, Platform | nlohmann |
| 2 | `Asset` | AssetHandle, TypedAssetHandle, AssetRef, AssetType (and its registration), AssetReference, AssetMetadata, AssetRegistry, AssetDiagnostic, the `AssetManager` interface, the `IAssetLoader` registry (`AssetLoaderRegistry`), `RuntimeAssetManager` (paks), cooked formats and loaders (CPU data: MeshData, TextureData, MaterialData, EnvironmentData, AudioClipData, ScriptData, FontData, ReplayData, DocumentData, SpriteSheetData (M18)), built-ins, the pak format, reader and mount, the source-format table (§15.6 gate 9), `IEnvironmentBaker`, `IScriptDiagnosticsProvider` | Core, Reflection, Platform | — |
| 2 | `Project` | ProjectSettings (the reflected settings structs, §6.1), ProjectSerializer (`.eproj`), GameManifest (`Game.json`, §14.1); ADR 0006 decision 4, ADR 0012 decision 1 | Core, Reflection, Platform | — |
| 3 | `Renderer` | RenderSnapshot (with M18's output-stage mapping), DebugDrawList, SceneRenderer, RenderStage and RenderPassDeclaration (M18: the stages and the pass declarations, ADR 0029), SceneTargetFormats, RenderPrepare, RenderStats, PassBindingCache, BrdfLut, the passes (ShadowPass, DepthPyramidPass, GtaoPass, SkyboxPass, BloomPass, TonemapPass, FxaaPass, BlitPass, SelectionPass, TrianglePass, DebugRenderer, TextRenderer, M18's UpscalePass), QuadBatcher (M18), ShadowCascades, SpotShadowAtlas, GpuResourceCache, StaleMirrorSchedule, EnvironmentBaker, BlueNoise, TextLayout, AsyncPicker, EditorOverlay, ViewportCapture, camera math | Core, Platform, Graphics, Asset, `ShaderShared` | NVRHI |
| 3 | `ImGui` | ImGuiRenderer (NVRHI backend), ImGuiLayer, ImGuiScreenshot, StatsOverlay (the Runtime's F1 overlay, M15), GLFW glue, theme | Core, Platform, Graphics, `ShaderShared` | imgui, ImGuizmo, NVRHI / GLFW and Vulkan-Headers (the GLFW glue `ImGui/ImGuiGlfwImplementation.cpp` only, §2.2) |
| 3 | `Scene` | Scene, Entity, components, component registration and access (ComponentAccess, ComponentHostOps), hierarchy, TransformSystem, SceneSerializer (LoadReport, Migrations, StructuralValidator), ChangeTracker, Prefab, PrefabAsset, PrefabInstantiator, PhysicsSystem (with PhysicsComposition, PhysicsValidation and ColliderDebugDraw), AudioSystem (with its private `AudioSourceRuntime`), RenderExtraction, RenderAnnotations, SceneRaycast, EntityBounds | Core, Reflection, Platform, Asset, Physics, Audio, and only `Renderer/RenderSnapshot.h` + `Renderer/DebugDrawList.h` | EnTT |
| 4 | `Scripting` | ScriptEngine, Sandbox, LoadTimeVm, bindings (RegisterBindings), ScriptApiRegistry, ScriptProxy, LuaHelpers, RequireResolver, ScriptCompiler, TaskScheduler, ScriptReference (retained functions, coroutines and tasks), ScriptTime (the ticks of a script wait), Watchdog, TrackingAllocator, ScriptLimits, ScriptError, ScriptHost, ScriptTestHost | ≤3 | — / Luau (VM, Compiler, Config, Require) |
| 5 | `Session` | PlaySession (runtime scene + PhysicsWorld + ScriptEngine + voices), PlayInput and its event codec, step order, lockstep, state hash, ReplayRecorder, ReplayPlayer | ≤4 | — |
| 5 | `Testing` | FeatureTestRunner (suites, cases, replays, scripted clock), CoverageGates (counters and gates), ImageCompare, TestResults (result schema, JUnit writer) | ≤4, `Session` | — (PNG through `Graphics/Image`, ADR 0009 decision 11) |
| 5 | `Automation/Methods` | Handlers shared by Editor and Runtime (session, play, observe, entity, scene, script, input, replay, screenshot, raycast, physics, audio, stats), AutomationMethodContext, RuntimeAutomationServer | ≤4, `Session` | — |
| 5 | `AssetPipeline` | `EditorAssetManager` (source → cook → load), `IAssetImporter` and `ImporterRegistry`, the importers (glTF, texture, environment, font, audio, material, sound effect, script, scene, prefab, replay, sprite sheet (M18)), AssetCache (the project and engine cooked caches), EngineAssetBaker, AssetDependencyGraph, AssetHotReloader, AssetWriter, PakWriter | ≤4 | — / cgltf, stb (stb_image, stb_image_resize2), `imstb_truetype.h`, MikkTSpace (`Private/TangentGenerator.cpp` only, ADR 0010 decision 16) |
| 5 | `App` | ProcessContext, Application, EngineContext, EntryPoint, FrameLoop, CommandLine, ExitCode, ProjectSettingsApplication, VulkanErrorHandler | ≤4, `Session`, `Testing`, `Automation/Methods` (never `AssetPipeline`) | — / Vulkan-Headers (the frame-boundary `vk::SystemError` catch in `App/FrameLoop.cpp`, §4.6, and `VulkanErrorHandler`) |

| Project | May include |
|---|---|
| `EditorCore` (StaticLib, no ImGui) | all Engine modules except `ImGui`; LuauAnalysis, plus the Luau Ast and Common headers its API is written in (only `EditorCore/Scripting/ScriptTypeChecker.cpp`, which `EditorCore` registers as the `IScriptDiagnosticsProvider` used by `ScriptImporter`) |
| `Editor` (exe) | everything |
| `Runtime` (exe) | Engine except `AssetPipeline`; no `Editor*` headers; `ImGui` only under `#if !defined(ENGINE_DIST)` |
| `Tests` (exe) | everything |

`EditorCore` also holds `EngineAssetGenerators` (the editor's generators of the Generated built-ins, ADR 0013 decision 8) and `Audio/AudioPreview` (the asset browser's preview, ADR 0015 decision 17).

`ShaderShared` (`Resources/Shaders/Shared`, the headers C++ and Slang share, §8.4) includes only itself and the standard library; `Graphics`, `Renderer`, `ImGui`, `EditorCore`, `Editor` and `Tests` may include it. In Tests only `Support/PlatformProbes.cpp` has OS headers. `Scripts/ModuleRules.json` is the enforced form of both tables, and a change to one changes the other.

Rules, enforced by the `CheckIncludes` step of `Scripts/Lint.py` against `Scripts/ModuleRules.json`:
1. A module includes only lower layers and the same-layer exceptions listed above. Layer 5 is an explicit DAG, written into `ModuleRules.json` as allowed edges: `Session` → {`Testing`, `Automation/Methods`} → `App`, with `AssetPipeline` beside them, where X → Y means Y may include X. `AssetPipeline` includes no other layer-5 module and none of them includes it. Same-layer edges not listed are errors, so a cycle cannot form.
2. `Renderer` never includes `Scene`; it renders a plain `RenderSnapshot`. `Asset` holds CPU data only; the renderer owns GPU mirrors. Physics never includes `Asset`: Scene hands it a mesh's `MeshData` as vertex and index arrays (ADR 0014 decision 2), and it never touches the renderer.
3. Public headers never expose GLFW, Jolt, Luau, miniaudio, cgltf or stb types. NVRHI handles may appear in `Graphics`, `Renderer` and `ImGui` headers; EnTT only in `Scene` headers. In `Core`, spdlog appears only in `Log.h` among public headers (Core `.cpp` files and `Private/` headers may use it), so no other Core header leaks it into every module. `Renderer/RenderSnapshot.h` and `Renderer/DebugDrawList.h`, the only Renderer headers `Scene` may include, include nothing beyond `Core`, `Asset` and the standard library, and in particular no NVRHI. A dedicated lint rule checks their transitive includes.
4. Dependency inversion keeps layers intact. `Asset` defines the `IAssetLoader` registry, the `AssetManager` interface, `IEnvironmentBaker` and `IScriptDiagnosticsProvider`. `AssetPipeline` defines `IAssetImporter` and `ImporterRegistry`, registers the importers and provides `EditorAssetManager` (ADR 0010 decision 1); the Runtime never calls that registration, so the linker drops importers. `Renderer` implements `IEnvironmentBaker`, and `EditorCore` registers the type checker as `IScriptDiagnosticsProvider`. `EditorApp` constructs an `EditorAssetManager` and `RuntimeApp` a `RuntimeAssetManager`, and each injects it into `EngineContext` as `AssetManager&`.
5. No mutable globals except **process-level state** (§4.1): the logger registry, the profiler's per-thread rings (constructed and ordered like the logger's, ADR 0003 decision 6), the assert handler, the fatal-error handler, the crash handler, the current `ProcessContext`'s registration (`ProcessContext::GetCurrent`), the Luau process state (fast flags, assert bridge, monotonic VM identity counter, ADR 0019, and the live-VM count that `ShutdownScriptingRuntime` asserts is zero, ADR 0025), Jolt's allocator hooks, `Factory::sInstance` and its `JobSystemThreadPool`, the Vulkan loader with its memory-allocation hook (`Graphics/VulkanDispatch.cpp`), vulkan.hpp's default dispatcher, and GLFW's library state. `ProcessContext` initializes all of them once from `EntryPoint`, or from the Tests main.

---

## 4. Core

### 4.1 Application lifecycle
```cpp
struct ApplicationSpecification                     // abridged; Application.h documents every member
{
	std::string Name;
	std::filesystem::path UserDataRoot;             // --user-data-dir; empty: the OS's
	CommandLine Args;
	WindowMode Window = WindowMode::Windowed;       // Windowed | Headless (GLFW null platform)
	WindowSpecification WindowSettings;
	FrameLoopConfig Loop;                           // FixedHz 60, MaxStepsPerFrame 5, MaxFrameDelta 0.25
	ClockKind Clock = ClockKind::System;            // System | Manual (headless/lockstep); Scripted only per test suite
	std::optional<uint64_t> MaxFrames;              // --frames
	RendererMode Renderer = RendererMode::Vulkan;   // Vulkan | None (logic-only)
	GraphicsSpecification Graphics;                 // validation, GPU override, frames in flight (2), API cap
	std::optional<AudioEngineSpecification> Audio;  // device and decoding; nullopt: GetDefaultAudioSpecification(Window) (§10.1)
	std::optional<LogLevel> MinimumLogLevel;        // --log-level: the output sinks only (§4.4, §13.9)
	// also: EnginePak, the engine resource and cache directories, ExpectNoErrors, ExpectNoGpuErrors, ThrottleHeadless,
	// WorkerCount, RegisterTypes; outside Dist, EnableImGui and ImGuiIniPath
};

class Application
{
public:
	explicit Application(ApplicationSpecification specification);
	virtual ~Application();
	[[nodiscard]] int Run();                        // returns a process exit code (§4.1 table)
	void RequestExit(int exitCode = ExitCode::Success);
	[[nodiscard]] EngineContext& GetContext();
protected:
	[[nodiscard]] virtual Status OnInitialize() { return {}; }
	virtual void OnShutdown() {}
	virtual void OnEvent(Event& event) {}
	virtual void OnSafePoint() {}                   // before the steps: automation requests run here (§4.2 step 3)
	virtual void OnFixedStep(const SimStep& step) {}  // 0..N per frame
	virtual void OnUpdate(const FrameTime& frame) {}
	virtual void OnRender(RenderContext& context) {}
#if !defined(ENGINE_DIST)
	virtual void OnImGuiRender() {}                 // Dist has no ImGui (§14.4; ADR 0024 decision 11)
#endif
	virtual void OnRenderSubmitted(uint64_t frameIndex, uint64_t submissionId);  // after the frame's submit, before present
	[[nodiscard]] Status RequestOffscreenUiFrame();  // Unsupported without a device or ImGui (always in Dist)
};
```
There is no layer stack: `EditorApp` and `RuntimeApp` are the only subclasses. `EngineContext` owns every per-context service (`VirtualFileSystem`, `JobSystem`, `MainThreadQueue`, `Window`, `InputState`, `GraphicsDevice*` (null with `RendererMode::None`), `AssetManager&` (injected, §3 rule 4), `AudioEngine`, `TypeRegistry`, `ScriptApiRegistry`, `EventLog`). Engine code receives `EngineContext&` or the specific service explicitly; there is no `Application::Get()`.

**Initialization has two levels**, because GLFW, the Vulkan loader and Jolt's factory are process-global, and GLFW is main-thread-only with one platform per process:
1. **Process level** (`App/ProcessContext`, created once by `RunApplication` or the Tests main and destroyed last): Log → Profiler → CrashHandler → Luau fast flags → `PhysicsEngine::Initialize` (the `Physics` step, configured by `ProcessContextSpecification::Physics`: Jolt allocator, `Factory::sInstance`, `RegisterTypes`, version check, `JobSystemThreadPool` with `WorkerThreads` threads, by default `clamp(hardware concurrency − 2, 1, 4)`; §9.1, ADR 0014 decision 1) → if rendering, the Vulkan loader (`vk::detail::DynamicLoader`, `VULKAN_HPP_DEFAULT_DISPATCHER.init(vkGetInstanceProcAddr)`, §8.1) and `glfwInitVulkanLoader(vkGetInstanceProcAddr)` → `glfwInitHint(GLFW_PLATFORM, …)`, with the platform chosen **once** (null for headless, native otherwise) → `glfwInit()`. A process is therefore either windowed or headless, never both.
2. **Per context** (`Application`/`EngineContext`, the `EngineContextStep` order): Services (VFS, MainThreadQueue, JobSystem, EventLog, InputState, and the TypeRegistry, which is built and frozen first; ADR 0008 decision 2) → UserData (`user://`) → EngineResources (`engine://` and `enginecache://`, or an exported game's `Engine.pak`) → Window (`GLFW_NO_API`) → Graphics (the `shaders://` mount of dev builds, the GraphicsDevice, the ShaderLibrary and the PipelineFactory) → Audio. The AssetManager is injected (`SetAssetManager`), not a step. The AudioEngine is the `Audio` step after Graphics and exists only when `EngineContextSpecification::Audio` is set: every `Application` sets it, from `ApplicationSpecification::Audio` or `GetDefaultAudioSpecification(WindowMode)` (windowed runs: the System device and threaded decoding; headless runs: no device and deterministic decoding), while in-process test contexts usually have none (`GetAudioEngine()` is null and play sessions are silent). It is the context's last member, so it is destroyed first, after its owners (play sessions, the editor's preview) released their voices. `Application` updates it after `OnUpdate` with the frame clock's accumulated unscaled time and unscaled delta, so the frame phase has set the voices before a device-less engine pulls the frame (ADR 0015 decisions 3 and 14).

Each step returns `Status`. On failure, the completed steps are torn down in reverse and `Run` returns `ExitCode::InitFailed`. Teardown is the reverse order and asserts that every live-object counter (GPU resources, Jolt worlds and bodies, audio voices, Luau VMs: `ShutdownScriptingRuntime` asserts that no `Sandbox` VM is open, ADR 0025) is zero. Tests may build several `EngineContext`s side by side in one process, as long as they share that process's GLFW platform (headless or `RendererMode::None`). Windowed GPU tests (swapchain, present, minimize) run in a child process, `Tests --windowed-child=<name>`, spawned like a death test (§4.5).

`Engine/App/EntryPoint.h` exposes `int RunApplication(int argc, char** argv, ApplicationFactory factory)`. It creates the `ProcessContext`, catches `std::exception` as a last resort (→ fatal), and maps outcomes to **exit codes**, which CI, the MCP bridge and the exporter rely on:

| Code | Name | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `Success` | |
| 1 | `Failed` | tests, validation, `--expect-no-errors` or a replay expectation failed |
| 2 | `UsageError` | bad command line |
| 3 | `InitFailed` | no Vulkan loader or suitable device, project failed to load, project locked by another editor (§4.13), required file missing |
| 4 | `Crash` | fatal error (incl. device loss, GPU out-of-memory or hang, §8.14), unhandled exception, signal, assert in a non-debugger run |
| 5 | `Timeout` | watchdog expiry; a script that stops a process at its own timeout (`Export.py --timeout`, `Test.py`) exits with it too (ADR 0024 decision 3: the timeouts belong to the calling scripts, and the engine has no `--timeout` option) |

### 4.2 Main loop: fixed-step simulation, variable rendering
```cpp
struct FrameSteps { uint32_t StepCount = 0; double Alpha = 0.0; double DroppedSeconds = 0.0; uint64_t FirstTick = 0; };
class FixedStepScheduler   // pure; exhaustive table tests
{
public:
	explicit FixedStepScheduler(const FrameLoopConfig& config);
	[[nodiscard]] FrameSteps Advance(double realDeltaSeconds, double timeScale);
	[[nodiscard]] FrameSteps StepExactly(uint32_t stepCount);  // ManualClock frames and lockstep play.step: Alpha = 1
	void Reset();                                              // tick 0 for a new play session
	[[nodiscard]] uint64_t GetTick() const;
};
```
`Clock` is an interface. `SystemClock` (steady clock) is used for windowed runs. `ManualClock` advances exactly one `FixedDelta` per frame and is used for headless runs, lockstep play and most tests. With `ManualClock` every frame contains exactly one fixed step and `Alpha` is defined as 1, so screenshots show the exact simulated state. `ScriptedClock` replays a table of frame deltas (cycled). The FeatureTest `Timing` suite (§15.5) uses it to run zero-, one- and multi-step frames, clamping and dropped time end to end, deterministically. At most `MaxStepsPerFrame` (5) steps run per frame; time beyond that is dropped and reported in `stats.get`.

Headless play without lockstep, for example a human watching a headless editor over `viewport.screenshot`, throttles the loop to `FixedHz` frames per wall-clock second, so a `ManualClock` session never runs unthrottled. Lockstep, batch runs and test runs are the only unthrottled modes, and in them time advances only on request.

**Minimized windows** (0×0 framebuffer) skip extraction, rendering and present. The loop then waits in `glfwWaitEventsTimeout(FixedDelta)` instead of `glfwPollEvents`, so the CPU idles instead of spinning without GPU pacing. Simulation and audio keep running in real time. The editor keeps pumping automation, and lockstep is unaffected. Games that want to pause on minimize react to `WindowFocusEvent` through `Application.IsFocused()`.

Frame:
1. `Window::PollEvents()` (or `WaitEventsTimeout` when minimized) → events → `InputState` (real devices and injected events share this path).
2. `MainThreadQueue::Drain()`: job completions, asset swaps, file-watcher results, in submission order. Stale job completions are dropped (§7.5).
3. `AutomationServer::Pump(budget 4 ms)` (Editor; Runtime non-Dist with `--automation`): runs queued requests at a safe point and polls pending operations.
4. `steps = scheduler.Advance(clock.Delta(), timeScale)`. In lockstep, the steps are those requested by `play.step`, at most as many as fit the 50 ms per-frame budget (§13.6).
5. `OnFixedStep` × steps → `PlaySession::FixedStep` (§5.7).
6. `OnUpdate(frame)` → `PlaySession::FrameUpdate` (script `OnUpdate`/`OnLateUpdate`, §5.7), editor camera and UI. `frame.Alpha` is already known here, so `Transform.RenderPosition` and `Time.GetInterpolationAlpha()` return this frame's interpolated values (§5.2).
7. `OnRender`: snapshot extraction with interpolation `Alpha`, `SceneRenderer`, ImGui, present, `runGarbageCollection()`. `App/FrameLoop.cpp` wraps the whole frame in the single allowlisted frame-boundary `catch` for `vk::SystemError` (§4.6, §8.14).

### 4.3 Platform, window, input
- **Window** is one concrete GLFW class (no per-OS hierarchy), created with `GLFW_CLIENT_API = GLFW_NO_API`. GLFW itself is initialized at process level (§4.1). `Headless` processes use `glfwInitHint(GLFW_PLATFORM, GLFW_PLATFORM_NULL)` (the null backend is built on every OS in the vendored GLFW 3.5.1), so time, input, window logic and `imgui_impl_glfw` run unchanged without a display. GLFW is initialized after `glfwInitVulkanLoader(vkGetInstanceProcAddr)` so it shares the engine's loader. Framebuffer size and window size are handled separately (Retina, DPI scale).
- **Events** are `using Event = std::variant<WindowCloseEvent, WindowResizeEvent, WindowFocusEvent, KeyEvent, CharEvent, MouseButtonEvent, MouseMoveEvent, MouseScrollEvent, FileDropEvent, GamepadEvent>`, handled with `std::visit` and an `Overloaded` helper, with a `Handled` flag. There is no event class hierarchy.
- **InputState** is plain data owned by `EngineContext`: keys, mouse buttons, cursor, scroll, up to 4 gamepads (GLFW mappings). `InputState::Inject(const Event&)` is the only write path, used by GLFW callbacks, automation and tests.
- **Phase-aware edges.** `InputState` keeps two latched views. `LatchStep()` runs at the start of every fixed step and records `Pressed`/`Released` edges accumulated since the previous step. `LatchFrame()` runs once per frame before `OnUpdate`. A tap that goes down and up between two steps reports both edges on the next step. Script queries read the step view inside `OnFixedUpdate` and physics callbacks, and the frame view inside `OnUpdate`/`OnLateUpdate`. Edges are therefore never dropped or doubled, whatever the frame rate.
- **Actions.** `InputActionMap` resolves the project's named actions: `Button` actions (any bound key, mouse button or gamepad button) and `Axis` actions (positive/negative bindings plus an optional gamepad axis with dead zone 0.15, combined as the max magnitude). Binding names: `Key.Space`, `Mouse.Left`, `Gamepad.South`, `Gamepad.LeftX`. **Axis convention:** every engine axis is right-positive and up-positive. GLFW reports the stick Y axes as positive-down, so `InputState` negates `LeftY` and `RightY` when it reads GLFW, and `Gamepad.LeftY` bound to `MoveZ` means forward is positive. An axis action may set `"Invert": true` to flip its gamepad axis for players who want it. A unit table test covers both sticks and the inversion.
- In the editor, real input reaches the game only while the Game view has captured it: a click on its image captures it, and it stays captured while that window keeps focus, the image is held or the game has locked the cursor; Escape, focus moving elsewhere, native window blur and the end of play release it (releasing the held input of an ordinary session, never of a lockstep one). Injected input always reaches the game and never the editor UI.
- **View coordinates.** Scripts measure the screen in the pixels of the session's view (`PlaySession::SetViewSize`). `Input.GetMousePosition`, `Application.GetWindowSize` and `Camera:ScreenToWorldRay`/`WorldToScreen` all use it, origin top-left. Ordinary live play's view is the image its game camera renders into: the Runtime's framebuffer, the editor's Game panel image (its fixed resolution, or the panel's extent times the framebuffer scale, letterbox bars excluded), followed as either changes.
