Names and messages come from scripts, bounded as §11.1 says (a suite or case name at most 256 bytes; a message at most 8 KiB of the script's text, with authored locations, before the sanitizing below, which can expand one control byte into the six-byte escape `\u00XX`; at most 32 script-driven failures per row, the last then counting any more as `[truncated: <n> more failures]`, and 1,024 per suite), so the runner makes every reported text valid for JSON and XML: invalid UTF-8 bytes (and U+FFFE/U+FFFF) become U+FFFD and control characters other than tab, line feed and carriage return become the visible text `\u00XX`. **`filter`** (in `test.run`, `--run-tests --filter`) is a case-insensitive substring matched against `"<suite>/<case>"`, so `"Tetris"` selects a suite and `"Tetris/hard drop"` one case. Replays match on their path.

The runner is part of the `Testing` module, which stays in Dist, so exported Release and Dist runtimes execute exactly the same suites and replays (§14.4). The test template, a documentation example and a TypeChecker fixture keep this shape type-checked.

---

## 12. Editor

### 12.1 Structure
- **`EditorCore`** (static library, no ImGui, linked by `Editor` and `Tests`): `EditorContext` (project, **one** open scene (v1 edits a single scene at a time; opening another requires saving or discarding, §13.5), `SelectionSet`, `CommandHistory`, `PlaySession`, diagnostics, editor camera state), `ProjectManager` (create from template, open, recent list in `user://Editor.json`), commands (§12.3), `AutomationServer` and all editor method handlers (`EditorCore/Automation/*Methods.cpp`), `Exporter`, `ScriptTypeChecker`, `ProjectValidator` (§13.7), `Autosave`.
- **`Editor`** (executable): `EditorApp`, `EditorLayer`, panels, gizmo, drawers. Panels read `EditorContext` every frame and never treat ImGui state as truth; every mutation goes through `EditorContext::Execute(Scope<Command>)`, the same entry point automation uses.
  M10 keeps CPU camera, gizmo-preview, reflected-edit, viewport and UI-state logic in EditorCore for tests without ImGui; the panels, ImGuizmo adapter and drawers remain in Editor (ADR 0018). A gizmo gesture changes an owned scene preview, then commits once on release; a conflicting edit cancels it before any live write. Preview state is excluded from simulation and autosave.
- CLI: `Editor [--help | -h]`, or `Editor [--project <path.eproj or its directory>] [--read-only] [--strict] [--headless] [--renderer vulkan|none] [--automation[=port]] [--automation-test-hooks] [--batch f.jsonl [--id-seed N]] [--run-tests [--filter F] [--junit out.xml]] [--check-scripts] [--upgrade] [--export <dir> --config Debug|Release|Dist [--testing] [--smoke-test] [--export-report out.json]] [--export-root <dir>] [--bake-engine-assets] [--engine-cache-dir <dir>] [--dump-reference <dir>] [--frames N [--viewport-screenshot <path>] [--editor-screenshot <path>]] [--gpu-validation] [--vulkan-api=1.3]`, plus the engine options of §13.9. `ParseEditorLaunchOptions` (`EditorCore/EditorCommandLine.h`) and `CreateEditorApp` hold the option-combination rules, which their tests pin (ADR 0008 decision 14). `--help` (or `-h`, the one single-dash option, which `ExpandEditorShortOptions` spells `--help` wherever it stands as an option) prints the usage, every engine and editor option with its one-line description (`CommandLine::FormatUsage`), to standard output and exits 0 from `RunEditor` (`Editor/EditorApp.h`) before any application or process context exists, so it writes no log file, creates no user-data directory and loads no Vulkan loader; the other arguments must still parse, so an unknown option stays a usage error (exit code 2), but no combination rule is checked (ADR 0026, friction F20). `--strict` reads every project the editor opens strictly, so unknown keys, fields and components in its `.eproj`, scenes and prefabs are errors instead of warnings, and a scene or prefab that does not load strictly for any reason refuses the project, naming the file (§6); it combines with every other option, and the feature, determinism and export suites pass it (§15.8; ADR 0025). One-shot validation is `--batch` with a `project.validate` line, and timeouts belong to the calling scripts (§4.1; ADR 0024 decision 3). `--upgrade` opens the project with its asset scan deferred, so opening writes nothing (`EditorContextSpecification::DeferAssetScan`), then runs the `cli` batch `[project.refreshAssets, project.upgrade]` with both requests transcribed: `project.refreshAssets` runs the deferred scan first, so the `.meta` files it writes are attributed to that request (id 1) and the rewrites to `project.upgrade` (id 2), each with its own transcript line, and a failure of either ends the run non-zero with its response line transcribed (§13.12; ADR 0026 decision 11). `--export` exits 0 when it exported, 1 when the export failed, 3 when the project does not open, its lock is held or a build output is missing (`Exporter::IsMissingBuildOutput`), and 2 for a usage error, `--frames` included (a frame limit would end the run before the export); an editor that shuts down while its export still runs finishes it as a failure (exit 1, the report written). It never replaces the project, the checkout, the working directory, the user-data root, a file-system root or a non-empty directory that holds no earlier export, which it recognizes only by a `Game.json` (on macOS `<Name>.app/Contents/Resources/Game.json`) that loads as a `GameManifest` (§14.2; ADR 0025). Without `--project`, the windowed editor shows the `ProjectLauncher`, and `--automation` starts in the **launcher state**: only `session.*`, `rpc.discover`, `docs.get`, `project.create` and `project.open` are available, and every other method returns `InvalidState` ("no project open").

### 12.2 Panels
| Panel | Function |
|---|---|
| `SceneHierarchyPanel` | tree with drag reparent/reorder, multi-select, search, active toggles, prefab instance badges |
| `InspectorPanel` | registry-driven drawers; Add Component menu by category; prefab override highlighting with per-field revert; material and import-settings editing for the selected asset |
| `SceneViewportPanel` | `EditorCamera` (orbit Alt+LMB, pan MMB, zoom wheel, fly RMB+WASD), ImGuizmo translate/rotate/scale (W/E/R) in local/world space with snapping (Ctrl: 0.5 m, 15°, 0.1), GPU picking, outline, grid, icons, collider view, debug-view dropdown, asset drag-drop (mesh → entity, prefab → instance, HDR → Environment). Viewport icons (camera, lights, audio, triggers) come from the renderer (`RenderViewFlags::Icons`: `Scene/RenderAnnotations` collects them, `Renderer/EditorOverlay` draws them, the scene view's single producer); content-browser and hierarchy type icons are first-party `ImDrawList` vector glyphs (`Editor/Icons.cpp`). Both are procedural, so no icon image set or license is needed. The image is one ImGui item owning left, right and middle presses (navigation keeps running while its button is held; the first press on an unfocused view focuses it and acts); the wheel zooms the hovered view; the gizmo shows for the selection even while another panel has focus |
| `GameViewportPanel` | primary camera at fixed or free resolution, letterboxed; receives game input once a click on its image captured it (§4.3), in view coordinates; "Agent controls time" banner in lockstep |
| `ContentBrowserPanel` | folder tree + grid (the listing is cached and rebuilt only after a change, never walked per frame), offscreen-rendered cached thumbnails, create (folder, scene, material, script, prefab, sound effect), rename/move (handles survive), delete to `Library/Trash/` (undoable), OS file drop import (a drop opens and focuses the panel; drops that arrive while it was hidden ask for their destination folder), import-error badges; opening a scene over unsaved changes asks to save or discard first |
| `ConsolePanel` | ring-buffer log + script errors, level/logger filters, click opens file:line in the external editor (started without captured output and detached when the editor exits, so it outlives it) and selects the entity |
| `DiagnosticsPanel` | asset, validation and type-check diagnostics with auto-fix buttons |
| `ProjectSettingsPanel` | window, simulation, physics layers and collision matrix, input actions, export, scripting (reflected `ProjectSettings`) |
| `StatsPanel` | frame time, per-pass GPU time, draw counts, memory, bodies, voices, Luau heap |
| `AutomationPanel` | connected clients, live request log with timings, pause-agent toggle, deny-mutations switch, "Allow AI automation" preference (lets agents attach to this editor, §13.8), external source editor preference (executable plus one argument per line, `{path}` and `{line}` substituted without a shell; written to `user://Editor.json` at the next safe point) |
| `UndoHistoryPanel` | command labels, `[agent]` prefix for automation-originated commands |
| `ProjectLauncher` | recent projects (the host's memory snapshot of `user://Editor.json`, read at startup and after each project open or close, never while drawing), New from template (Empty, Basic3D), Open; ImGui folder picker (no per-OS dialog code, automatable) |

### 12.3 Commands and undo
```cpp
class Command
{
public:
	virtual ~Command() = default;
	Command(const Command&) = delete;
	[[nodiscard]] virtual Status Execute(EditorContext& context) = 0;   // atomic: fully applies or leaves state unchanged
	[[nodiscard]] virtual Status Undo(EditorContext& context) = 0;      // atomic: restores the exact prior state, or fails (file I/O) still applied
	[[nodiscard]] virtual std::string_view GetLabel() const = 0;
	[[nodiscard]] virtual std::string_view GetMergeKey() const { return {}; }   // equal non-empty keys and origins may merge
	[[nodiscard]] virtual bool MergeWith(const Command& next) { return false; }  // gizmo drags, slider scrubs
	[[nodiscard]] virtual bool ChangesScene() const { return true; }            // false: settings commands, no dirty scene
	[[nodiscard]] virtual size_t GetMemorySize() const = 0;                     // the history's 256 MB bound
	[[nodiscard]] virtual Status ReplayOnSceneCopy(Scene& scene, bool after) const;  // scene.diff against a revision
	CommandOrigin GetOrigin() const; void SetOrigin(CommandOrigin origin);     // User | Agent; set by EditorContext::Execute
private:
	CommandOrigin m_Origin = CommandOrigin::User;
};
```
Almost every operation uses one generic implementation, **`SceneEditCommand`** (snapshot undo): `SceneEdit edit(context, "Reparent")` begins tracking, the operation mutates the scene through the normal `Scene`/`Entity` APIs, and `edit.Commit()` builds the command from the scene's change tracker: for each touched entity, the canonical JSON before and after (absent = created or destroyed) plus parent and sibling index. Undo and redo restore through the heavily tested serializer. Debug builds verify after each commit that the canonical JSON of every untouched entity, the state hash's input, is unchanged (ADR 0008 decision 34). Other command types: `AssetEditCommand` (file bytes before/after, for materials, sound effects, scripts written through automation and import settings), `ProjectSettingsCommand` (settings JSON before/after), `AssetMoveCommand`, `AssetDeleteCommand` (moves file + `.meta` to `Library/Trash/`; never a permanent delete) and `CompositeCommand` (transactions and `edit.batch`; atomic: if a child fails, executed children are undone in reverse).

Continuous edits begin on `IsItemActivated` or gizmo mouse-down and commit on release (one undo step). `CommandHistory` is bounded at 1,000 entries or 256 MB (configurable through `EditorContextSpecification::HistoryLimits`, for the property test), keeps a save point for the dirty flag, and records `EditorContext::GetRevision()`, the editor's never-repeating revision, before and after each command's first execution; undo and redo never change those values, and a merge keeps the first one (ADR 0008 decision 28). There is one history per open scene. An `EditorTransaction` collects the commands executed inside it into one `CompositeCommand`, and a nested transaction joins the outermost one (ADR 0008 decisions 13, 18 and 29). Property tests: for random sequences of 10,000 operations over random scenes, undo-all restores byte-identical JSON and redo-all reproduces the final state; per command type, Execute → Undo equals the original and Execute → Undo → Redo equals Execute.

### 12.4 Play mode, assets, projects
- Toolbar: Play, Simulate, Pause, Step, Stop; lockstep indicator. Behaviour per §5.6. Play is blocked only by Error-severity asset diagnostics (and type errors when configured).
- Ordinary Play consumes `Application.Quit` at the editor safe point through the normal Stop path. Test, replay, recording and lockstep drivers retain control of their sessions. Script environment queries use the live borrowed window, including its headless state, except the window size, which is the session's view (view coordinates, §4.3: the Game image for ordinary play, the project's window size for test, replay, recording and lockstep sessions); cursor changes use that window and restore the editor baseline on Stop or replacement. Focus loss releases capture. Native input and viewport focus-loss input changes respect exclusive test/replay ownership, while authored test input remains effective (ADR 0019 decision 10).
- Autosave and recovery per §4.13.
- New-project templates write the folder skeleton `Assets/{Scenes,Scripts,Prefabs,Materials,Models,Textures,Audio,Fonts,Tests}`, `.luaurc`, `.gitignore` (`Library/`), an `AGENTS.md` stub and, for Basic3D, a scene with camera, sun, environment, post-process and ground. Script templates: `Behaviour` (the class pattern of §11.2), `Module` (a plain table module), `Test` (a suite, §11.10); each is type-checked by a fixture.
- `SceneChangedOnDisk` (§7.5) shows a banner offering reload; it never reloads silently.

### 12.5 Editor design quality

The editor's functional gates and its product design are separate acceptance obligations (ADR 0020). Editor changes must be reviewed in actual rendered, populated screens and exercised through the corresponding editing workflow. Screenshot comparisons guard a reviewed design against regressions; an unchanged screenshot alone is not a design approval.

- Use the committed Inter font, shared `Editor/Ui` style and a restrained dark palette. Size controls from font/style metrics, keep keyboard focus visible and apply display scaling to both text and spacing without compounding it across monitor changes. Account for framebuffer scaling so Retina displays do not apply the monitor scale twice. Game input ownership suspends UI keyboard navigation until focus returns to the editor.
- Distinguish the menu, primary toolbar, panel tools and status. The project, current scene, unsaved state and play mode must be understandable without opening a diagnostic panel. Friendly panel titles are presentation names; automation names remain stable, and existing saved layouts are migrated when their ImGui IDs change.
- Inspector labels precede their controls in aligned rows. Ordinary transforms expose position, Euler rotation and scale; raw quaternion and derived values remain available under advanced disclosure. Show readable entity and asset names with paths/identifiers on demand. Removal and other occasional actions belong in component menus rather than competing with every editable field.
- Property editing never rounds a stored value to its display format. Drags and typed numbers stay inside the field's range and step over its excluded magnitudes, and a multi-edit changes only the vector components the user touched. Every rotation edits as Euler degrees, with the raw quaternion components renormalized after an edit. Runtime (Play) content carries the play accent. Which panels are open persists with the saved workspace layout, and the undo, redo and save shortcuts work from any panel.
- Browsing scenes, importing assets and opening projects must have discoverable selection flows. Import and project-opening pickers also accept typed paths. Empty, busy, failed, read-only and agent-controlled states explain what happened and what the user can do next.
- Review populated and empty screens at 1280×720 and 1600×900, and at a larger display scale. Required controls must remain reachable at narrow widths through wrapping or scrolling. Review selection, property editing and undo, asset browsing, scene opening and Play/Stop with the production command paths.

---

## 13. AI automation

### 13.1 Topology
```
Claude Code ──MCP (stdio)──▶ Tools/MCP bridge (Python, official mcp SDK) ──JSON-RPC 2.0 / TCP 127.0.0.1──▶ Editor AutomationServer
                              launch / attach / supervise / relaunch            I/O thread: framing, auth
                              catalog from the method registry                  main thread: Pump() → MethodRegistry
                              crash reports, output capping, transcript         → Commands / PlaySession / Renderer
```
The engine owns a stable, semver'd JSON-RPC protocol; MCP lives only in the thin Python bridge, so MCP revisions are absorbed by the pinned official SDK, the bridge survives editor crashes, and the same protocol serves Python tests, batch files and the Runtime.

### 13.2 Protocol, transport, security
- **Framing:** LSP-style `Content-Length: N\r\n\r\n<UTF-8 JSON>`; frames over 64 MB, invalid UTF-8 or nesting depth over 128 close the connection. A first line that is not a `Content-Length` header (e.g. `GET / HTTP/1.1`) closes the socket immediately, which defeats browser-based localhost attacks and DNS rebinding.
- **Transport:** TCP bound to `127.0.0.1` only, port 0 (OS-assigned) or `--automation=<port>`. The editor writes `<UserData>/<Product>/Automation/Sessions/<pid>.json` `{pid, port, token, protocolVersion: "1.0", engineVersion, projectPath, headless, startedAt}` with user-only permissions (0600 on POSIX; per-user `%LOCALAPPDATA%` ACL on Windows); deleted on clean exit; stale files (dead pid) are ignored.
- **Handshake:** the first request must be `session.hello {token, protocolVersion, client: {name, version}}`. The 256-bit token (OS CSPRNG) is compared in constant time; 3 failures close the connection; a major-version mismatch is rejected listing both versions.
- **Availability:** the server runs only with `--automation`, `--headless` or the editor preference "Allow AI automation". It does not exist in Dist builds.
- **Threading:** the I/O thread decodes frames into a queue; `AutomationServer::Pump` executes requests on the main thread between input and update, so handlers see a consistent frame and need no locks. Up to 4 clients; requests from one client run in order; mutations from all clients serialize through the single `CommandHistory`.
- **Disconnect.** When a client's connection closes, cleanly or not, the server cancels that client's pending operations (each resolves internally as `Cancelled`). If the client owned lockstep, it releases lockstep and pauses play, so the session never stays locked in "Agent controls time". It stops any recording that client started, marking the file invalid and not writing it, and logs one `AutomationClientDisconnected` event. Python tests cover a kill during `play.step`, during `input.replay` and while recording.
- **Pending operations:** handlers return `MethodResult = std::variant<nlohmann::json, Error, Scope<PendingOperation>>`; `PendingOperation::Poll(MethodContext&) -> std::optional<Result<nlohmann::json>>` runs once per frame until it resolves (`play.step`, `play.waitFor`, `asset.import`, `asset.reimport`, `project.export`, `test.run`, `input.record`, `input.replay`, `editor.screenshot`), with optional `$/progress` notifications.
- **Watchdog:** if the main thread has not pumped for 5 s, the I/O thread itself answers new requests with `Busy` (−32007) including the main thread's current phase from an atomic phase marker (`"Script:Board.OnFixedUpdate"`, `"AssetImport:Track.glb"`). An agent never hangs on a frozen editor. Play sessions report every protected gameplay script entry to their host, which sets the script phase `Script:<script file stem>.<callback>` (`Task` for a resumed task, `Test.Case` for a case body) over whatever request or pending operation is current, wherever the session is advanced from; one-shot evaluations keep the request's phase.
- **Paths:** every path argument resolves through `VfsPath` inside `project://`. Runtime recording is a narrow exception because its project mount is a read-only pak: relative `input.record` output paths are confined below `user://Replays/`, and Runtime `input.replay` also accepts the returned canonical path there. Absolute paths, traversal and other schemes remain rejected (ADR 0019 decision 7). `asset.import` may read an absolute source path (read-only). It copies the importable file and, for a `.gltf`, its **dependency closure**: every buffer and image URI, which must be relative and inside the source's directory tree (no `..`, no absolute paths, no schemes other than `data:`). Relative paths are preserved under `destDir`, and each copied dependency gets a dependency meta (§6.4). Nothing else is copied. `project.export` writes only under `<Project>/Build/` or `--export-root` given on the command line. No method executes arbitrary processes, shell commands or native code: the only processes a method starts are `project.export`'s smoke test of the exported game (`smokeTest`) and, on macOS, `codesign` in its Sign phase (§14.2), each by a fixed path with fixed arguments, never a command a request supplies. `script.eval` runs in the Luau sandbox.

### 13.3 Error codes
JSON-RPC standard codes plus: −32000 `Internal`, −32001 `NotFound`, −32002 `InvalidState`, −32003 `ValidationFailed`, −32004 `Conflict`, −32005 `Timeout`, −32006 `ScriptError`, −32007 `Busy`, −32008 `Unauthorized`, −32009 `Unsupported`, −32010 `Cancelled`. `error.data` carries `errorCode` (the engine `ErrorCode` name) and `issues[]`, each `{pointer, message, hint?, suggestions?}`:
```json
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":8,"error":{"code":-32602,"message":"Invalid params","data":{"errorCode":"Validation",
 "issues":[{"pointer":"/components/RigidBody/Mas","message":"unknown field 'Mas' on 'RigidBody'","hint":"did you mean 'Mass'?"},
           {"pointer":"/components/RigidBody/Friction","message":"must be >= 0 (got -0.2)"}]}}}
```

### 13.4 Conventions every method follows
- Names `domain.verb`; params and results are JSON objects with **camelCase** keys; embedded component/asset data keeps registry PascalCase. Enum values are parsed case-insensitively and echoed in canonical PascalCase (§6). Each method's params and result are **reflected C++ structs**, so parsing, defaults, validation and the JSON Schemas (2020-12, with `$defs` per component) are generic; there is no hand-written JSON plumbing. Every method has a description, `exposeAsTool`, `mutates`, `supportsDryRun`, `availableInRuntime`, `availableInLauncher` and `timeoutSeconds` metadata.
- **`EntityRef`** params (a method param that names an entity, such as `entity.update`'s `entity` or `entity.create`'s `parent`) accept a 16-hex UUID, a unique UUID prefix (≥ 6 hex digits) or a path (`/Game/Board`, `/Track/Piece[3]`); ambiguous paths fail and list candidates. Inside a batch a path also names an entity an earlier op created (`"parent": "/Game"`), and unlike a `$ref` index it does not shift when an op is inserted. An `EntityRef` **inside component data** (a component field, a script's `Field.Entity()` value reached through `Script.Fields`, an element of an array or map of them) takes the 16-hex id only, since such a value keeps the handle pattern (ADR 0010 decision 32): any other string is `InvalidArgument` at its pointer (`expected a 16-digit hex UUID string`), with the hint `an Entity value in component data takes the entity's 16-hex id; use {"$ref": "<op>.entity.id"} in a batch or the id entity.get returns` (ADR 0027 decision 3, A4; paths there are deferred, decision 12). **`AssetRef`** per §7.1, a path resolving inside component data too. Responses always include canonical ids plus readable names/paths.
- **Seed.** A play session started without `seed` (`play.start`, `input.record {action: "Start"}`), every test suite and every recording uses the **session seed**: the project's `Simulation.Seed` XOR the scene's `Seed` (an empty scene's is 0; §4.12); a replay restarts with its header's seed. So `play.start {lockstep: true}` without a seed shows the piece order and spawns that suites and recordings of the same saved scene see.
- **Target.** `target: "Edit" | "Play"`. Reads default to the play scene while playing. Mutations of the play scene must say `target: "Play"`; they are transient (no undo) and the response says so.
- **Undo.** An edit-scene mutation is exactly one `Command` per call (one `CompositeCommand` per `edit.batch`), labelled `[agent] …`; the result includes `undoIndex`.
- **`dryRun: true`** validates and returns the would-be result without applying it, and is accepted only by methods with `supportsDryRun`. Others return `Unsupported`; this includes `asset.import`, `project.export`, `play.*`, `input.*` and `test.run`. Scene mutations execute then undo inside a sandboxed history. **File-writing methods** (`asset.create`, `asset.setProperties`, `script.write`, `script.create`, `prefab.create`, `project.setSettings`) write into an `OverlayMount` (§4.10) layered over `project://` for the duration of the call: parsing, compiling, type-checking, registry updates and `$ref` results all happen in memory, and nothing touches the disk, the file watcher, the trash or provenance. `edit.batch {dryRun: true}` rejects a batch containing an op without `supportsDryRun` before running anything, reporting `failedOp: k` and `Unsupported`.
- **`ifRevision: N`**: optimistic concurrency; if the editor's revision (`_meta.revision`) moved since, the call fails with `Conflict` and the current revision. The revision counts changes to the open scene and the project settings only: creating or writing asset and script files leaves it unchanged, so `ifRevision` guards the scene and the settings, not asset files (ADR 0026, friction S1-14).
- **Atomic batch.** `edit.batch {label, ops: [{method, params}], dryRun?}` runs inside one `CompositeCommand`; if op *k* fails, ops 0..k−1 are undone and the error reports `failedOp: k`. `{"$ref": "<opIndex>.<jsonPath>"}` substitutes earlier results (e.g. `"3.entity.id"`); a path that names no member is `InvalidArgument` naming the object inspected (`no member 'ids' in '3.prefab' (in the result of op 3)`, or `in the result of op 3` for the result itself), with a hint that lists at most 32 of its members and how many more, after "did you mean" for close ones (ADR 0026, milestone review R27).
- **Diagnostics delta.** Every response carries `_meta: {revision, dirty, undoLabel?, tick?, playState, diagnostics: {newErrors, newWarnings, newScriptErrors, logCursor, firstNew: [≤ 3 entries]}}`, so an agent learns about problems without polling.
- **Bounded output.** Lists take `limit` (default 100, max 1,000) and `cursor`; any result over 48 KB (minified, `_meta` excluded) is written to `Library/Automation/Out/<pid>-<startSeconds>-<seq>.json` (`user://Automation/Out/` in the launcher state and for read-only editors) and returned as `{path, truncated: true, summary}`. The summary lists the result's members in order, each verbatim when its minified JSON is at most 512 bytes (numbers, booleans, paths, codes, counts and short name lists such as `test.run`'s `counts`, `failedCases` and `jsonPath`) and otherwise as its type with its length or count, a longer array whose elements are all strings adding `first`, its leading strings within 1,536 bytes (`MaxOffloadSummaryListBytes`), so an offloaded `test.run` still names its first failing cases (ADR 0026, milestone review R26); it stays below 4 KB (a `first` that would not fit is left out). Every file a server writes to that directory, offloaded results and output files (screenshots, test reports) alike, takes its name from one per-server sequence (`<seq>`, at least 8 digits, from 1), so no two share a name; a file is created exclusively, never replaced (`VirtualFileSystem::WriteNewFile`, §4.10: a taken name is `AlreadyExists`, also one another process took a moment before), so nothing there is overwritten and no `.bak` is made. A server's first write to the directory removes every file a server whose process no longer runs left there, whatever its extension, with the `.bak` and `.tmp-*` leftovers of older or interrupted writes; a server that shuts down removes exactly the offloaded results it wrote and keeps its output files, so a test run's reports and a `--batch` run's screenshots outlive it (ADR 0026, friction S1-02 and S1-03). Screenshots are downscaled to `maxDimension` (default 1024).
- **Provenance.** The editor records every file it writes under `Assets/`, the root `.eproj` and the root `.luaurc` in the project's **committed** `<Game>/Automation/Provenance.json` (`<Game>/` is the project directory: `Projects/<Game>/`, or `Tests/Projects/Breakout/` for the M16 dry run): `{"Format": "Provenance", "Version": 1, "Entries": [...]}`, one entry per path, `{Path, XXH64, Method, RequestId, Client, TranscriptLine}` with PascalCase keys like every canonical file (§6; ADR 0008 decision 10). `XXH64` is the seed-0 hash of the bytes written (16 hex digits), `RequestId` the JSON-RPC id as sent, `Client` the `session.hello` client name (or `batch`, `cli`, `test`, `ui`), and `TranscriptLine` the request's 1-based line in `BuildLog.jsonl` (§13.8), null for a request that carried none (a `--batch` run writes no transcript). Entries are kept sorted by path and written canonically. Writes by the windowed UI are recorded with `Method` and `Client` `"ui"`, and only they have a null `RequestId`; the audit rejects them for demo projects. A `cli` entry (`Editor --upgrade`, §12.1) carries its own request's id and transcript line. The `.meta` writes an import publishes after its request returned keep the attribution of the request that started the import (ADR 0026 decision 10). A move (`asset.move`) carries a file's entry to its new path, with the move's attribution, only when the old path had an entry whose `XXH64` equals the moved bytes; otherwise the destination gets no entry, so a file the editor did not write (no entry, or bytes changed since its entry) stays an audit finding after a move (ADR 0026, milestone review R02). Dry runs never record. `project.upgrade` records its rewrites with `Method` `"project.upgrade"` (§13.12).

### 13.5 Method list (86)
| Domain | Methods |
|---|---|
| session (3) | `session.hello`, `session.info` (versions, capabilities, project, play state with a fatal stop's `stopped`/`stopError` as `play.state` reports them, renderer, lockstep owner, read-only flag, and the open scene as `scene {path, name, revision, dirty, entityCount}`, null without one: the editor's edit scene, which neither `--project` nor `project.open` opens, or the Runtime's game scene; ADR 0026, friction S2-03), `session.shutdown {save?, force?}` |
| rpc (1) | `rpc.discover {method?, domain?}` (descriptions, schemas, flags, examples) |
| project (10) | `project.create {path, name, template}`, `project.open {path, recover?}`, `project.save` (the open scene if dirty, dirty native assets and settings), `project.info`, `project.getSettings`, `project.setSettings {patch}` (RFC 7386 merge patch, undoable), `project.refreshAssets`, `project.validate {scope?, fix?: true \| [diagnosticIds \| codes]}` (each diagnostic has a stable `id`; `fix: true` applies every safe fix), `project.upgrade {dryRun?}` (applies migrations and canonical re-saves to every project file, records each rewrite in provenance and the transcript, reports changed files and, in `changes`, each with its reason: `"format version <file> to <current>"`, `"component migration"` or `"canonical form"`; scenes and prefabs load strictly, so one that needs a repair fails the upgrade instead, and materials, sound effects and replays are read with unknown members refused, as their importers read them, so such a member fails the upgrade at its file and pointer instead of being dropped (ADR 0026, milestone review R16); it refuses an open scene with unsaved changes (`InvalidState`) and needs no scene open; a tool since ADR 0026 friction S3-09), `project.export {config, outDir?, smokeTest?, testing?}` |
| scene (8) | `scene.new {path, template?}`, `scene.open {path, save?, discardChanges?, reload?, repair?}` (with a dirty open scene exactly one of `save`/`discardChanges` is required, else `InvalidState`; `reload` adopts a `SceneChangedOnDisk` version; `repair` loads with structural repairs and reports them, §6), `scene.save {path?}`, `scene.tree {root?, depth?, format: text|json}`, `scene.query {where: {name?, tag?, component?, path?}, select?, limit?, cursor?}`, `scene.get {target?}` (full JSON, offloaded when large), `scene.diff {against: saved|revision, revision?}` (RFC 6902 per entity), `scene.raycast {origin, direction, maxDistance, layerMask?}` |
| entity (7) | `entity.create {name, parent?, index?, active?, tags?, components?}`, `entity.get {entity, components?: [...]|"all", children?}`, `entity.update {entity, name?, active?, tags?, components?: {Type: {Field: value}}, removeComponents?: []}` (adds missing components), `entity.destroy {entities}`, `entity.duplicate {entities}`, `entity.reparent {entity, parent, index?, keepWorld?}`, `entity.bounds {entities}` (world AABBs) |
| component (2) | `component.list`, `component.schema {name}` (fields, types, ranges, defaults, enum values, script methods, docs) |
| edit (6) | `edit.batch`, `edit.undo {steps?}`, `edit.redo {steps?}`, `edit.history {limit?}`, `edit.select {entities}`, `edit.getSelection` |
| asset (11) | `asset.list {dir?, type?, recursive?}` (`dir` `engine://` or a folder below it, such as `engine://Environments`, lists the built-in assets of §7.1 as project assets are listed (`id`, `path`, `type`), sorted by path; every other scheme is refused; ADR 0027 friction F02), `asset.info {asset}` (metadata, dependencies, dependents, diagnostics), `asset.import {source, destDir, settings?}`, `asset.reimport {asset}`, `asset.create {type: Material|Scene|Prefab|SoundEffect|Folder, path, values?}` (a sound effect needs at least one layer; invalid values of a material or a sound effect are `ValidationFailed` located under `/values`, here and in `asset.setProperties`; missing parent folders are created with the asset; a `Folder` whose path is already a directory succeeds as a no-op, recording no command, writing nothing and reporting `created: false`, while a file at the path stays `AlreadyExists`: ADR 0027 friction F07), `asset.getProperties {asset}`, `asset.setProperties {asset, values}` (native assets: materials, sound effects), `asset.getImportSettings`, `asset.setImportSettings`, `asset.move {asset, path}`, `asset.delete {asset}` (to trash, undoable; a folder below `Assets/` that holds no files, only empty folders, is removed with them as one undoable command, `removedFolders`, and one that holds files is `Validation` naming them: ADR 0026, friction S3-07; the folders are removed without recursion, so one that also holds an entry no `VfsPath` can spell, which no listing shows, is `InvalidState` and nothing is removed) |
| prefab (5) | `prefab.create {entity, path, replaceWithInstance?}`, `prefab.instantiate {prefab, parent?, index?, transform?, name?, components?}` (`components`: per-instance component values for the instance root, `{Type: {Field: value}}` merged as `entity.update` merges them, script fields as `{Script: {Fields: {...}}}` with the prefab root's script handle by default, recorded as the instance's overrides; the result names the new root `entity`, where `prefab.create` names its replacing instance `instance`), `prefab.apply {instance}`, `prefab.revert {instance, overrides?}`, `prefab.unpack {instance}` |
| script (7) | `script.create {path, template: Behaviour|Module|Test}`, `script.read {path}`, `script.write {path, source}` (undoable; triggers compile + check; diagnostics in the result), `script.check {paths?}`, `script.errors {since?, limit?}` (retained errors after the cursor, `nextCursor`, and `evicted` when an error after it was evicted, §11.1), `script.fields {script}`, `script.eval {code, context: edit|play, entity?}` (returns value + captured prints; edit context is read-only) |
| play (8) | `play.start {mode: play|simulate, lockstep?, seed?, scene?, parameters?, paused?, timeScale?, pauseOnError?}`, `play.stop`, `play.pause`, `play.resume`, `play.step {ticks, input?: [events], render?: every|last|none}` (result includes `tick` and `stateHash`), `play.state` (mode, tick, `stateHash`, lockstep owner, `modified`, recording, `stopped` and `stopError`: the structured error of a fatal stop, §11.1), `play.waitFor {until: luauExpr, timeoutTicks}`, `play.setTimeScale {scale}` |
| input (3) | `input.inject {events, releaseAll?}`, `input.record {action: start|stop, scene?, parameters?, seed?, restart?, path?, expect?}` (a pending operation with a 600-second timeout: `start` and an event-free `stop` resolve in their first poll, a `stop` with input events first replays the recording with its input and then runs the input check, both within the timeout; the result adds `checkpoints` and `inputCheckTick`, §13.6), `input.replay {path, verify?, strictHash?, withoutInput?}` (`strictHash` and `withoutInput` each require `verify`; result includes per-`Expect` outcomes, `finalTick`, `stateHash`, `stopReason`, `checkpointsChecked`, `checkpointFailed` and `withoutInput`) |
| viewport (5) | `viewport.screenshot {view: scene|game, width?, height?, camera?, debugView?, annotate?, maxDimension?, inline?}`, `viewport.camera {position?, target?, projection?, verticalFov?, orthographicSize?, nearClip?, farClip?}` (get/set editor camera), `viewport.frame {entities}`, `viewport.setOptions {grid?, gizmos?, colliders?, icons?, wireframe?, debugView?, gameResolution?}`, `viewport.pick {x, y, view}` |
| editor (2) | `editor.screenshot {maxDimension?}` (whole editor UI), `editor.state` (open panels, mode, selection, lockstep owner) |
| observe (6) | `log.read {cursor?, minLevel?, loggers?, contains?, limit?}`, `events.read {cursor?, types?}`, `stats.get`, `physics.bodyInfo {entity}` (`{entity, body, origin, type, isSensor, layer, colliders, position, rotation, linearVelocity, angularVelocity, sleeping, boundsMin, boundsMax, contacts: [{other, collider, otherCollider, isTrigger, sinceTick}], grounded, groundNormal, tick}`; reads the play session: InvalidState in Edit mode, NotFound at `/entity` for an entity without a body), `audio.stats` (device state and name, time source, sample rate, master and group volumes, voice count and capacity, stolen and pulled frames, and every voice with its handle, clip, owning entity, group, volume, pitch, loop, spatial, position, paused, streamed, priority, cursor and length; Unsupported when the host has no audio engine), `docs.get {topic?, section?, offset?, length?, toc?}` (topic list with each topic's size when omitted; topics: `skills/<name>`, `reference/<Name>` (the generated `Docs/Reference/*.md`), `reference/Schemas/<Name>` (the JSON schemas), `scripting/Engine.d.luau` (the declarations) and `templates/Scripts/<Behaviour|Module|Test>` (the script templates); `section` (or `"<topic>#<section>"`) selects a Markdown heading's subtree or a top-level declaration by heading text or anchor, in §13.10's lookup order; `offset`/`length` page any text in pages of at most 32 KB that end after a line break when one falls inside, with `nextOffset` and `totalBytes`; a text too large to return inline comes back as its first page with its section list, so every response stays inline; `toc: true` returns no text, only the section list with each section's offset and size, §13.10) |
| test (2) | `test.list` (suites with their cases, replays), `test.run {filter?, timeoutTicks?, record?, coverage?}` (results in the §11.10 schema with `counts` and `failedCases`, failures with locations, the coverage report (its fingerprint and gates always, its counters only with `coverage: true` or `Testing.CoverageGates`), the JSON and JUnit paths; `record: path` requires the filter to select exactly one suite and writes a replay of all input applied during it, §13.6, reported in the `recording` block, §11.10; runs play saved scene files, so once `Begin` has selected the suites and replays, and before any of them plays, it refuses with `InvalidState` (`'<scene path>' is open with unsaved changes, and the selected suite '<name>' plays its saved file`, or `... the selected replay '<path>' ...`, hint `scene.save first; test runs play the saved scene file`) while the open scene has unsaved changes and a selected suite is bound to it or a selected replay plays it, every earlier param, preflight and selection error winning; a suite or replay of another scene, an empty-scene suite and a clean open scene are unaffected (ADR 0027 decision 3, A2); a recording refused because a selected row did not pass names those rows, `recording requires passing selected cases and a valid starting scene; <n> selected row(s) did not pass: <suite>/<case> (<status>), ...` in report order (a replay's suite is `replay:<path>`; at most 10, then `, and <k> more`), with a hint to rename the recording suite when a replay row is among them, A3) |

Both screenshot methods return `{path, mimeType, width, height}`; `viewport.screenshot` adds `view` and, with `inline`, PNG base64 `data` when the PNG is at most 30 KB. Larger images report `inlineOmitted: true` so the result stays below the offload threshold. Output PNGs outlive the server, including a `--batch` run. Viewport captures use the requested camera, target scene, project rendering quality, debug view and strictly validated annotations. All M9 data views are supported. `editor.screenshot` is a pending operation: it requests and waits for a UI frame completed after admission, then reads back the whole UI with its embedded viewport textures. This includes earlier changes in the same automation pump. A minimized editor renders that fresh frame offscreen at the last usable extent, without acquiring a swapchain image, and without publishing the views' extents (they stay unavailable to `viewport.pick` until the window is restored). A frame whose viewport textures were withdrawn after it was built (the window was minimized in between) is never captured: the request asks for another frame. A frame request the host cannot schedule fails the request at once. Cancellation releases the request; failure to publish a fresh frame within the method's 60-second deadline returns Timeout. Both methods return Unsupported with `--renderer none` (ADR 0017 and ADR 0018).

**Runtime subset** (Runtime Debug/Release with `--automation`; handlers shared from `Engine/Automation/Methods`): `session.*`, `rpc.discover`, `scene.tree`, `scene.query`, `scene.get`, `scene.raycast`, `entity.get`, `entity.bounds`, `play.pause`, `play.resume`, `play.step`, `play.state`, `play.waitFor`, `play.setTimeScale`, `input.*`, `viewport.screenshot` (game view), `log.read`, `events.read`, `stats.get`, `physics.bodyInfo`, `audio.stats`, `script.errors`, `script.eval` (play context). Handlers reach the host's audio engine through `AutomationMethodContext::GetAudioEngine()` (the Runtime's comes from `RuntimeAutomationServerSpecification::Audio`; ADR 0015 decision 15).

### 13.6 Lockstep, input injection, replays, batches
- `play.start {lockstep: true, seed: 42}` decouples simulation from the wall clock (without `seed` the session runs with the session seed, as suites and recordings do, §13.4); ticks advance only through `play.step` (one tick = fixed step + frame phase). `play.step {ticks: 600, render: "Last"}` fast-forwards 10 s of game time and renders one frame. A `play.step` is a pending operation that runs as many ticks per editor frame as fit a **50 ms budget** (at least one), so the UI, the watchdog and other clients stay responsive during long fast-forwards. Its result carries the final `tick` and `stateHash`. The UI keeps drawing and shows "Agent controls time". Lockstep is owned by one client; others get `InvalidState`. A disconnect releases it (§13.2). Audio advances by simulation time while lockstep owns time (§10.1).
- Input events are stamped with a tick offset relative to the step start: `{"tick": 0, "type": "Action", "name": "MoveLeft", "state": "Tap"}` (`Tap` = down on that tick, up on the next, so both edges are observed). Types: `action {state | value}`, `key`, `mouseButton {position?}`, `mouseMove`, `mouseDelta`, `scroll`, `gamepadButton`, `gamepadAxis {value}`, `text`. Type and state names are case-insensitive; files use PascalCase (§6.7).
- `play.waitFor {until: "return Scene.FindByName('GameOver'):IsActive()", timeoutTicks: 3600}` evaluates the predicate in the play VM after every tick and returns `{satisfied, tick, value}`.
- **Recording** always starts at tick 0, so a replay can reproduce it. `input.record {action: "Start", scene?, parameters?, seed?}` is accepted in Edit mode: it starts a lockstep play session of the scene (default: the open scene) with the given load parameters and seed (default: the session seed, the project's `Simulation.Seed` XOR the scene's `Seed`, §13.4), and records from tick 0. The session plays the saved scene asset the replay header names, so the open scene must be saved: with unsaved changes `start` fails with `InvalidState` (save first). The parameters start the session in the header's stored form (§6.7). While playing, `start` requires `restart: true`, which restarts the session at tick 0 with the recorded seed; otherwise it fails with `InvalidState` ("recording must start at tick 0"). The recording's own owner may restart an active recording this way. The recorder captures **every event applied at step 1** of each tick (§5.7), whatever its source: `play.step` input, `input.inject`, `Test.Inject*` from test threads, and real devices in the game viewport. `input.record {action: "Stop", path, expect?}` writes the replay with its full header (§6.7), its checkpoints and `FinalStateHash`. A recording with input events is first **verified with its input** (ADR 0027 decision 3, A1): the play session restarts from the candidate's header, as for the input check, and plays it with its events (`verify` and `strictHash`, evaluating `stop`'s `expect`). Unless that playback reproduces every checkpoint and the final hash, satisfies every expectation, stops nowhere early (a quit or a fatal error) and raises no script error (as `test.run {record}`'s ordinary-play verification requires, except that it counts only the errors raised up to its final tick: the verification's session is not torn down, a refused one staying live, so an error raised only at teardown, in `OnDestroy`, is not counted, while `test.run`, whose verification and replay rows stop the scripts first, counts it; an accepted limit, ADR 0027 decision 12), `stop` refuses with `Validation` at `/path`: `refusing to write '<path>': replayed with its input, the recording does not reproduce itself: <reason>; nothing was written`, `<reason>` naming the first failure in tick order (a checkpoint's observed and recorded hashes, an expectation that is false or raised, the final hash, where playback stopped, or, only when the playback otherwise passed, the first of its script errors), with a hint naming the usual causes and the playback's outcome as error data `verification`; the recording is ended, and the session is the verification's, paused where it stopped. This catches what nothing detects when it happens: a `script.eval` or `play.waitFor` that writes script state (a `self` field, a module table) during the recording, an expectation that is false or raises, a script that reads the host, and any other nondeterminism. A failure to start or run the verification keeps its error's code, writes nothing and ends the recording. The candidate then gets the **input check** (the recording guard, M16 friction S2-01): the play session restarts for the candidate as for `input.replay` and plays it with none of its events (`ReplayPlaybackOptions::WithoutInput`, expectations compiled from their source); when that run passes the candidate's verification (every checkpoint, the final hash and every expectation), the recording's outcome does not depend on its input and `stop` refuses it with `Validation` naming the replay, a hint (end the recording while the played state is still in the scene) and the check's outcome as error data `inputCheck`, writing nothing. Because playback stops at the first checkpoint that differs, the check refuses only a recording whose input never shows in the world at a checkpoint, at `FinalTick` or in an expectation: one shorter than one checkpoint interval that ends idle, input that changes only script state, or input that acts only after the last checkpoint and is undone by the end. A longer recording that ends with a restart is accepted, its checkpoints having seen the play, but its `FinalStateHash` then proves only the restored state and the play after its last checkpoint is unchecked (M16 friction S3-01), so recordings end while the played state is in the scene. Either way the session is then the check's, paused where it ended (`inputCheckTick`). A recording without input events is a pure determinism replay: `stop` writes it as recorded, with neither playback, and the recorded session stays the play session (unlike `test.run {record}`, which verifies an event-free recording too; its expectations and any `script.eval` write during it are first checked by the `input.replay {verify: true, strictHash: true}` that follows every recording, an accepted limit, ADR 0027 decision 12). **One rule decides input independence everywhere** (the recording guards of `input.record` and `test.run {record}`, and the input check of the games and determinism suites): a replay is input-independent when its input-free run passes verification (every checkpoint, the final hash, every expectation), whatever script errors that run raised; a script error that only the input-free run raises does not let the recording through. A hot reload or a play-scene mutation during recording (an edit with target `Play` from automation, the inspector or a gizmo; a dry run excepted; a `script.eval`, `play.waitFor` or test case that calls a mutating engine API, such as a component write, `SetActive`, a session `Random` draw or `Scene.CreateEntity`, `Scene.Instantiate`, `Scene.Destroy` or `Scene.Load`, while `Scene.FindByName` and the other `Scene` queries only read) invalidates it: `stop` then fails with the reason, writes nothing and ends the recording, so `start` (with `restart: true`), `test.run` and `input.replay` are accepted again.
- **Replays** are checked with `input.replay {verify: true}`: a fresh session of the header's scene starts with its parameters and seed, events are applied by tick, every `Expect` must hold, and `strictHash: true` also compares every checkpoint as playback reaches it (the first that differs stops the replay there with `checkpointFailed`, the tick and both hashes) and `FinalStateHash`. `withoutInput: true` applies none of the events: the check that a replay depends on its input, which must fail for a replay with events (the games and determinism suites run it for every committed replay with events). Like `strictHash` it requires `verify` (`InvalidArgument` at `/withoutInput` otherwise, since an unverified run checks nothing and would always pass), so the input check is `input.replay {verify: true, strictHash: true, withoutInput: true}` (ADR 0026, milestone review R19). A checkpoint or final-hash failure of a playback with the recording's input carries the divergence hint (the world differs from the recording: a serialized component of the scene, the look's included, a script, an asset or the engine changed since it was recorded, or the play is not deterministic; re-record it with `test.run {record}`); a `withoutInput` playback carries none, since failing is its expected outcome. Gameplay that quits or stops fatally before `FinalTick` fails the replay with `stopReason`, the tick it reached and its later expectations unevaluated (`Validation` with the outcome in error data), never `Cancelled`. They double as regression tests (§15.7) and run in exported builds through the cooked replay player (§14.4).
- **Autopilot workflow** (documented in the `game-building` skill). Steering a physics ball blind with tick-stamped injections is impractical, so the agent writes a test-only **autopilot suite**, `Assets/Tests/Autopilot/Level1.test.luau`, bound to the level scene with `Modes: ["Editor"]`. Its single case reads the ball's state, follows an authored list of waypoint entities with a PD controller, and injects analog `MoveX`/`MoveZ` actions with `Test.InjectAction` every tick until the goal is reached. The case only reads the scene and injects input. It never mutates entities and never uses the session's `Random` stream, so the run is identical to a player pressing the same inputs. `test.run {filter: "Autopilot.Level1", record: "Assets/Tests/Replays/Level1.replay"}` records the run. The replay is then verified **without** the autopilot (`input.replay {verify: true}` and `Testing.Replays`), which proves that plain input reaches the goal. `Level2` and `Level3` suites set `Parameters` (e.g. the carried timer), which the replay header stores.
- **Recording publication** (ADR 0019 decision 6). `test.run {record}` verifies the candidate in fresh ordinary gameplay without the test driver, including every checkpoint and the original final hash, before writing; a candidate with input events then gets the input check in another fresh session (the rule above), and one whose outcome does not depend on its input fails the run with `Validation` (its context names the replay; the case rows and reports are kept). It preserves all input from case-isolated segments with cumulative tick offsets, without encoding resets. A non-reproducible start, clock grouping or reset returns located Validation with no recording file; ordinary test runs remain supported. Verification is cancellable and counts toward the explicit run-wide tick limit, not case ticks or coverage.
- **Recording suites.** Every replay of a demo is recorded by a **recording suite**: a `Modes: ["Editor"]` suite with a single case, either an autopilot as above or a case that injects a planned input list at fixed ticks with `Test.InjectAction` (a Tetris opening), with `Test.Expect` on what only the input causes; its name occurs in no replay path (`Record.Opening`, not `Opening`), so `test.run {filter: "<its exact name>", record}` selects it and no committed replay (a selected replay row that fails refuses the recording and is named, §13.5). Re-recording is then one call, and the input plan stays readable through `script.read`. No method reads a replay's events or the transcript back (deferred, ADR 0027 decision 12), so a replay made with `input.record` is re-recorded only by planning its input again.
- **Re-recording.** When an engine change legitimately alters simulation results (a physics or gameplay fix), the agent re-runs the recording suites with `test.run {record}` through automation, so provenance and the transcript record the new replays. The change's review must state why the hashes changed. After a gameplay change a replay is re-recorded when it must cover the new behaviour: a matching hash means only that the worlds matched at each checkpoint and at the end (§5.1).
- Batch files per §6.7.

### 13.7 Observability
- **Screenshots** (§8.13): fresh frame after `WaitIdle`; `annotate: {labels: "all"|"selection"|[ids], colliders, bounds, axes}` draws entity names and short ids so a vision model can tie pixels to entities. The MCP tool returns an image content block plus the saved path.
- **`scene.tree {format: "Text"}`**, the token-efficient outline:
```
Main.scene  rev 58  dirty  14 entities
├─ Camera      7c1f09a2  Transform Camera(Ortho 11.5)            (7.5, 10, 20)
├─ Sun         09bb1e2f  Transform DirectionalLight              (0, 10, 0)
└─ Game        8f3a2c1d  Transform Script(Scripts/Game.luau)     (0, 0, 0)
   ├─ ScoreText 1d2e3f4a Transform Text("Score 0")
   └─ … 3 children (depth limit)
```
- **Logs and events** are structured with source locations and entity context, read by cursor so nothing is lost between polls. **Script errors** are deduplicated with tracebacks and pause play on the first error; the agent then inspects with `script.eval {context: "Play", entity: "/Game", code: "return self:BoardToAscii()"}`.
- **`scene.diff {against: "Saved"}`** gives the agent a precise self-review. `entity.bounds` and `physics.bodyInfo` support layout and physics reasoning (gaps between track pieces); in scripts and test suites the same questions are answered by `Entity:GetWorldBounds()`, `Physics.GetColliderBounds()` and `Physics.Raycast` (§11.5).
- **`project.validate`** returns `{id, severity, code, message, entity?, component?, field?, asset?, file?, line?, hint, autoFixable}`. `fix: true` applies every safe fix, and `fix: [ids or codes]` applies only the selected ones (the editor's per-item buttons use the same path); either way the fixes form one undoable command. Codes (generated into `Docs/Reference/Validation.md`): `SCENE_NO_PRIMARY_CAMERA`, `SCENE_MULTIPLE_PRIMARY_CAMERAS`, `SCENE_DUPLICATE_UNIQUE_COMPONENT`, `SCENE_NONCANONICAL_ORDER`, `SCENE_INVALID_HIERARCHY` (dangling or cyclic parent), `SCENE_INCONSISTENT_PREFAB_LINK`, `ENTITY_DUPLICATE_ID`, `ENTITY_DANGLING_REFERENCE`, `COMPONENT_FIELD_OUT_OF_RANGE`, `COMPONENT_MISSING_REQUIREMENT`, `COMPONENT_CONFLICT`, `ASSET_MISSING`, `ASSET_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `ASSET_IMPORT_FAILED`, `ASSET_ORPHAN_META`, `ASSET_DUPLICATE_HANDLE`, `ASSET_ORPHAN_DEPENDENCY`, `ASSET_TANGENTS_APPROXIMATED`, `ASSET_UNSUPPORTED_UV_SET`, `ASSET_VERTEX_COLORS_IGNORED`, `ASSET_CONTENT_SKIPPED`, `PATH_CASE_MISMATCH`, `PREFAB_MISSING_ASSET`, `PHYSICS_NONCONVEX_DYNAMIC`, `PHYSICS_MIXED_TRIGGER`, `PHYSICS_DYNAMIC_TRIGGER`, `PHYSICS_ALL_DOFS_LOCKED`, `PHYSICS_INVALID_SHAPE`, `PHYSICS_ADJACENT_STATIC_BODIES`, `PHYSICS_UNKNOWN_LAYER`, `PHYSICS_NONUNIFORM_SCALE`, `PHYSICS_DYNAMIC_UNDER_MOVING_PARENT`, `PHYSICS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `SCRIPT_COMPILE_ERROR`, `SCRIPT_TYPE_ERROR`, `SCRIPT_NOT_A_BEHAVIOUR`, `SCRIPT_UNKNOWN_FIELD_OVERRIDE`, `SCRIPT_FIELD_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `INPUT_UNKNOWN_ACTION` (string literals passed to `Input.*Action*`/`GetAxis`, found by AST scan), `AUDIO_NO_LISTENER`, `AUDIO_MULTIPLE_PRIMARY_LISTENERS`, `RENDER_NO_LIGHTING`, `RENDER_LIGHT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` (more than 256 visible lights), `RENDER_SPOT_SHADOW_BUDGET` (more than 8 shadowed spot lights), `BUILD_START_SCENE_MISSING`, `BUILD_SCENE_MISSING`, `TEST_SUITE_INVALID` (a `Testing.Suites` entry whose script is not a TestSuite or whose scene is not a registered Scene asset; ADR 0024 decision 6). Severities where the list leaves them open: the physics codes as ADR 0014 decision 9 gives them (`PHYSICS_ADJACENT_STATIC_BODIES`, a warning fixable by a Static `RigidBody` on the nearest common ancestor unless it is missing or has trigger colliders of its own; `PHYSICS_NONUNIFORM_SCALE` and `PHYSICS_DYNAMIC_UNDER_MOVING_PARENT`, warnings; `PHYSICS_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, a warning past 90 % of a limit and an error at it; the others errors whose body is not created, except `PHYSICS_UNKNOWN_LAYER`, whose body goes to layer 0); `AUDIO_MULTIPLE_PRIMARY_LISTENERS`, a warning fixable in the open scene by clearing `Primary` on each extra listener, and `AUDIO_NO_LISTENER`, an unfixable warning (ADR 0015 decision 12); `RENDER_LIGHT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, an unfixable warning on the scene file that counts its effectively enabled lights (ADR 0013 decision 7).
- **`stats.get`**: measured FPS, CPU frame and per-pass CPU/GPU timings, dropped simulation time, entity/body/voice counts and native Vulkan allocation counts. Scene and game views have independent histories; transient screenshot captures never replace them. The views are the editor's displayed viewport panels: a panel that is not displayed is not rendered and its view reports unavailable, and a headless editor displays the Scene view only, so its game view is unavailable (ADR 0027 friction F08). Each view also reports the quality settings its recorded frame rendered with (`shadowMapSize`, `ssaoHalfResolution`: the project's `Rendering` settings, also in an exported Runtime). Exact decimal frame strings distinguish current CPU work from delayed GPU samples, with explicit availability flags when no sample exists. Reading stats never waits for the GPU or advances simulation. Luau heap usage and soft/hard limits come from the live session's allocator; they are explicitly unavailable without a live VM (ADR 0019 decision 9).
- **State hash** is reported by `play.state`, `play.step` and `input.replay` (and `Test.GetStateHash()` in scripts, which hashes the tick too, so two reads at different ticks always differ), so acceptance checks such as "repeated runs give identical hashes" need no evaluation trick: two `input.replay {verify: true, strictHash: true}` runs of one replay report their `stateHash`. It covers the simulated world, never script tables or audio (§5.1).

### 13.8 MCP bridge (`Tools/MCP`)
- Python 3.10+, the **official `mcp` Python SDK** (low-level server API, dynamic tool registration), hash-pinned in `requirements.lock`, installed into `Tools/MCP/.venv` by `Setup.py`. Registered for Claude Code in `.mcp.json`, which `Setup.py` **generates** (gitignored) with the absolute path of the venv interpreter: `{"mcpServers": {"engine": {"type": "stdio", "command": "<repo>/Tools/MCP/.venv/Scripts/python.exe", "args": ["<repo>/Tools/MCP/run.py"]}}}` (`.venv/bin/python` on POSIX). No `python` on `PATH` is assumed, which matters on Windows, where only the `py` launcher may exist. `run.py` also works when started by any other interpreter (`py -3 Tools/MCP/run.py`, `sys.executable`): it locates the venv and re-executes itself inside it, or fails with "run python Scripts/Setup.py".
- Package: `server.py` (tool registration), `connection.py` (framing, handshake, reconnect; reuses `Tools/Automation/engine_client.py`), `launcher.py` (finds `bin/<cfg>-<sys>-<arch>/Editor/Editor(.exe)`, preferring Release; spawns a launcher-state editor with `--automation` and creates the project through `project.create` or opens an existing one through `project.open {path, recover}`, never with `--project`, so every write of the creation or the open, such as the `.meta` files the open's scan writes for new sources, is attributed to a transcribed request (§13.12; ADR 0026 decision 11); a missing binary returns an error naming `python Scripts/Build.py --config Release --project Editor`), `transcript.py`, `catalog.json` (generated by `--dump-reference`; tools are listed before the editor runs; `GenerateDocs.py --check` keeps it current).
- **Launch or attach.** Before spawning, `editor_launch` scans `Sessions/*.json` (§13.2) for a live editor whose `projectPath` matches. If one exists and its "Allow AI automation" preference is on (so its server is listening), the bridge **attaches** to it instead of spawning; two processes never write the same project. If there is no matching session file but `Library/Editor.lock` is held (an editor without automation enabled), `editor_launch` fails with `EditorAlreadyOpen {pid}` (pid read from the lock file) and the hint "enable Allow AI automation in the editor's Automation panel, then call editor_attach". The `Library/Editor.lock` check (§4.13) is the backstop for any other launcher. `editor_attach {project? | pid?}` attaches explicitly. Every attach, `editor_launch`'s included, first sends a transcribed `session.info`, so the attach leaves a request line whose response summary names the editor's pid, project and renderer; the tool's result reports it as `session`, with the open scene (or how to open one, since an attached or launched editor may have none open), and a refused `session.info` leaves the editor attached (ADR 0026, frictions S2-10 and S2-03). A launch sends the same transcribed `session.info` right after its `project.create` or `project.open`, so `editor_launch` reports the session and its open scene too (none: opening a project opens no scene; ADR 0026, friction S3-06). A spawned editor whose `project.create`, `project.open` or `session.info` raises (a timeout, a broken connection, a protocol error) is killed before the error returns, since the bridge keeps no connection to it (milestone review R30). A bridge never supervises or kills an editor it attached to; `editor_shutdown` on an attached editor only disconnects. Before it spawns an editor, `editor_launch` waits until no gate runs, and a rendering editor (`renderer: "vulkan"`) takes the machine-wide GPU lock, which the bridge holds until that editor exits (§2.3, machine-wide locks), unless the bridge runs under a process that already holds it (the automation suite); after 30 min of waiting the launch fails with `LockUnavailable`, naming the holder, and spawns nothing. An attach takes no lock.
- **Compact tool schemas.** MCP `inputSchema`s stay small: component and asset values are free-form objects (`{"type": "object", "description": "Field values by registry name; see component_schema"}`) instead of inlined `$defs` for every component, so the tool list does not flood the agent's context. Full schemas remain available through `rpc.discover` and `component_schema`, and the editor validates every call against them anyway.
- **Tools.** Bridge-local: `editor_launch {project, create?, template?, headless?, renderer?, recover?}` (`template`: `project.create`'s, read from the catalogue), `editor_attach {project?, pid?}`, `editor_status`, `editor_shutdown`, `engine_methods {domain?}`, `engine_call {method, params}` (escape hatch to all 86 methods), `result_read {path, pointer?, offset?, length?}` (reads a file the editor wrote to an offload folder, the connected project's `Library/Automation/Out/` or the user-data `Automation/Out/`, and nothing else: the location is checked as text before anything touches the file system, a `..` part refused and the normalized parent required to be one of the two folders, so a path outside them, a UNC path included, is refused alike whether or not it exists and opens no connection; then the file's resolved location, symbolic links included, must still lie directly inside a folder (milestone review R33, R35): an offloaded result or a test report, one page of at most 32 KB of its text with `nextOffset`, or the JSON value at `pointer`; not transcribed, since it reads nothing of the editor's; ADR 0026, friction S1-01). A relative project path given to `editor_launch`, `editor_attach`, `project.create` or `project.open` names a directory below the repository root, never the bridge's working directory (`"Tests/Projects/Breakout"`; friction S1-16). Proxied (methods flagged `exposeAsTool`, named `domain_verb`): `project_create`, `project_open`, `project_save`, `project_get_settings`, `project_set_settings`, `project_validate`, `project_export`, `scene_new`, `scene_open`, `scene_save`, `scene_tree`, `scene_query`, `scene_diff`, `entity_create`, `entity_get`, `entity_update`, `entity_destroy`, `entity_duplicate`, `entity_reparent`, `entity_bounds`, `component_list`, `component_schema`, `edit_batch`, `edit_undo`, `edit_redo`, `asset_list`, `asset_import`, `asset_create`, `asset_get_properties`, `asset_set_properties`, `asset_move`, `asset_delete`, `prefab_create`, `prefab_instantiate`, `prefab_apply`, `script_create`, `script_read`, `script_write`, `script_check`, `script_errors`, `script_eval`, `play_start`, `play_stop`, `play_step`, `play_wait_for`, `input_inject`, `input_record`, `input_replay`, `viewport_screenshot`, `editor_screenshot`, `log_read`, `test_run`, `docs_get`, `project_upgrade` (54 proxied + 7 local; `project_upgrade` since ADR 0026 friction S3-09, `asset_get_properties` since ADR 0027 friction F11). A proxied tool's arguments are checked against the current catalogue before they are forwarded: a client whose tool list predates it sends an argument its cached schema lacks as a string, so a string whose parameter the catalogue declares as a boolean, integer, number, object or array is converted when it is exactly a JSON literal of that type (`"true"`, `"0"`, `"{...}"`; object or array text holding a number that overflows a double stays a string, so no non-JSON `Infinity` reaches the editor or the transcript), and every other value goes to the editor unchanged for its validation to judge (ADR 0026, friction S3-02, milestone review R32). The bridge-local tools' arguments are converted against their own schemas the same way, and their boolean parameters (`create`, `headless`, `recover`, `save`, `force`) refuse anything but a JSON boolean with `InvalidParams`, so the string `"false"` never counts as true (milestone review R34).
- Results return as `structuredContent` plus a short text summary that always includes the `_meta` delta; text over 48 KB is truncated with a pointer to the offloaded file, which `result_read` reads; screenshots return image content, read from the PNG the result names (or its inline `data`), and the summary names the file.
- **Tool list refresh** (ADR 0026, predicted friction P10). The server declares `tools.listChanged`. On a handshake-era connection (one opened with `initialize`) it sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` once after the client's `notifications/initialized`, so a client that kept the tool list of an earlier bridge process (restarted after a change to the bridge or its catalogue) fetches it again. `editor_launch` and `editor_attach` first reread `catalog.json` when its bytes changed since it was read (their SHA-256 decides, not the modification time, so a rewrite within the file system's timestamp resolution is not missed and a rewrite with the same content changes nothing): a changed tool list, `editor_launch`'s templates included, is listed from then on, the result reports `toolListChanged: true`, and before returning the bridge sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` on the session of a handshake-era connection and publishes it to the `subscriptions/listen` streams of a 2026-07-28 one, the only place that protocol carries change notifications (the bridge serves `subscriptions/listen`, and its `tools/list` results are immediately stale, `ttlMs` 0). A launch or attach that fails reports `toolListChanged` the same way, and one that the client cancels or that raises still notifies; a change whose notification could not be sent goes out after the next call. A `catalog.json` that cannot be read or parsed keeps the listed tools; the result, a failed one too, reports `catalogError`, and the next launch or attach reads the file again. A change to the bridge's own Python takes effect only in a new bridge process, which the client starts when it reconnects (Claude Code: `/mcp`, Reconnect).
- **Resilience.** The bridge supervises the editor it launched. On unexpected exit the next call returns `EditorCrashed {exitCode, lastLogLines (50), crashReportPath, autosaveAvailable}`; `editor_launch` then offers recovery: an existing project always opens through `project.open {path, recover}` on a launcher-state editor, the result reports `recoveryAvailable` (a published autosave) and `recovered`, and only `recover: true` adopts the autosave (never when attaching to an editor that already has the project open). Calls time out after their method's `timeoutSeconds` (default 60): a proxied tool's from the catalogue, and `engine_call`'s as the connected editor declares it in `rpc.discover {domain}` (asked once per domain and connection, not transcribed), else the catalogue's, so a long `project.refreshAssets` or `test.run` through `engine_call` is not cut off at 60 s (ADR 0026 decision 13).
- **Transcript.** The bridge **always** appends every request and a response summary as JSONL to `<ProjectDir>/Automation/BuildLog.jsonl` of the project it launched or attached to, and passes each request's transcript line number to the editor (request field `_meta.transcriptLine`), which stores it in provenance (§13.4). The transcript is committed, so it names no machine path where it can avoid one: the `path` of a `project.create` or `project.open` inside the repository is recorded relative to the repository root (the editor receives the absolute path), and a response summary names an offloaded result's file relative to the project root, or by its file name when it lies outside the project (ADR 0026, friction S1-16, amending ADR 0008 decision 11's verbatim params). Request ids belong to one connection: each launch or attach connects anew and its ids start again after `session.hello`'s, so an id names a request only together with its line (provenance keys on `TranscriptLine`), and the first line of a connection marks where it begins: `project.create` or `project.open` for a launched editor (its `session.info` follows), the attach's `session.info` for an attached one (ADR 0026, friction S2-10). Ids can also skip where the bridge read `rpc.discover` for itself (`engine_call`'s timeout), which is not transcribed (friction S3-11). The lines of a `project.create` or `project.open` sent to an editor without a project are held until it answers: a success writes them; a refusal writes them when the target directory now holds a project file (the editor may have recorded files under that line before refusing) and drops them otherwise, so nothing is written to a directory that is not a project; a call the bridge stopped waiting for (a timeout, a broken connection) keeps them pending until the project file appears, which the bridge checks after the failure and before every later call, and a later project call for another directory drops them (milestone review R05, R31). Nothing depends on remembering an environment variable; `ENGINE_MCP_TRANSCRIPT=<path>` only redirects the file (Python tests use it to keep fixtures clean).

### 13.9 Headless and batch modes
`Editor --headless` initializes GLFW on the null platform and renders offscreen with the same renderer and the full editor UI (so `editor.screenshot` works); `--renderer none` serves logic-only CI and makes screenshot methods return `Unsupported`. GPU-dependent imports return `Unsupported` there unless a cooked bake exists (§7.4). `--batch f.jsonl` replays RPC calls and exits non-zero at the first error. The non-Dist engine option `--audio-device system|null|none` chooses the audio device and keeps the window mode's decoding; windowed Editor and Runtime processes that tests start pass `--audio-device none` (C++ through `Test::WithoutAudioDevice`), so no test opens a real audio device (§15.1; ADR 0015 decision 23). All headless runs use `ManualClock`, and headless play without lockstep is throttled to `FixedHz` (§4.2). `Runtime [--headless] [--frames N] [--expect-no-errors] [--screenshot-at tick:path] [--feature-test [--filter F]] [--automation[=port] [--paused]] [--replay R --verify] [--manifest P] [--gpu-validation]`; `--replay R --verify` checks the replay's expectations, every state-hash checkpoint and its final state hash, as `input.replay {verify: true, strictHash: true}` does (§6.7). `--manifest` (a `Game.json` elsewhere than beside the executable, which the C++ tests use) and `--paused` (the session starts paused at tick 0, with `--automation`) are non-Dist (ADR 0012 decision 10). Dist honours only `--headless`, `--frames`, `--expect-no-errors`, `--feature-test`, `--filter` and `--log-level`. `--log-level <trace|info|warn|error|critical>` sets the minimum level of the output sinks (console and log file) only, so `--expect-no-errors`, `log.read`'s ring buffer and expected-log capture still see every entry; in Dist, where Trace is compiled out, `trace` acts as `info` (ADR 0024 decision 17). Dist has no standalone `--replay`, but `--feature-test` runs every suite **and every cooked replay** listed in the manifest's testing data through the same runner (§11.10).

### 13.10 Agent-facing documentation and skills
- `AGENTS.md`: build/test commands, module rules, error policy, style, the commit gate and review checklist. `CLAUDE.md` contains `@AGENTS.md`.
- Skills (`.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` with `name`/`description` frontmatter): **build-and-test**, **add-component**, **add-script-api**, **add-automation-method** (each lists files to touch, generated artifacts to refresh and the FeatureTest coverage to add), **golden-images** (update policy and review), **commit-review** (§15.9; not named `code-review`, which would collide with the generic skill Claude Code ships), **game-building** (the end-to-end MCP workflow and definition of done; `Testing.Suites` setup; the recording suites (autopilots and planned input) and replay re-recording of §13.6; the look, the soak rules and the build settings a demo needs; the shared static body per level for tracks), **luau-gameplay** (idioms: the `Script.Define(name, class)` pattern, the `GetScript() :: Other.Type?` cast, fields, tasks, state machines, grid logic, DAS/ARR input, follow cameras on `RenderPosition` in `OnLateUpdate`, pooling with `SetActive`, `Transform:Teleport` for respawns; pitfalls: module tops without engine side effects (what the load-time VM offers), `Task.Wait` only inside `Task.Spawn`/`Task.Delay`/`Test.Case`, phase-aware input, strict-mode `self` typing, nil-guarding optional fields, `math.pow` instead of `^` for non-trivial exponents), **game-debugging** (the observability loop and an error-code → fix table).
- Generated reference in `Docs/Reference/` (with its JSON schemas), the script declarations `Resources/Scripting/Engine.d.luau` and the script templates, all served by `docs.get` (§13.5), so an agent with only the automation tools reads every document the builder skills name; the unit case "ObserveMethods: every document and topic the game-building skills name is one docs.get serves" keeps the `game-building`, `luau-gameplay` and `game-debugging` skills to served topics.
- **Sections of a long topic** (`docs.get`, ADR 0026, frictions S1-01 and S2-05). A Markdown topic's sections are its headings outside code fences, each with its text up to the next heading of the same or a higher level; a `.d.luau` topic's are its top-level declarations. Each has an anchor: the heading's text lowercased, spaces turned into `-`, every other ASCII character but letters, digits, `-` and `_` removed, and `-1`, `-2`, ... added to a repeated anchor. `section` names the first match in this order: the heading whose text is exactly `section` (case and punctuation kept, so `Test.Suite` and `TestSuite` differ); the heading whose text is `section` followed by a parenthesized qualifier (`TestSuite` finds `TestSuite (type)`); the section whose anchor is `section` after the same conversion (`testsuite`, `input-1`). A text that several headings share names none of them and is refused with `InvalidArgument` listing their anchors. The generated `ScriptAPI.md` adds the kind to a heading whose anchor would repeat another's (`Script (component)`, `TestSuite (type)`, `TextAlignment (enum)`, `TextSpace (enum)`), so every section of it is reachable by its heading text.
