In a `.d.luau` topic, adjacent declarations of one name form one section, and a name declared again further on titles its extern type's section `<name> (type)` and its global's `<name>`, so `scripting/Engine.d.luau` has `Script (type)` (the component's type) and `Script` (the global) and each section is reachable by its title (milestone review R25). `{topic, section?, toc: true}` returns only the section list of the topic (or the subsections of `section`) with each section's offset and size and the text's `totalBytes`, down to the deepest heading level whose list fits in 40 KB (`DocsTocBytes`), so an agent finds the section it needs without reading a 32 KB first page; `toc` with `offset` or `length`, or without a topic, is `InvalidArgument`.

### 13.11 Walkthrough: an agent builds Tetris
1. **Launch.** `editor_launch {project: "Projects/Tetris", create: true, template: "Empty", renderer: "vulkan"}` (it attaches instead if the user already has that project open with automation allowed, §13.8; the bridge launches `--renderer none` by default, which answers the screenshot methods with `Unsupported` and leaves a Dist smoke test unrun, and steps 7, 8 and 10 need the device); `docs_get {topic: "skills/game-building"}`. From here on the bridge writes `Projects/Tetris/Automation/BuildLog.jsonl` and the editor writes `Automation/Provenance.json`.
2. **Settings.** `project_set_settings` merge patch: window "Tetris" 720×900, `FixedHz` 60, seed, `StartScene: "Assets/Scenes/Main.scene"`, `Export.BuildScenes: ["Assets/Scenes/Main.scene"]` (the games suite requires a set `StartScene` that a non-empty `BuildScenes` lists, §15.1 T5), actions `MoveLeft`, `MoveRight`, `SoftDrop`, `HardDrop`, `RotateCW`, `RotateCCW`, `Hold`, `Restart` (keyboard + gamepad), and `Testing.Suites: [{Script: "Assets/Tests/Board.test.luau"}, {Script: "Assets/Tests/Tetris.test.luau", Scene: "Assets/Scenes/Main.scene", Isolation: "Case"}, {Script: "Assets/Tests/Record/Opening.test.luau", Scene: "Assets/Scenes/Main.scene", Modes: ["Editor"]}, {Script: "Assets/Tests/Record/LineClear.test.luau", Scene: "Assets/Scenes/Main.scene", Modes: ["Editor"]}]` (the last two are step 9's recording suites), `Testing.Replays: ["Assets/Tests/Replays/*.replay"]`. `project_set_settings` accepts the patch whatever exists yet. `project_validate {}` reports the codes: `BUILD_START_SCENE_MISSING` and `BUILD_SCENE_MISSING` until step 3 creates the scene, and `TEST_SUITE_INVALID` for each suite until step 9 writes its script; `_meta.diagnostics` never lists them, since it only counts the warnings and errors logged since the client's previous call (§13.4).
3. **Content and scene.** `scene_new {path: "Assets/Scenes/Main.scene"}`. A first `edit_batch` labelled "Tetris assets" creates the materials and sounds, which later calls name by path, so inserting an op never shifts a `$ref` index (`dryRun: true` checks a batch first):
```json
{ "label": "Tetris assets", "ops": [
	{ "method": "asset.create", "params": { "type": "Material", "path": "Assets/Materials/I.material", "values": { "BaseColor": [0.1, 0.8, 0.9, 1], "Roughness": 0.35, "Emissive": [0.1, 0.8, 0.9], "EmissiveStrength": 4 } } },
	"… six more piece materials (O, T, S, Z, J, L), Wall, Backboard (dark and rough), Ghost (AlphaMode Blend, a BaseColor alpha below 1) …",
	{ "method": "asset.create", "params": { "type": "SoundEffect", "path": "Assets/Audio/Lock.sfx", "values": { "Layers": [ { "Wave": "Square", "Notes": ["A3:0.05"] } ] } } },
	"… Move, Rotate, Hold, LineClear, LevelUp and a short GameOver sound …"
] }
```
Then one atomic `edit_batch` labelled "Scaffold Tetris". An entity param names an entity that an earlier op of the batch created by its path (`"parent": "/Game"`), and component data names an asset by its path; `{"$ref": "<op>.entity.id"}` stays for an `EntityRef` value inside component data, which takes the id only (§13.4):
```json
{ "label": "Scaffold Tetris", "ops": [
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "Camera", "components": { "Transform": { "Translation": [7.5, 10, 20] }, "Camera": { "Projection": "Orthographic", "OrthographicSize": 11.5, "Primary": true, "Clear": "Color", "ClearColor": [0.02, 0.02, 0.03] } } } },
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "Environment", "components": { "Environment": { "Environment": "engine://Environments/Studio", "Intensity": 0.6 }, "PostProcess": { "Tonemap": "ACES", "ExposureEV": -0.8, "BloomIntensity": 0.3 } } } },
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "Sun", "components": { "Transform": { "EulerAngles": [-50, -30, 0] }, "DirectionalLight": { "Intensity": 3, "LightAngle": 10, "ShadowDistance": 40 } } } },
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "Game" } },
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "WallLeft", "parent": "/Game", "components": { "Transform": { "Translation": [-1, 9.5, 0], "Scale": [1, 21, 1] }, "MeshRenderer": { "Mesh": "engine://Meshes/Cube", "Materials": ["Assets/Materials/Wall.material"] } } } },
	{ "method": "entity.create", "params": { "name": "Backboard", "parent": "/Game", "components": { "Transform": { "Translation": [4.5, 9.5, -1], "Scale": [12, 22, 0.2] }, "MeshRenderer": { "Mesh": "engine://Meshes/Cube", "Materials": ["Assets/Materials/Backboard.material"] } } } },
	"… WallRight, Floor, ScoreText / LevelText / LinesText / NextLabel / HoldLabel (Text, Screen), GameOverText (active false) …"
] }
```
The look needs these content choices: the camera looks straight at the well, so a cell's shadow and most of its ambient occlusion fall behind it, where only the backboard receives them; PCSS penumbrae grow with `LightAngle` (the default 1° gives near-hard edges, §8.7), and a `ShadowDistance` of about twice the camera's distance to the board (40 here, the default is 100) spreads the cascades (up to four) over less depth; bloom has no threshold (it blends `BloomIntensity` of a blurred copy into the image), so a visible glow needs HDR emission and a `BloomIntensity` well above the default 0.04: `EmissiveStrength` 4 with 0.3 under ACES at `ExposureEV` -0.8 is the pair Tetris tested (emission alone pales the pieces towards white before they glow, and a high intensity also lifts the shadows); `PostProcess` keeps GTAO, bloom and FXAA on by default; an orthographic camera's skybox is one flat colour, hence `Clear: "Color"`. The camera stands right of the well so that the HUD texts and the next and hold previews fit beside it at 720 × 900; step 7 iterates the framing on screenshots.
4. **Prefab.** `entity_create` "Cell" (cube mesh, scale 0.95, no `RigidBody` and no collider), `prefab_create {entity: "/Cell", path: "Assets/Prefabs/Cell.prefab"}`, `entity_destroy {entities: ["/Cell"]}`.
5. **Logic.** `script_write` for `Assets/Scripts/Pieces.luau` (Module: tetromino tables, SRS kick data, frozen with `table.freeze`), `Assets/Scripts/Board.luau` (Module: a pure 10×22 grid with no engine calls holding the rules: collision, SRS rotation and kicks, line clears, scoring, levels, the 7-bag drawn from a `RandomGenerator` it is given, hold once per piece, top-out and `ToAscii`, so that `Board.test.luau` covers them in the empty scene), `Assets/Scripts/Game.luau` (Behaviour requiring `Pieces` and `Board`, which the load-time VM resolves during import (§11.2)) and `Assets/Scripts/Hud.luau`. `Game.OnStart` creates the bag generator with `Random.New(Random.Integer(0, 2147483647))`: `Random` is unavailable at a module's top level, a generator's own state is outside the state hash, and that one draw from the hashed session stream makes the piece order follow the session seed (§13.4). Gravity is a frozen integer table of ticks per row for each level, counted by tick counters, never a curve written with `^`, whose computed exponent calls the C runtime's `pow` (§4.12). Lock delay, DAS 10 ticks and ARR 2 ticks on actions. The board is drawn by a pool of 220 well cells (10 × 22, the two hidden rows kept inactive) plus the preview cells, created once in `OnStart` with `Scene.Instantiate(self.CellPrefab, position, nil, self.Entity)` at fixed positions and touched (`SetActive`, `MeshRenderer:SetMaterial`) only where a cell changed: the cells and the HUD `Text` mirror the board, so the state hash and the replays see the play, and the soak's counters stay flat (§15.1 T6). The ghost piece is drawn on empty cells with the Ghost material, line clears play `Audio.PlayOneShot`, and game over waits for `Restart`, which restarts in place. Each `script_write` result carries compile, type and lint diagnostics; the agent iterates until `script_check {}` returns no diagnostic at all: its `passed` counts only errors, but the games suite fails a game on any diagnostic, lint warnings included.
```lua path=Assets/Scripts/Game.luau prelude=TetrisGame
function Game.OnFixedUpdate(self: Game, dt: number)
	if self.GameOver then
		if Input.IsActionPressed("Restart") then self:Reset() end
		return
	end
	self:HandleShift()
	if Input.IsActionPressed("RotateCW") then self:TryRotate(1) end
	if Input.IsActionPressed("HardDrop") then
		self:HardDrop()
		return
	end
	self.GravityTicks += if Input.IsActionDown("SoftDrop") then 20 else 1
	if self.GravityTicks >= Board.GravityTicksForLevel(self.Level) then
		self.GravityTicks = 0
		if not self:TryMove(0, -1) then self:BeginLockDelay() end
	end
end
```
6. **Wire-up.** `entity_update {entity: "/Game", components: {Script: {Script: "Assets/Scripts/Game.luau", Fields: {CellPrefab: "Assets/Prefabs/Cell.prefab", PieceMaterials: [...], ScoreText: "<the ScoreText entity's 16-hex id>", LockSound: "Assets/Audio/Lock.sfx"}}}}`. An `Entity` field takes the id that the scaffold batch returned (each op's `entity.id`) or that `entity_get {entity: "/Game/ScoreText"}` returns: an entity path there is refused with `InvalidParams` and a hint naming the id (§13.4), while asset paths resolve. An override that names no declared field, or has the wrong type or range, fails the call with `InvalidParams` at `/components/Script/Fields/<Name>`, so a call that succeeds matched every override.
7. **Look.** The cells exist only in play, so the look is checked in step 8's lockstep session once hard drops have stacked some cells: `viewport_screenshot {view: "Game", target: "Play", width: 720, height: 900}`, and again with `debugView: "AO"` and `debugView: "ShadowCascades"`, which show that GTAO and the shadow cascades reach the board. Without `width` and `height` a screenshot renders at 640 × 360 whatever the project window, which misframes the portrait game and scales the HUD text with the wrong height. `annotate: {labels: "all"}` ties pixels to entities; the camera is adjusted with `entity_update`. Enum values are written in their canonical spelling (`"Game"`, `"Play"`, `"Orthographic"`). The PNGs stay in `Library/Automation/Out/` only until the next editor session prunes them (§13.4).
8. **Verify deterministically.** `scene_save`, then `play_start {lockstep: true}` without a seed: the session seed, which every suite and every recording of the saved scene uses, so the piece order is theirs; `play_start` plays the open scene with its unsaved edits, while recordings and test runs play the saved file. `play_step {ticks: 1}` (`_meta.diagnostics` counts no new error); `script_eval {context: "Play", entity: "/Game", code: "return self.Board:ToAscii()"}` shows the spawned piece; `play_step {ticks: 3, input: [{tick: 0, type: "Action", name: "MoveLeft", state: "Tap"}]}` then eval: the piece column decreased by 1; `play_step {ticks: 120}` checks gravity against the level-1 interval; eval seeds a nearly full row and forces an I piece, `HardDrop` tap, `play_step {ticks: 2}`, eval asserts `Lines == 1` and `Score == 100` (such a script-table write is fine here, never during a recording, §13.6); step 7's screenshots; `play_stop`.
9. **Tests.** `script_create {template: "Test"}` + `script_write` for the suites (§11.10; the template names each suite after its file stem, `Board` for `Board.test.luau`): `Assets/Tests/Board.test.luau` (pure logic in the empty scene: kicks at both walls and the floor, clears of 1–4 lines and their scores, the 7-bag yielding each piece once per bag from a seeded `Random.New`, top-out ending the game, hold swapping once per piece) and `Assets/Tests/Tetris.test.luau` (bound to `Main.scene`: integration cases driven by `Test.InjectAction`, `Test.WaitTicks` and `Test.WaitUntil` that read the game through `GetScript()` and component values; hard drops keep a case within the 600-tick `CaseTimeoutTicks` default, and a gravity-paced case sets its own `TimeoutTicks`; `Test.GetStateHash` hashes the tick too, so it cannot compare two moments or two runs: runs are compared with `input_replay`). Then the two replays, each recorded from tick 0 by its Editor-only recording suite (§13.6), whose single case injects planned taps with `Test.InjectAction` at fixed ticks (the first well inside the first 60 ticks) and asserts with `Test.Expect` what only the input causes, and whose name occurs in no replay path (`Test.Suite("Record.Opening", ...)`): `scene_save` (`test_run` refuses a dirty open scene that a selected suite or replay plays, §13.5), `test_run {filter: "Record.Opening", record: "Assets/Tests/Replays/Opening.replay"}` (several pieces placed with moves, rotations, a hold, a soft drop and hard drops, ending with them in the well) and `test_run {filter: "Record.LineClear", record: "Assets/Tests/Replays/LineClear.replay"}` (a planned placement of the known pieces that clears at least one line, the HUD showing it), both ending with the played state on screen, never with a restart. Re-recording is the same call. Each replay is then verified twice with `input_replay {path, verify: true, strictHash: true}`, the two `stateHash` values equal, and once with `withoutInput: true`, which must fail. Finally `test_run {}` until green: it runs every suite and verifies every replay.
10. **Ship.** `scene_save`; `project_validate` must be clean, warnings included, and `script_check {}` must report zero diagnostics, lint diagnostics included; `project_export {config: "Dist", smokeTest: true}` (the exported executable runs `--headless --frames 300 --expect-no-errors` and must exit 0); the agent writes `Projects/Tetris/AGENTS.md` directly (documentation, outside the audit; no automation method writes it). The unit suite type-checks its `luau` fences against the documentation stubs unless a fence carries `skip="<reason>"`; it does not lint them (§11.9).

**Rolling Ball 3D** follows the same loop. Layers are `Ball`, `Track` and `Trigger`. Track pieces are built from scaled primitives with collider-only box colliders and saved as prefabs (`Straight`, `Ramp`, `Curve` segments, `Narrow`, `Gap`). The kinematic `MovingPlatform` prefab carries its own `Kinematic` `RigidBody` driven by `MoveKinematic`. Each level has a root entity `Level` with a **`Static` `RigidBody`**, so every track piece instantiated under it joins one static compound, and the ball rolls over seams without bumps (§5.3, §9.2; `project_validate` would report `PHYSICS_ADJACENT_STATIC_BODIES` otherwise). Each level is laid out as a single `edit_batch` of about 60 `prefab.instantiate` ops chained with `$ref`, plus an ordered list of `PathNode` waypoint entities along the main route and `Gap` markers where jumps are intended. `entity_bounds` gives the agent layout feedback. The ball prefab is a Dynamic sphere with `LinearCast`, `EnhancedInternalEdgeRemoval`, `MaxAngularVelocity` 120 and camera-relative torque. A follow camera in `OnLateUpdate` uses `Math.SmoothDamp` toward `ball.Transform.RenderPosition` (§5.2). Checkpoint and goal triggers sit under the level root as implicit sensor bodies. A kill plane respawns the ball at the last checkpoint with `RigidBody:Teleport` and a zero velocity. The game also has a HUD timer, synthesized sounds, and `Scene.Load(next, {Time = ...})` between three levels. Tests: `RollingBall.test.luau` (respawn at the last checkpoint, platform carry, timer carried across `Scene.Load`, goal loads the next level, and a **main-path support check** that samples the `PathNode` polyline every 0.25 m and requires a downward `Physics.Raycast` hit on the `Track` layer everywhere except inside `Gap` markers, reporting the first unsupported point). One autopilot suite per level records a replay with `test_run {record}` (§13.6). Each replay must then reach the goal under `input_replay {verify: true}` in lockstep without the autopilot.

### 13.12 Demo acceptance rule
For Tetris and Rolling Ball (and the Breakout dry run), every engine-consumed file under the game's project directory, `<Game>/` below (`Projects/Tetris/`, `Projects/RollingBall/`, and `Tests/Projects/Breakout/` for the dry run; the root `*.eproj` and `.luaurc` and every file below `Assets/`, `.meta` files included), must have been written by the editor through automation. Everything the audit needs is committed: `<Game>/Automation/Provenance.json` (§13.4) and the transcript `<Game>/Automation/BuildLog.jsonl` (§13.8). Nothing in the gitignored `Library/` is consulted, and the acceptance mode audits the staged blobs as a fresh clone holds them (`AuditProvenance.py --files index`, §2.3); `.gitattributes` marks the engine-consumed files of every project under `Projects/` and `Tests/Projects/` `-text`, so a clone holds the bytes provenance hashed. `Scripts/AuditProvenance.py` passes when, for every engine-consumed file:
1. `Provenance.json` has an entry whose `XXH64` equals the file's current hash;
2. that entry was produced by an automation method (`Method` and `Client` never `ui`), and the request line at its `TranscriptLine` in `BuildLog.jsonl` has the entry's `RequestId`, `Method` and `Client`; or it was produced by `project.upgrade` with client `cli` and no line (a legacy entry), and the transcript holds a `cli` `project.upgrade` request;
3. no provenance entry refers to a file that no longer exists, unless a later request in the transcript is an `asset.delete` or `asset.move` of it (or of its source), named by the method's `asset` param, that succeeded; a deleted folder or a move's destination never excuses an entry (`asset.delete` removes only a folder that holds no files and a move removes only its source, so an entry below a deleted folder or at a move's destination survives only when something else removed its file; ADR 0026, milestone review R03).

The finding codes and the warnings (`crlf`, `torn-line`) are `Scripts/Lib/provenance.py`'s (ADR 0026 decision 4), and `AuditProvenance.py --self-test` proves each against the seeded fixtures of `Tests/Data/Provenance/`. Index mode also fails every staged path that equals another once both are NFC-normalized and case-folded (`path-collision`): a case-insensitive file system holds one file for them, so a clone there, and the copy the rules read on such a machine, would lose the others. It is the one index-only code (`INDEX_ONLY_CODES`), which the disk-mode fixtures cannot hold, so `test_audit_provenance.py` builds it in a temporary repository instead (milestone review R07).

**Keeping the audit reproducible across engine changes.** An engine change that alters the canonical form of project files (a new component field written with its default, a migration, a `.meta` schema change) makes the byte-identical re-save test (§6) fail for the demo projects. The same change must therefore run `project.upgrade` on each affected project through automation (`Editor --headless --project P --upgrade` runs the same method and appends its own transcript lines, `project.refreshAssets` for its deferred asset scan and then `project.upgrade`, marked `client: "cli"`; §12.1). That rewrites the files, appends provenance entries and transcript lines, and keeps both the audit and the re-save test green (a material, sound effect or replay with a member its reader does not know fails the upgrade at its file and pointer instead of losing the member, §13.5). `project.validate {fix}` and replay re-recording (§13.6) are likewise automation calls, recorded the same way. Documentation (`AGENTS.md`, `README.md`) is exempt. Any capability an agent finds missing becomes an engine change (method + test) before the demo continues. The games suite runs the audit of every staged demo, in index mode, in PreCommit and in `CI.py`'s games stage (§15.1 T5, §15.8, §15.9; Roadmap M16; ADR 0026 decisions 8 and 9).

---

## 14. Runtime and export

### 14.1 Package format
```
Build/Windows-Dist/Tetris/
  Tetris.exe                         Runtime (chosen config), renamed; icon + version resource patched from Export.Icon/Version/Company
  vcruntime140.dll vcruntime140_1.dll msvcp140.dll   app-local CRT (staticruntime "off")
  Game.json                          manifest
  Data/Engine.pak                    target-config SPIR-V, the Default font, blue noise, the ten sfx presets and the baked
                                     built-in environments (procedural built-ins are generated at run time, §7.6)
  Data/Game.pak                      cooked project assets (Luau bytecode with line info, no source)
  THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt            generated from Vendor/*/VENDOR.md + Resources/LICENSES.md
Linux:  Tetris + Data/ + Game.json, RPATH $ORIGIN; system libvulkan.so.1 required (clear error if missing)
macOS:  Build/macOS-Dist/Tetris/ holds only Tetris.app/Contents/{Info.plist, MacOS/Tetris,
        Resources/{Game.json, Data/*.pak, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt, AppIcon.icns}, Frameworks/{libvulkan.1.dylib,
        libMoltenVK.dylib}, Resources/vulkan/icd.d/MoltenVK_icd.json}; ad-hoc signed; the Runtime opens
        Frameworks/libvulkan.1.dylib by its explicit path (dlopen of a bare leaf name does not search LC_RPATH, §8.1),
        and its -Wl,-rpath,@executable_path/../Frameworks serves only the dylibs' own @rpath references
        (ADR 0024 decision 5)
```
```json
{
	"Format": "GameManifest", "Version": 1, "Name": "Tetris", "EngineVersion": "0.1.0",
	"StartScene": "8a61c0d2e4f31b77",
	"Paks": [ { "Path": "Data/Engine.pak", "XXH64": "…" }, { "Path": "Data/Game.pak", "XXH64": "…" } ],
	"Window": { "Title": "Tetris", "Width": 720, "Height": 900, "VSync": true, "Fullscreen": false, "Resizable": true },
	"Simulation": { "FixedHz": 60, "MaxStepsPerFrame": 5, "Seed": 1, "MaxEntities": 65536 },
	"Testing": false
}
```
`Name` determines the exported game's user-data folder (`<UserData>/Tetris/` for logs, crashes and `user://`), never `ENGINE_PRODUCT_NAME`. `Window` carries every `WindowSettings` field. `EngineVersion` is the constant `0.1.0` in v1, which replays and session files carry too: a per-commit build version would change every replay header and session file, so build versioning is deferred (Appendix C; ADR 0025). A testing export's default directory is `Build/<Platform>-<Config>-Testing/<Name>`, so it never replaces the plain export (ADR 0024 decision 6). In a testing export (`"Testing": true`), the project's `Testing` settings (suites, replays, timeouts) ride in the Game.pak TOC like the other project settings.
- **Pak layout** (little-endian): 64-byte header `{char Magic[8] = "ENGPAK01"; uint32 Version; uint32 EntryCount; uint64 TocOffset; uint64 TocSize; uint64 TocXXH64; uint8 Reserved[24]}`, 16-byte-aligned entry data (each a cooked artifact with the §6.8 header), then a canonical JSON TOC (easy to inspect) `{"Entries": [{"Handle", "Type", "Path", "Offset", "Size", "XXH64"}]}` sorted by handle and loaded into a binary-searched table. Project settings and the asset registry ride in the TOC.
- **Integrity:** the TOC hash is verified at mount; every entry's XXH64 is verified on first load in all configurations, so script bytecode is always verified before `luau_load`. A failed check is an `InitFailed` (mount) or asset diagnostic (entry) with a clear message.
- **Compression:** none in v1 (no approved compressor). **Determinism:** same inputs → byte-identical paks (tested).

### 14.2 Export pipeline (`EditorCore/Export/Exporter`, `project.export`, `Editor --export`)
1. **Validate**: every build scene loads strictly, every script compiles and type-checks, no asset diagnostic has severity Error, every referenced handle resolves. A testing export also requires every `Testing.Suites` entry to be valid (`TEST_SUITE_INVALID`, located at `/Testing/Suites/<i>/Script` or `/Scene`) and every replay to compile (located at `/Testing/Replays/<i>`). Validation works under `--renderer none`, using cooked environment bakes (§7.4); an included environment without one is a located issue with the GPU hint, and a testing export also loads its suite scenes strictly. An existing output directory must be empty or hold an earlier export, which the exporter recognizes only by a `Game.json` (on macOS `<Name>.app/Contents/Resources/Game.json`) that loads as a `GameManifest`; any other file there, or a status the file system cannot report, refuses the export, and `MoveToOutput` checks the directory again right before it replaces it, so an export never replaces anything else (ADR 0024 decisions 2, 3 and 6; ADR 0025).
2. **Cook** every included asset (§7.6) incrementally from `Library/Cache` and the engine cooked cache, in parallel on jobs.
3. **Write** `Engine.pak` (with the target configuration's SPIR-V) and `Game.pak` atomically, then reopen and verify every hash.
4. **Copy** `bin/<Config>-<sys>-<arch>/Runtime/Runtime(.exe)`, renamed. A missing binary is an error naming `python Scripts/Build.py --config <Config> --project Runtime`; the editor never spawns a build. Windows also copies `Runtime/Redist/*.dll` (the app-local CRT, §2.3); macOS assembles the bundle with the SDK's loader and MoltenVK, staged by `Build.py` (the assembly is host-independent code that every OS's unit suite tests; only `codesign` is macOS-only, ADR 0024 decision 5).
5. **Patch resources** (Windows): `Platform/ResourcePatcher` (`Platform/Windows/ResourcePatcherWindows.cpp`) writes the icon group (PNG images of 16 to 256 px reduced from the square `Export.Icon`) and a `VS_VERSIONINFO` (`Version`, `Company`, product and file description = project `Name`) into the copied executable with `BeginUpdateResource`/`UpdateResource`/`EndUpdateResource`, keeping its manifest. Without an icon no icon group is written and the platform's default application icon shows (no engine icon exists). macOS writes the same data into `Info.plist` and an `.icns`; Linux has no executable resources (ADR 0024 decision 4).
6. **Write** `Game.json` (with pak hashes) and the notices file (`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, copied from the build output, ADR 0024 decision 8); macOS then ad-hoc signs the bundle (`codesign`, the only other process an export starts).
7. **Smoke test** (optional, always in CI): run the exported executable with `--headless --frames 300 --expect-no-errors`; exit 0 required.
8. Return a JSON report: assets, sizes, warnings, the time of each phase.

### 14.3 Runtime executable
`RuntimeApp` reads `Game.json` next to the executable (or in the bundle), sets the app name for logs, crashes and `user://` from the manifest `Name` (§4.4), mounts the paks with `RuntimeAssetManager`, creates the window from the manifest, loads the start scene through the **same `SceneSerializer::FromJson` path Play uses**, and runs a `PlaySession` until `Application.Quit()` or window close. With no Vulkan loader or device it shows a message box naming the problem and exits 3. `Escape` does nothing by default; games decide. When minimized, the loop throttles and the simulation keeps running (§4.2).

For `--feature-test` with `Testing:true`, an unspecified development user-data root defaults to `<export>/bin/TestUserData/<Name>` for logs, crashes and `user://`, including Dist; `<export>` is the manifest's directory, or for a macOS bundle the directory that holds the `.app`, so a test run never writes into the signed bundle (`GetTestingUserDataRoot`, ADR 0024 decision 6). An explicit development root takes precedence. Ordinary game runs retain the platform user-data folder (ADR 0019 decision 10).

### 14.4 Dist stripping
Dist Runtime builds contain no editor or `EditorCore`, no automation server or handlers, no ImGui (Release keeps an F1 stats overlay), no asserts or Trace logs, no validation layers, no asset hot reload (shaders hot-reload in no configuration, §8.12), no slangc lookup, no Luau Analysis, no reachable source compilation (bytecode only: `ScriptCompiler` is `Unsupported` in Dist; Luau's `Compiler`, `Parser` and `Ast` objects still link, reached by the require library's configuration parsing and by `ScriptApiRegistry`'s signature parsing, never by game input; ADR 0025, Appendix C), no `AssetPipeline`, and the WindowedApp subsystem; logs go to the user data folder. The **FeatureTest harness stays in Dist**: `FeatureTestRunner` with the suite runner, the cooked-replay player and the coverage counters. It is inert unless the manifest has `"Testing": true`, because testing the actual shipping binary matters more than a few kilobytes. In a testing run, behaviour that differs from the editor is defined, not accidental: hot reload does not exist (EditorOnly members are excluded from that mode's gates, §15.6), `Debug.Break` is a counted no-op, `Application.Quit` ends the current suite with a recorded result instead of exiting, `Application.IsEditor()` returns false, and replay expectations run from precompiled bytecode, because Dist compiles no source. The script watchdog stays on (Dist minimum 5 s outside test runs, §11.1). The Dist Runtime links with a map file (`/MAP` on MSVC and lld-link, `-Wl,-Map` with GNU ld and lld, `-Wl,-map` with ld64); a test fails if it lists any `AutomationServer`, `Luau::Frontend`, `ImGui` or `AssetPipeline` symbol. Because the commit gate builds no Dist, the test is the `CI.py` build-stage step named "Dist: map file contains no automation, Analysis, ImGui or AssetPipeline symbols", right after the Dist build (`CheckRuntimeMap.py`); it reads MSVC, LLVM lld, GNU ld and Apple ld maps, matches symbol names through namespace and type components of decorated, mangled or demangled names and origins through their library component, never string literals or object-file names; the families are derived from the headers (every type and every free function outside a `Utils` namespace of `Engine/Automation/**.h`, `Engine/ImGui/**.h` and `Engine/AssetPipeline/**.h`, plus `Luau::Frontend` and the ImGui (with ImGuizmo) and LuauAnalysis libraries; a `Utils` name also names internal-linkage helpers that stay in Dist), and it also requires the harness's `FeatureTestRunner` and `ReplayPlayer` (ADR 0024 decision 11).

---

## 15. Testing strategy

### 15.1 Tiers
| Tier | Content | Runner | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| T0 Static | format, include rules, banned APIs, clang-tidy naming, header self-containment, generated-file staleness, JSON validity, vendor define consistency, `spirv-val`, `Shaders.LayoutsMatchReflection`, `SharedStructsMatchReflection` | `Lint.py`, `CheckBuildConfig.py`, `Tests --test-suite=Static`, the `--check` of `GenerateDocs.py`, `GenerateDetMathReference.py`, the fixture generators and `FetchAssets.py` (§15.8, §15.9) | the milestone-end `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main); checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11) |
| T1 Unit | per-module doctest; no window, GPU or audio device (processes the tests start are headless or pass `--audio-device none`; only miniaudio's Null backend runs as a device, in the device tests of §10.4) | `Tests` | the milestone-end `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main); checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11) |
| T2 GPU | device/swapchain state machines, every pipeline creates, readback, CPU-reference GPU tests, golden images, leak counters, zero validation messages | `Tests --test-suite=GPU,Golden` | the milestone-end `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main); checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11) (this machine's GPU) |
| T3 FeatureTest | every component, field, enum value, setting, format, function, callback (§15.5) in three modes: editor headless lockstep, exported Release and exported Dist (testing exports); gates evaluated per mode (§15.6) | `Editor --run-tests`, `FeatureTest(.exe) --feature-test` | editor mode: the milestone-end `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main); checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11); the exported modes in the milestone-end `CI.py`'s export stage (ADR 0024 decision 9) |
| T3d Determinism | one 600-tick physics-and-script replay plus every FeatureTest replay: final state hashes identical across Editor Debug, Editor Release, exported Release and exported Dist, and equal to the committed `FinalStateHash` (every checkpoint compared on the way); in the editor runs, every replay with input events fails its verification without them (the input check, M16 friction S2-01) | `Test.py --suite determinism` | `CI.py` (editor Debug vs Release from M14, all four runs from M15; the exported runs' hashes come from the export stage's reports, ADR 0024 decision 10) |
| T4 Automation | Python `unittest` against a headless editor: every method, undo/redo, batch rollback, `$ref`, dry runs, security, watchdog, crash recovery, attach/lock/disconnect, export + run exported, MCP bridge conformance via the SDK client | `Test.py --suite automation` | the milestone-end `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main); checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11) |
| T5 Games | every staged demo below `Projects/` and `Tests/Projects/` (Breakout, Tetris, RollingBall; `Projects/FeatureTest` excluded): the provenance audit of its staged files against the committed `Provenance.json` and transcript (§13.12), its build settings first in each editor run (`StartScene` set, `Export.BuildScenes` not empty and listing `StartScene` as written; one failure line each, the run's other checks still made; `games.check_build_settings`, ADR 0027 decision 4, B1), `script.check` (no diagnostic at all, lint warnings included) and `project.validate` clean, the canonical re-save check (`project.upgrade {dryRun: true}` changes nothing), its suites and every `Testing.Replays` match at its committed `FinalStateHash` in the Debug and Release editors and in a testing Dist export (hashes identical across them, every checkpoint compared on the way), at least one committed replay with input events, and every replay with input events failing its verification without them in the editor runs (`input.replay {verify: true, strictHash: true, withoutInput: true}`, M16 friction S2-01; a game whose `Testing.Replays` matches no replay with input fails, milestone review R09), and a plain Dist export's smoke test | `Test.py --suite games` (ADR 0026 decisions 7 and 8) | from M16: `PreCommit.py` (commits straight to main: the audit and the Debug editor half) and checkpoint commits when their change touches it (ADR 0028 decision 11); all three halves in the milestone-end `CI.py`'s games stage |
| T6 Hardening | MSVC ASan build (`--sanitize=address` option) with the unit and feature suites, long fuzz runs (1,000,000 mutations per reader of external input), 10k-frame soak of every scene below `Assets/Scenes/` of every project under `Projects/` (FeatureTest and the demos there, not those under `Tests/Projects/`; Release editor, lockstep, no input, rendering the play scene through the editor's displayed Scene view, since a headless editor displays no Game view (soaking each scene's game view is a Backlog row, ADR 0027 friction F08); a script error raised during a scene's soak, in `OnStart` included, fails it, ADR 0027 decision 4, B4) plus the exported Release FeatureTest in the Runtime, which renders its game camera (private memory growth < 1 %, the script heap's lowest post-collection trough in the last quarter within max(128 KiB, 10 %) of its lowest trough in 0.25 to 0.75, a window without a collection failing unless its samples bound the growth, counters flat; a collection cycle longer than about a quarter of the soak can leave the heap rule inconclusive depending only on its phase, which a longer soak, `Hardening.py --soak-frames N`, confirms), Release perf capture (warning-only); the fuzz step's shard count and timeout follow the per-target costs its previous run measured (ADR 0028 decision 16), and it needs as many logical CPUs and GiB of available memory as its minimum shard count (14 with M17's measured costs) and otherwise refuses to start (exit code 3) | `CI.py --milestone` (or `--stages hardening`) | every milestone gate from M15 to M17, and from M18 each phase's last lander (local; ADR 0024 decisions 12 to 19, ADR 0028 decision 12) |

### 15.2 Unit tests per module (representative, not exhaustive)
- **Core:** `ENGINE_TRY`/context chaining; UUID hex round trip, prefix matching, seeded generator; XXH64 test vectors; `HandlePool` generation reuse; VFS escape, reserved-name and case rejection; `OverlayMount` never writes through; atomic writes under injected failure; `JobSystem(0)` ordering; `FixedStepScheduler` tables; `ScriptedClock` cycling; DetMath accuracy (≤ 1 ULP of correctly rounded references) and committed output hash; JsonReader error paths with pointers; canonical float formatting; `BinaryReader` bounds; deterministic mutation fuzzing (seeded byte flips, truncations) of BinaryReader, JsonReader, pak and cooked-header readers with a never-crash, always-`Result` oracle.
- **Platform:** input latching tables (taps between steps, multiple steps per frame, zero steps per frame); action/axis resolution incl. up-positive stick Y and `Invert`; null-platform window event path; crash child (`Tests --crash-child` writes a report and exits 4); `Editor.lock` exclusivity (a second lock attempt fails; the lock is released when the child is killed).
- **Reflection/Scene:** registry-parametrized component and struct tests (§5.4) incl. `Map`/`Variant` and non-finite rejection; hierarchy ops and cycle rejection; canonical order independence from insertion history; TransformSystem vs reference math; byte-identical re-save; every migration; `UnsupportedVersion` for a newer file; one fixture per structural defect (duplicate IDs, dangling and cyclic parents, child before parent, inconsistent prefab links, duplicate unique components) in strict and repair mode; prefab derived IDs, overrides surviving a prefab edit, user children preserved, external references intact; deferred destroy; serializer-copy state hash equality; interpolation snapshot rules (§5.2: script-moved, kinematic, frame-phase-written, teleported, created).
- **Asset:** Khronos glTF sample fixtures (textured, normal-mapped, alpha modes, embedded/data-URI, external `.bin`/`.png` closure); URI rejection (`..`, absolute, `http:`); `TEXCOORD_1` and `COLOR_0` diagnostics; dependency metas move and trash with their owner; a standalone texture referenced by a glTF is not duplicated; required-extension rejection; stable sub-asset handles after reimport; double-import hash equality; cache corruption recovery; registry diagnostics; pak round trip, tamper detection and determinism; `SoundSynth` bit-exactness; hot-reload races (no echo reimport after an editor write, stale job completion dropped, `SceneChangedOnDisk` raised and never auto-applied, reload deferred during lockstep).
- **Renderer (CPU):** device scoring (incl. storage-format support and the Bloom fallback); projection math (perspective and orthographic reverse-Z mappings, linear-depth and view-ray reconstruction for both, clip-space +Y up without a Y flip); cascade splits, frustum-corner fit for both projections, texel snapping; GTAO radius for both projections; atlas allocation; light culling; transparent sort ties; tonemapper CPU references; blue-noise hash.
- **Physics, Audio:** §9.7 and §10.4. **Scripting:** one test per registered function, method, property, operator and constructor (enforced by comparing the test registry with `ScriptApiRegistry`); every callback; sandbox escape suite (`getfenv`/`setfenv`, `setmetatable(_G)`, writing library tables or another module's globals, `string.dump`, `debug` access, `gcinfo`/`collectgarbage`, `require` outside `Assets/`); load-time VM (multi-file require graph, shared budget, engine API at module top rejected, classification of Behaviour/Module/TestSuite); yield rule (§11.3); watchdog (including during GC-heavy loops and nested callbacks inheriting the deadline); memory soft limit, second breach and hard limit; error-message format and locations (callbacks and resumed threads); hot reload (behaviour state, module patch plus dependents, chain rollback); `math.random` and DetMath-rebound `math` and `vector.angle` determinism; bounded host copies of script strings (log size and rate, debug text, names, tags); test runner (suite collection, sequential cases, per-case timeout, isolation, quit handling, filter, result schema); type-checker fixtures (§11.9). **Play-session fuzz:** 10,000 seeded random calls through the bindings and automation (NaN, ±Inf, 0, negative, huge, subnormal, wrong types, destroyed entities) never crash, assert or reach Jolt; every rejection is a located script or protocol error. A fixed corpus with exact expectations is followed by calls whose arguments are generated from the seed for Transform and collider writes, `AddComponent` values, `Scene.Instantiate` position, rotation and parent (including parents whose scale or translation make a finite position unrepresentable) and `SetParent` on physics entities; after each, every transform in the scene and every body stays finite.
- **EditorCore:** command property tests (§12.3); composite atomicity; dirty tracking; validator codes (incl. selective fixes); exporter validation; automation framing, auth, schema validation, `dryRun` (overlay, `supportsDryRun`, no provenance), `ifRevision`, `$ref`, bounded outputs, case-insensitive enums; an in-process round trip for every method.

**Test infrastructure** (`Tests/Source/Support`): custom doctest main (`DOCTEST_CONFIG_IMPLEMENT`) that creates the `ProcessContext` (headless platform) and parses `--require-gpu`, `--death-test=`, `--windowed-child=`, `--update-golden`; the recording assert handler and death-test helper (§4.5); the `ExpectLog` listener (§4.4); `TempDirectory` RAII; seeded `UUIDGenerator`/`Random`; `HeadlessGpuFixture` (skips with a reason when no Vulkan 1.3 device exists unless `--require-gpu`, which `CI.py` and `PreCommit.py` pass unless `--gpu-optional`; fails a test on any validation or NVRHI error or warning and on any object still alive; `Test::ProbeGpuForProcess` for tests whose code under test creates its own device); per-test timeout watchdog; glm helpers `ApproxEqual(a, b, epsilon)` plus `doctest::StringMaker` specializations for vec/quat/mat.

### 15.3 GPU correctness tests (device-independent oracles)
Shadow fallback coverage also creates a device with `GraphicsSpecification::DisableDepthClamp`: the reported pancaking capability is false, and real caster/receiver tests exercise the extended-near path under both API caps. The next default device pancakes again (§8.7; ADR 0017).

Clear and exact readback; compute arithmetic; matrix convention; winding/culling; octahedral pack/unpack vs CPU; **white furnace**: BRDF energy ≈ 1 with multi-scatter compensation, prefiltered constant environment = 1 ± 0.5% in every mip, SH irradiance constant; DFG LUT vs CPU reference; GPU prefilter vs CPU reference at 16²; equirect-to-cube seam continuity; tonemapper curves vs CPU at 1,024 points; shadow stability under a 0.37-texel camera move; picking returns the expected UUID at known pixels; ImGui texture protocol; pipeline count; zero leaks at device destruction; host-image-copy and staging uploads read back identically (§8.1); the swapchain state machine (windowed child process) recovers from out-of-date and minimize; fault injection (§8.14) exits 4 with a crash report and, in the editor, an autosave (§8.14 item 8); each test also runs with `--vulkan-api=1.3`. The GPU stage enables `VK_VALIDATION_FEATURE_ENABLE_SYNCHRONIZATION_VALIDATION_EXT` together with standard validation, and any validation or synchronization-validation message, error or warning, fails the test: in process through `HeadlessGpuFixture`, which reads the device's counts after its teardown, and in the Editor and Runtime processes a test starts through `--expect-no-gpu-errors`. A meta-test provokes a real hazard and a real validation error to prove the wiring.

### 15.4 Golden images
Deterministic scenes from `Projects/FeatureTest/Assets/Scenes/Golden/*.scene`, rendered headless at 640×360 at fixed ticks with FXAA on and fixed noise: PBR roughness × metallic sphere grid under the Studio HDRI, IBL only, CSM PCSS near/far cascades, orthographic-camera shadows (`ShadowsOrtho`), spot shadows, GTAO on/off and with an orthographic camera (`GtaoOrtho`), each tonemapper, bloom, alpha mask/blend, debug draw, selection outline, SDF text, an imported glTF fixture, the default editor layout (`editor.screenshot`). Goldens live in `Tests/Golden/<DeviceClass>/` (device class = vendor + driver major, e.g. `nvidia-58x`). Comparison: fail if more than 0.1% of pixels differ by more than 2/255 or any pixel by more than 24/255 (thresholds tunable per test); failures write `actual`, `expected` and `diff` PNGs to `bin/TestResults/Golden/`. On a device class without goldens the suite runs in **smoke mode** (renders, zero validation errors, finite pixels, luminance statistics within loose bounds) and reports "goldens missing" as a warning, never a pass of the comparison. `Test.py --update-golden` writes candidates that must be reviewed (skill `golden-images`) before commit. No software rasterizer goldens and no cross-device comparison. The scenes are written by the committed scaffold `Projects/FeatureTest/Scaffold/Golden.jsonl`. Its M9 part (the nine `Golden/*.scene` files `AnnotatedScreenshot`, `GtaoOff`, `GtaoOn`, `GtaoOrtho`, `SelectionOutline`, `ShadowsFar`, `ShadowsNear`, `ShadowsOrtho` and `SpotShadows`, and the three `Materials/Golden/RendererII` materials, each with its `.meta`: 24 files) was copied in from a unit test's replay of its last 30 requests, not written by an editor run of the project, so `Provenance.json` has no entry for them; FeatureTest is never audited, so this is accepted until a reviewed regeneration through a recorded editor run (ADR 0022 review fix M11; ADR 0025; Appendix C) (§15.5; the fixtures it imports sit in `Scaffold/Sources` as byte-identical copies of `Tests/Data/Assets`, which a test checks) and rendered by `Test::RenderGoldenScene` from a temporary copy of the project (an `EditorAssetManager` with the engine resources, an `EnvironmentBaker` and the editor's generators, then `ViewportCapture`), which fails on any load or scan error and on any Error-level asset diagnostic instead of rendering placeholders. The M8 golden scenes and the legacy LitScene fixture turn SSAO off and disable shadow casting, so M9's passes leave their images unchanged. M9 scenes explicitly enable the passes they exercise. The editor layout golden renders the actual Editor host at its full retained UI extent, rather than the 640×360 scene-capture extent. The DebugDraw golden appends its primitives to the extracted snapshot instead of running a script, so its image depends on the renderer alone. The committed goldens are those of device class `nvidia-61x` (ADR 0013 decisions 14, 21 and 24).

### 15.5 The FeatureTest project (`Projects/FeatureTest`)
Each scene below is bound to one or more suites in `Testing.Suites` (§11.10). Every suite runs in all three modes unless noted.

| Scene | Exercises |
|---|---|
| `Components.scene` (with `Components/Tonemap{Aces,Linear,PbrNeutral}.scene` for the tonemappers one scene's unique `PostProcess` cannot hold together) | one or more entities per component, **every field set to a non-default value** somewhere and **every value of every serialized enum** used somewhere (gate 1b); a suite checks the round trip and renders it |
| `Scripting.scene` | `Assets/Tests/Api/*.test.luau`: one suite per API module and userdata type (`Entity`, `Transform`, `Scene`, `Input`, `Time`, `Physics`, `Audio`, `Assets`, `Task`, `Random`, `Math`, `Quat`, `Color`, `Debug`, `Log`, `Application`, `Script`, `Field`, `Test`, and the `RigidBody`, `CharacterController`, `AudioSource`, `Camera`, `MeshRenderer` proxies), covering every function, method, property, operator, constructor and every value of small string-enum parameters, plus proxy read/write of every scriptable field of every component; `Api/EditorOnly.test.luau` runs in the Editor mode only (`Modes: ["Editor"]`, the EditorOnly members) |
| `Callbacks.scene` | suites that assert every lifecycle callback fires in the documented order, including `OnEnable`/`OnDisable`, collision and trigger enter/exit (incl. synthesized exits on destroy and disable), `OnDestroy`, and `OnHotReload` through `Test.ReloadScript` (Editor mode only, EditorOnly members) |
| `Physics.scene` | bodies, compounds (contacts name the child collider), shared static bodies, implicit static and sensor bodies, kinematic platform, CCD ball, character controller (inner body seen by triggers and raycasts), triggers on sleeping bodies, queries and bounds, layer matrix, seam roll |
| `Audio.scene` | spatial/non-spatial sources, groups, one-shots, streamed clip, `.sfx` clips, asserted with `Test.CaptureAudio` (deterministic: lockstep pulls 48000 / FixedHz frames per tick, 750 at FeatureTest's 64 Hz, no-threading resource manager, §10.1) |
| `Prefabs.scene` | instantiate from scripts and editor, overrides, nested-flattened prefab, references into instances |
| `SceneLoadA.scene` → `SceneLoadB.scene` | `Scene.Load` with parameters, VM reset |
| `Input.scene` | driven by `Assets/Tests/Replays/Input.replay`: keys, mouse, gamepad (incl. up-positive stick Y), actions, axes, phase-aware edges |
| `Timing.scene` | a suite with `Clock: [0.004, 0.016, 0.0334, 0.083, 0.5, 0.0]`, which yields 0, 1, 2 and 5 steps per frame plus clamping and dropped time. It asserts exactly-once `Pressed`/`Released` edges in both phases, `Time.GetDeltaTime()` per phase, the extraction `Alpha` (`Test.GetLastExtraction`), and interpolated positions of a script-moved, a physics-moved and a teleported entity (`Test.GetExtractedPosition`), all against analytic expectations. The suite binds the scene with `Parameters: {RecordTiming: true}`, and the scene's probe records only then, so a play outside the suite (editor Play, the soak) calls no test-only API (ADR 0027, friction F01) |
| `Errors.scene` | **runtime faults only**: a runtime error, a timeout (suite override `CallbackBudgetMs: 50`) and a memory-cap breach (override `MemoryLimitMB: 16`). Asserts isolation, `Test.ExpectScriptError` and structured reports. Compile and type errors are not in the project, because they would block test runs and export (§11.9); their error kinds are covered by unit fixtures (`Tests/Data/Scripts/Errors/`) |
| `Formats.scene` | loads every asset under `Assets/Formats/`: PNG, JPEG, TGA, BMP; WAV, FLAC, MP3, Ogg Vorbis; TTF, OTF; Radiance HDR; glTF variants (`.glb`, `.gltf` with external buffers and images, embedded and data-URI); `.sfx`. The suite instantiates or plays each one, so every importer × extension is cooked and loaded in every mode (gate 9). PNG, JPEG (`.jpg` and `.jpeg`), BMP and TGA fixtures are generated deterministically by `Tests/Data/Generate/MakeTextureFixtures.py` (stdlib), HDR and TTF by `MakeFormatFixtures.py` (stdlib), glTF by `MakeGltfFixtures.py`, WAV and FLAC by `MakeAudioFixtures.py` (stdlib); `.sfx` is authored by the scaffold (ADR 0022 decision 16). MP3 and Ogg Vorbis are small pinned public-domain files and OTF a pinned OFL file, fetched with operator approval (§2.3; ADR 0015 decision 25) |
| `QuitApp.scene` | a suite with `ExpectQuit: 3` that calls `Application.Quit(3)` |
| `Determinism.scene` | the scene of `Determinism.replay` ("Determinism replay" below), bound to no suite |
| `Golden/*.scene` | §15.4 |

**Settings coverage.** `Projects/FeatureTest/FeatureTest.eproj` uses non-default values wherever they are observable at run time (gravity, layers and matrix, fixed rate, seed, input actions with inversion, rendering, scripting limits, `MaxEntities`). `Tests/Data/Project/AllSettings.eproj` sets **every** `ProjectSettings` field to a non-default value. A unit test loads it, applies it to an `EngineContext` and asserts each value is observed by its consumer (gate 8).

**Determinism replay.** `Assets/Tests/Replays/Determinism.replay` (600 ticks: a 50-body pile, a rolling ball on a compound track, scripted forces, script-side DetMath trigonometry and `^` with a non-trivial exponent) carries a committed `FinalStateHash`. It runs in every mode, and the `determinism` stage compares the hashes (§15.8).

FeatureTest scenes are produced by committed automation batch scaffolds (`Projects/FeatureTest/Scaffold/*.jsonl`, run with `Editor --batch --id-seed <n>` from `Scaffold/Baseline/` in the order and with the seeds of `Scaffold/Scaffolds.json`, by `Scaffold.py`; ADR 0022 decision 8), so they stay byte-for-byte reproducible and exercise the automation surface; test scripts live under `Assets/Tests/`. The harness (`Engine/Testing/FeatureTestRunner`, §11.10) runs every suite and replay in `Testing`, collects results in the shared schema, evaluates the coverage gates for the current mode (the project opts in with `Testing.CoverageGates`; only an unfiltered, non-recording run evaluates them) and writes JUnit XML plus `bin/TestResults/FeatureTest-<mode>.json` (the editor's `--junit <file.xml>` puts both beside each other elsewhere, so the Debug and Release editors do not collide); exit 1 on any failure (ADR 0022 decisions 3 and 7).

### 15.6 Coverage gates
- **Gate 1, components:** every registered component appears in FeatureTest scenes that are simulated or rendered, and every serialized field holds a non-default value somewhere.
- **Gate 1b, enum values:** every value of every enum reachable from a serialized component field (`Tonemap` AgX/ACES/PbrNeutral/Linear, `Attenuation`, `BodyType`, `MotionQuality`, `SsaoQuality`, `ClearMode`, `ProjectionType`, `TextSpace`, `TextAlignment`, `AudioGroup`, `PrefabOverrideKind`, …; ADR 0022 decision 4) appears in a simulated or rendered FeatureTest scene. Enums that only asset files hold (`AlphaMode`, `SoundWave`, import settings) are not gated per value; their importer and asset tests cover them, and gate 9 loads every format that carries them.
- **Gate 2, script API:** every `ScriptApiRegistry` counter is non-zero: functions, methods, properties, operators and constructors (§11.4), plus every per-value counter of string-enum parameters.
- **Gate 3, script callbacks:** every lifecycle callback was invoked at least once.
- **Gate 4, proxy fields:** every scriptable component field was read and written through a proxy at least once.
- **Gate 5, automation methods:** every registered method was called by `Tests/Automation` (the bridge-side client counts calls).
- **Gate 6, validation codes:** every `project.validate` code is triggered by a fixture in `Tests/Data/Validation/`.
- **Gate 7, descriptions:** every type, field, function, method, property and automation method has a non-empty description.
- **Gate 8, project settings:** every `ProjectSettings` field is non-default in `Tests/Data/Project/AllSettings.eproj`, and the settings test observes each one (§15.5).
- **Gate 9, formats:** every importer and every importable extension (`Asset/SourceFormats.h`; `.exr` is refused, so it is excluded) is cooked and loaded in the run, as observed through the asset manager's load observer; only the loads of the run's own work count (inside `FeatureTestRunner::Advance`), never those of the editor's viewports or thumbnails in the host frames between, so no count depends on how many frames ran; the foreign formats' fixtures live in `Assets/Formats/` (ADR 0022 decision 5 and "Integration").

**Per-mode evaluation.** FeatureTest runs in three modes: editor headless lockstep (which also catches editor/runtime divergence), exported Release and exported Dist (cook and strip bugs). Each run records its counters in `FeatureTest-<mode>.json`. Gates 1–4, 1b and 9 are evaluated **per mode**: in each mode, every entry available in that mode (`RunModes::All`, plus `EditorOnly` in the editor) must be covered, and an `EditorOnly` entry counts only in the editor run. A merge step (`Test.py --merge-coverage`) then checks that the union over the three runs covers every registered entry, so nothing is exempt in all modes; it compares id sets per mode, never the registry fingerprint, which differs between modes by design (ADR 0022 decision 6). Gates 5–8 are mode-independent. Each gate has a seeded-gap fixture (`Tests/Data/CoverageGaps/`) proving that it fails and names exactly what is uncovered. Gates 1–4, 1b and 9 have one fixture per mode, for example an entry covered only by the editor run makes the Dist evaluation fail.

### 15.7 Automation and game tests
`Tests/Automation/test_*.py` (stdlib `unittest` + `Tools/Automation/engine_client.py`) start headless editors on temp projects: project lifecycle, launcher state, every method, scaffold batches, play/step/waitFor (incl. the 50 ms step budget and `stateHash`), recording rules (Edit-mode start, `restart`, capture of `Test.Inject*`, header parameters), replays, undo/redo byte-equality, dry runs (no file, watcher event, trash item or provenance entry; `Unsupported` for non-dry-runnable methods; batch rejection with `failedOp`), crash recovery, `Busy` watchdog (a script spin-loop under a raised budget), project lock (a second editor exits 3; `--read-only` opens), `editor_launch` attaching to a running editor, client disconnect (pending operations cancelled, lockstep released, play paused), `project.upgrade` provenance entries, security (bad token, HTTP probe, path escapes, oversized frame), export and run exported, and MCP conformance (the SDK's client lists tools with compact schemas, calls `scene_tree`, receives an image from `viewport_screenshot`), and the provenance audit of a project built through the bridge's launcher (`test_audit_provenance.py`, ADR 0026). Game suites run each staged demo's `Testing` suites and replays (`Test.py --suite games`, §15.8).

### 15.8 CI entry point
`python Scripts/CI.py` runs stages in order, each failing fast: `setup → generate → lint → build → bake → unit → gpu → golden → feature → automation → export → determinism → games → portability` (plus `hardening` at each phase's last lander: `CI.py --milestone`, `Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 12). Every stage names its configuration:

| Stage | Configuration(s) | Content |
|---|---|---|
| setup, generate, lint | n/a | toolchain checks, project generation, the static checks (`Scripts/Lib/scripts.py` `run_static_checks`, the list §15.9 gives): `CheckBuildConfig.py` with its fixtures, `Lint.py` (header self-containment included) and its self-test, `CheckRuntimeMap.py --self-test`, the provenance audit self-test (`AuditProvenance.py --self-test` over `Tests/Data/Provenance/`), the format check, the fixture generators' `--check`, the DetMath reference check (`GenerateDetMathReference.py --check`) and both `FetchAssets.py` checks |
| build | Debug, Release, Dist | all projects existing in each configuration (EditorCore, Editor, Tests have no Dist); after the Dist build, the step "Dist: map file contains no automation, Analysis, ImGui or AssetPipeline symbols" (§14.4) |
| bake | Release | `Editor --headless --renderer vulkan --bake-engine-assets` fills the configuration-independent `bin/EngineCache` (incremental; a no-op when nothing changed); without a device it fails, and with `--gpu-optional` it reruns with `--renderer none` (the environments left out with a warning) and ends as a warning |
| unit | Debug, Release | `Tests` (T0 static suite + T1), incl. committed-hash physics and DetMath tests |
| gpu | Debug, Release | `Tests --test-suite=GPU --require-gpu`, validation + synchronization validation, both API caps; the fixture fails a test on any validation or NVRHI error or warning (`--gpu-optional` drops `--require-gpu` on a machine without a Vulkan device) |
| golden | Release | `Tests --test-suite=Golden --require-gpu` (CPU inputs are configuration-independent per §9.1; SPIR-V flags are identical in Debug and Release, so the commit gate compares the same goldens in Debug) |
| feature | Debug, Release | `Editor --headless --run-tests --strict` on FeatureTest (editor mode; §6) |
| automation | Release | Python suites and MCP conformance against the Release editor (the bridge's default); `--require-gpu` like gpu for the tests that start a rendering editor (the screenshot methods), `--gpu-optional` drops it |
| export | Release editor → Release and Dist testing exports | from a `--strict` editor, exports a copy of FeatureTest with `testing: true` into `bin/Exports/<Config>/FeatureTest/`, runs `--feature-test` in both, evaluates each mode's gates, then merges coverage over the three modes with the feature stage's Editor report (§15.6); `--require-gpu` like gpu (ADR 0024 decision 9) |
| determinism | Debug, Release editors (`--strict`); exported Release and Dist | compares the final state hash of `Determinism.replay` and of every FeatureTest replay across all four runs and with the committed `FinalStateHash` (each run compares the checkpoints as it plays); any difference fails. The editor runs also require every replay with input events to fail its verification without them (the input check; both FeatureTest replays fail it as required: `Determinism.replay` by its hashes and expectations, `Input.replay`, whose probe keeps its observations in script tables, by its expectations). The editor runs need no GPU; the exported runs' hashes come from the export stage's reports and, without them and without `--require-gpu`, are reported as not compared (a warning; ADR 0024 decision 10) |
| games | Debug, Release editors (`--strict`); Release editor → Dist exports | from M16 (ADR 0026 decision 8): every staged demo below `Projects/` and `Tests/Projects/` (FeatureTest excluded; one passed step, `games`, while none exists): the provenance audit of its staged files (index mode, §13.12), then, in strict rendering Debug and Release editors on its staged blobs, the build-settings check first (a set `StartScene` that a non-empty `Export.BuildScenes` lists; ADR 0027 decision 4, B1), `script.check` and `project.validate` clean, the canonical re-save check (`project.upgrade {dryRun: true}` changes nothing) and its suites and replays at their committed hashes, at least one committed replay with input events and every one failing its verification without them (the input check), and a testing Dist export run with `--feature-test` whose hashes must equal the committed ones and the editor runs', then a plain Dist export's smoke test; `--require-gpu` like gpu |
| portability | n/a | generation for other OSes (file lists, precompiled-header paths, xcode4 Dist LTO, the Runtime's link settings), clang-cl builds of Engine + Tests (Release) and of every Dist project |
| hardening | Release + ASan | perf capture (plain Release, warning-only), soak (plain Release editor per scene of every project under `Projects/`, FeatureTest and the demos there, rendering the play scene through the editor's displayed Scene view, a new script error failing the scene; and the exported Release FeatureTest, rendering its game camera), the AddressSanitizer workspace in `bin-int/Asan`, its unit and feature suites, long fuzz (`Scripts/Hardening.py`, `Scripts/Perf.py`; only with `--milestone` or an explicit `--stages`, ADR 0024 decision 19). Within a phase, only the last lander's milestone-end `CI.py` adds `--milestone` (the hardening stage: perf, soak, ASan, fuzz), on the merged tree of all the phase's milestones, when any milestone of the phase touches core systems, file readers (fuzzed formats) or the renderer, as its plan or contract decides; every other milestone end runs `CI.py --require-clang-cl` without `--milestone`. The program's setup gate keeps its own rule (ADR 0028 decision 16), and M27, alone in phase 4, decides in its plan (ADR 0028 decision 12) |

Because simulation is deterministic across configurations (§9.1), replays and expected hashes recorded in any configuration are verified in all of them; the `determinism` stage is the mechanical proof. **Portability** (local, no remote needed): premake generation for `--os=linux gmake`, `--os=linux ninja` and `--os=macosx xcode4` must succeed with the expected file lists and project/configuration sets; the gmake and xcode4 precompiled-header paths must exist (xcode4's `GCC_PREFIX_HEADER` resolved as Xcode does), and the first-party xcode4 projects must enable LTO (`LLVM_LTO`) in Dist; the Runtime's generated link settings must hold in every configuration (`Scripts/Lib/portability.py`, ADR 0024 decision 18: the Linux `$ORIGIN` RPATH and map file in gmake and ninja, the Frameworks rpath, map file, deployment target 14.0 and tool product type in xcode4, `/MAP` and the subsystems in vs2026); then `clang-cl` builds (`toolset "clang"`) of Engine + Tests in Release and of every Dist project (asserts compiled out, so Clang reports helpers only asserts use), with warnings tolerated only in vendored code, when the VS Clang component is installed, otherwise reported as skipped (a failure with `--require-clang-cl`). Header self-containment is not a portability check: it is the `headers` step of `Lint.py` in the lint stage, because it needs the compilation database `Lint.py` generates. GitHub is only an off-site backup of the repository: GitHub Actions is not part of the process, and nothing reads or waits for its results. From the program's setup commit, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs only when started by hand (`workflow_dispatch`), and no step of the process starts it. As written, it holds jobs for windows, ubuntu-24.04 with GCC 14 and Clang 19, and macos arm64, each running its stages through `CI.py` (the Windows job with `--require-clang-cl`; on Linux the lint stage in the Clang job only); the hosted runners have no GPU, so the Linux jobs run the gpu, golden and automation stages on Lavapipe (Mesa's software Vulkan driver, `mesa-vulkan-drivers`, which meets the Vulkan 1.3 feature set of §8.1) with the SDK's `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation`, inside the unit stage's Xvfb display and with `--require-gpu`, golden images in smoke mode (§15.4: no software-rasterizer goldens), and the Windows and macOS jobs run them with `--gpu-optional`. Windows is the only verified platform. First-party code stays portable by rule (the `Platform/` layering, no Windows-only shortcuts, the local portability stage's premake generation and clang-cl builds), but Linux and macOS are never verified: the Linux/macOS catch-up was dropped by the product owner, because there is no Linux or macOS machine without GitHub. This replaces ADR 0011's non-blocking remote CI and the catch-up of ADR 0026 decision 1 (ADR 0028 decision 10). Results: JUnit in `bin/TestResults/`, a summary table on stdout, exit code per §4.1.

### 15.9 Commit gate and code review
Before every commit, the gate that fits it (`Docs/Decisions/0023-verification-without-duplicate-work.md`; `Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decisions 11 and 12). Wave and contract commits are checkpoint commits on the milestone branch: each is reviewed and runs only the tests and suites its change touches, never `PreCommit.py` or `CI.py`. The milestone end runs one `CI.py` on the milestone's final tree instead of PreCommit. Within a phase, only the last lander's milestone-end `CI.py` adds `--milestone` (the hardening stage: perf, soak, ASan, fuzz), on the merged tree of all the phase's milestones, when any milestone of the phase touches core systems, file readers (fuzzed formats) or the renderer, as its plan or contract decides; every other milestone end runs `CI.py --require-clang-cl` without `--milestone`. The program's setup gate keeps its own rule (ADR 0028 decision 16), and M27, alone in phase 4, decides in its plan. (This supersedes ADR 0024 decision 19 in part.) CI runs every PreCommit stage (golden and automation in Release only, which covers them: CPU inputs and SPIR-V are configuration-independent and asserts stay on in Release). Commits straight to main outside a milestone keep ADR 0023's rule: `PreCommit.py`, or for documentation-only edits the checks that read the edited files. PreCommit is green when these pass (generate; the full lint-stage list of static checks: `CheckBuildConfig.py` on the workspace and on every fixture workspace, `Lint.py` and its self-test, `CheckRuntimeMap.py --self-test`, the provenance audit self-test `AuditProvenance.py --self-test` (ADR 0026 decision 9), the format check, every `Tests/Data/Generate/*.py --check`, the DetMath reference check `GenerateDetMathReference.py --check` (its self-test, then a byte comparison of `Tests/Source/Engine/Core/DetMathReferenceData.h` with a fresh generation; ADR 0025), `FetchAssets.py defaults --check` and `FetchAssets.py fixture --check`; Debug build; the `references` check, `GenerateDocs.py --check --config Debug`, which fails when any file of the generated reference (`Docs/Reference/*`, `Resources/Scripting/Engine.d.luau`, `Tools/MCP/catalog.json`; ADR 0022 decision 13) is missing, stale or extra compared with what the built editor generates (ADR 0019 decision 9); unit, gpu, golden, feature, automation and games suites in Debug, the automation suite against the Debug editor and the games suite as its audit of every staged demo and their Debug editor half (no Dist half: PreCommit builds no Dist; ADR 0026 decision 8), and the gpu, golden, feature, automation and games suites with `--require-gpu` (§15.1 T2 runs on this machine's GPU; `--gpu-optional` only on a machine without a Vulkan device)), then a **recorded review**: the `commit-review` skill (§13.10) reviews the staged diff against `Docs/ReviewChecklist.md` (correctness, error handling per §4.6, ownership, threading, determinism, tests added and meaningful, generated files refreshed, style and naming, no TODOs masking defects, docs updated). Blocking: any failing check, any unresolved checklist item, any unfixed confirmed finding, a nit included, missing tests for new behaviour, a style violation. One review pass per wave: blocker and major findings are verified before they are fixed, every confirmed finding is fixed at once, minors and nits included (never batched or deferred), and there are no rechecks of rechecks. A contract checkpoint gets one review of the frozen interfaces; the wave's one review pass covers the integrated wave diff; neither is rechecked. Milestones run in phases: the milestones of a phase are developed in parallel, each on its own branch in its own worktree, and their milestone-end `CI.py` runs go one at a time on the one GPU. Milestone end: the milestone branch merges current main, then one review pass of the milestone diff with every finding fixed, then one `CI.py` on the final tree; main fast-forwards to the milestone branch only after that run is green. Phase end: once the phase's last milestone has landed, main is pushed to `origin` as an off-site backup, and the work pauses for the product owner's go-ahead. (ADR 0028 decisions 11 and 14.) The review and its fixes precede the gate. The commit message ends with a `Reviewed:` trailer summarizing the review outcome and the gate result (a checkpoint commit names its targeted runs). Documentation edits after a green gate keep the result unless a check reads the edited file, and a red gate reruns only its failed stages unless the fix changed production code, build files or shared test support (ADR 0023). PreCommit and the milestone-end `CI.py` are strict: no `ENGINE_CONTRACT_STUB` and no test case skipped outside the child-process targets (`Test::ChildTargetSuite`) may remain (the `contract` step of `Lint.py` and the skip check of `Test.py`, `Docs/Decisions/0004-contract-stub-gate.md`). A contract checkpoint runs its targeted checks in contract mode (`Lint.py --allow-contract-stubs`, `Test.py --allow-skips`); the wave's integration replaces every stub and skip of its contract, and the milestone-end `CI.py` is strict, so none reaches main (Roadmap rule 3).

## 16. Cross-platform concerns

| | Windows x64 | Linux x64 (Ubuntu 24.04+) | macOS arm64 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Status | **verified** on every change; the only verified platform | generation-checked locally (the portability stage); never verified (ADR 0028 decision 10) | generation-checked locally (the portability stage); never verified (ADR 0028 decision 10) |
| Toolchain | VS 2026 (v145, MSVC 14.51), `vs2026` | GCC 14 / Clang 19+, `gmake`/`ninja` | Xcode 26 (minimum), `xcode4`, deployment target macOS 14.0 |
| Windowing | Win32 GLFW | **X11 GLFW only** (vendored build); Wayland sessions run through XWayland; native Wayland deferred (steps in `Vendor/GLFW/VENDOR.md`) | Cocoa GLFW; framebuffer ≠ window size (Retina) |
| Vulkan | driver `vulkan-1.dll`, loaded dynamically | `libvulkan.so.1` (package `libvulkan1`) | loader + MoltenVK (Vulkan 1.3/1.4 near-conformant, macOS 14+); KosmicKrisp used when installed (Vulkan 1.4 conformant, needs macOS 26 + Apple silicon); portability enumeration + `VK_KHR_portability_subset` |
| Audio | WASAPI → DirectSound → WinMM | PulseAudio/PipeWire → ALSA → JACK (dlopen) | CoreAudio (linked, `MA_NO_RUNTIME_LINKING`) |
| Export | app-local CRT DLLs; icon and version resources patched | RPATH `$ORIGIN` | `.app` bundle with loader + ICD, rpath `@executable_path/../Frameworks`, `Info.plist` + `.icns`, ad-hoc signed |

Windows is the only verified platform. First-party code stays portable by rule (the `Platform/` layering, no Windows-only shortcuts, the local portability stage's premake generation and clang-cl builds), but Linux and macOS are never verified: the Linux/macOS catch-up was dropped by the product owner, because there is no Linux or macOS machine without GitHub. Windows backends only: a kept item's platform interface stays, so a later port can add backends, but no Linux or macOS backend is written, and on Linux and macOS the interface reports the capability as unavailable (`ErrorCode::Unsupported`) through the platform layer's existing error path. The rule covers the platform capabilities the program adds in first-party C++: existing POSIX backends (`Platform/Posix/`) keep compiling when their interface changes, and the Python tooling keeps its guarded POSIX branches. Vendored libraries' premake scripts stay portable (the portability stage generates gmake and xcode4 projects from them); their GCC, Clang and Apple Clang builds are never compiled. (`Docs/Decisions/0028-backlog-program.md` decision 10.) Common rules: precise floating point and `JPH_CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC` on every platform (§2.2); all binary formats little-endian (asserted); text LF; JSON UTF-8 without BOM; data paths always `/`; the VFS case policy protects case-sensitive filesystems; paths are UTF-8 internally and converted only in `Platform`; platform code lives only in `Platform/<OS>/` and `Graphics` device setup (lint-enforced), so the untested platforms concentrate risk in few small files. The renderer avoids features with weak Metal mappings (geometry shaders, wide lines, large bindless arrays). Shaders are SPIR-V 1.6 from one source on every OS. `std::stacktrace`, modules, `std::flat_map` and coroutines are not used (CodeStyle §13).

## 17. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Linux/macOS rot without local builds | platform code isolated; generation smoke tests and clang-cl/header checks every run; honest status labels: Windows is the only verified platform. First-party code stays portable by rule (the `Platform/` layering, no Windows-only shortcuts, the local portability stage's premake generation and clang-cl builds), but Linux and macOS are never verified: the Linux/macOS catch-up was dropped by the product owner, because there is no Linux or macOS machine without GitHub. (ADR 0028 decision 10) |
| ODR/ABI mismatch with vendored libs (NDEBUG, Jolt, spdlog, miniaudio) | `Use<Lib>()` copied from VENDOR.md; `CheckBuildConfig.py`; workspace-scope `NDEBUG`; `VerifyJoltVersionID` fatal at startup |
| NVRHI or Slang drift | pinned commits/versions; NVRHI only in Graphics/Renderer/ImGui; reflection checks CPU-only; slangc version in cache key and checked |
| Swapchain sync hazards | per-image render-complete semaphores; resize/minimize state-machine tests; validation on in every GPU test |
| Golden flakiness | no temporal effects (from M26, captures reset temporal history and render a fixed warm-up, ADR 0028), fixed noise, per-device-class goldens (nvidia-61x only, the driver's major version held during the program, ADR 0028 decision 9), smoke mode elsewhere, device-independent oracle tests carry correctness |
| Non-determinism (threads, unordered iteration, Jolt callback order, time, build configuration) | canonical order, sorted events, seeded UUIDs/RNG, sim-time APIs, `JPH_CROSS_PLATFORM_DETERMINISTIC` + precise FP everywhere, DetMath on the simulation path, lint on unordered iteration in serialization and on CRT transcendental functions, thread-count hash tests, cross-configuration `determinism` stage |
| Variable frame rate bugs hidden by `ManualClock` | per-step interpolation snapshot of every entity, `RenderPosition` for cameras, `ScriptedClock` `Timing` suite in all three modes |
| Agent scripts hang or crash the editor | watchdog (GC-safe, nested deadline), soft memory limit with headroom, instance isolation, pause-on-error, non-finite and range rejection before Jolt, `ShapeSettings::Create` results as diagnostics, entity cap, play-session fuzz test, autosave + recovery, bridge supervision |
| Two editors writing one project | `Library/Editor.lock`, `editor_launch` attaches instead of spawning, `--read-only` second instance |
| GPU failure modes (device loss, OOM, hangs) | `VkResult`-checked swapchain, bounded timeline waits, null-checked NVRHI creation, frame-boundary catch, fault-injection tests, CPU-only autosave; recovery from device loss, driver-reset hangs and out-of-memory after startup in M18 (ADR 0028 decision 5) |
| Automation as an attack surface | loopback + token, HTTP probe rejection, path confinement, no method runs a request-supplied process or command (only `project.export`'s smoke test and macOS `codesign`, by fixed path, §13.2), absent from Dist, visible kill switch |
| Luau Analysis/definition churn | pinned release, one `.cpp`, `declare extern type` syntax, doc examples type-checked |
| Asset reference breakage on reimport/move | handles in `.meta`, stable sub-asset keys, derived prefab IDs, auto-fixable registry diagnostics |
| Agent context overflow | text tree, projections, pagination, file offload, short ids, downscaled screenshots |
| Tangent quality | glTF tangents used when present; MikkTSpace for generated tangents (vendored with M6, ADR 0010 decision 16); a documented perpendicular fallback + diagnostic for primitives without UVs |
| Scope creep | non-goals, "pays for itself" rule, milestone gates on tests, deferred list (Appendix C), the backlog program's scope and won't-do list (ADR 0028, `Docs/Backlog.md` §8) |
